# Porting Forbric to Minecraft 1.21.1

Everything below was measured in this repository on 2026-10-20 unless it says otherwise. The point of writing it
down is that most of it contradicts the natural first assumption, and the assumptions cost more time than the
measurements did.

**Status: not ported, and no longer blocked at definition.** Stages 1-4 are done. Stage 4's launch blocker — the
loader refusing to *define* `Entity`, `Level` and `BlockEntity` on this line — is removed and proved against the real
base (see "Stage 4: the 1.21.1 certificate" at the end). What remains is the rest of the runtime source set, which
still does not compile on 1.21.1, and everything about launching it.

---

## The one fact that explains most of the difficulty

**26.2 is unobfuscated (Mojmap-native). 1.21.1 is obfuscated.**

Forbric was built against 26.2 first, so a whole set of code paths that only matter on an obfuscated game has never
executed. They are not "buggy on 1.21.1"; they were never exercised at all. Four separate problems reduce to this:

| Where | What is wrong on 1.21.1 | Why 26.2 hid it |
|---|---|---|
| `KernelMappingResolver` | all six methods `return input;` | identity mapping is correct when obf-id == named-id |
| `KernelMixinBootstrap` | `REQUIRED_JAVA_FEATURE = 25` | 26.2 really is class-file 69 |
| `ForgeTransferShapeAudit` | 8 audited SHA-256 fingerprints are 26.2's | the classes are 26.2's Forge classes |
| `PatchedMcBuilder` | applies Forge binpatches to the obfuscated jar | on 26.2 the obfuscated jar *is* the Mojmap jar |

If a claim about 1.21.1 has no measurement behind it, the safe prior is that it is the 26.2 answer wearing a
different version number.

---

## Stage 1 - namespace mapping: DONE

Required because 1.21.1 fabric-api is built against intermediary names. Measured counts:

```
fabric-api 0.116.17+1.21.1   class_ 19,859   method_ 1,729   field_ 862   (48 of 49 modules)
fabric-api 0.161.0+26.2      class_      0   method_     1   field_   0
```

Chain for 1.21.1: obfuscated <-> intermediary <-> Mojmap. That needs a JOIN of two mapping files, because neither
side names all three.

What is in the tree and works:

- `tools/namespace/join-mappings.ps1` - class-level join (intermediary + Mojang client mappings -> Mojmap).
  Asserts against a real merged base: **8 of 8 probes PRESENT**.
- `tools/namespace/join-members.ps1` - member-level join, with field and method as separate dimensions.
  **77,152 of 78,922 = 97.7%**. Four mapped member names confirmed by `javap` against real bytecode.
- `tools/namespace/emit-table.ps1` - emits `mappings-1.21.1.tsv` (5,077,063 bytes). **Retired as the runtime
  producer**: the table `KernelMappingResolver` reads is now written by `join-keyed.py --full`. See "The runtime
  mapping table, replaced" at the end of this document for the measurement that forced it.
- `KernelMappingResolver` reads a pre-joined table from `-Dforbric.mappings=<file>`; identity when absent, which is
  the 26.2 path. `KernelMappingResolverTableTest` 6/0, existing `KernelMappingResolverTest` 3/0. (Now 9/0 and 3/0:
  the three added tests cover the table's class-name format and a refused truncated record.)

Mapping-format traps, all of which produced a silent zero or a wrong name rather than an error:

- tiny member lines are `\t<marker>\t<descriptor>\t<obf>\t<intermediary>` - field 2 is the DESCRIPTOR, not a name.
- Mojang's `client.txt` is `<mojmap> -> <obf>:`, i.e. reversed relative to what you expect.
- `# {...}` annotation lines reset the current owner if not skipped.
- Field and method must be a key dimension: obf names are shared across them (`field_18276` came out as a method).
- Path separators: `.` vs `/`.

---

## Stage 2 - Java version: DONE

`REQUIRED_JAVA_FEATURE = 25` became `requiredJavaFeature()`, which reads the class-file major from the game's own
`ItemStack.class` (bytes 6-7, feature = major - 44), validates `CAFEBABE`, and returns **0 for "no opinion"** on a
probe failure rather than claiming Java 0.

Measured: 26.2 `ItemStack.class` major 69; both 1.21.1 bases major 65.

`ForbricMixinService` used to clamp `CompatibilityLevel` down to `getCompatibilityLevel()`, which turned 60 fabric
configs into `JAVA_8` and produced `Unsupported registry: minecraft:block`. It now clamps only to `MAX_SUPPORTED`.

Test evidence: mixin + mapping suites 1769 cases, 24 failures, all of them Windows TempDir deletion (a known
limitation). Real failures: 0.

`tools/dev.py` derives its Java minimum from the version now, and this was verified on the real command path:
`FORBRIC_MC_VERSION=1.21.1` reports "JDK 21+ required" and 26.2 reports "JDK 25+ required", and
`compileToolsJava`/`mergeToolsJar` succeed under JDK 21.

---

## Stage 3 - bytecode fingerprints: 1 of 19 assessed

`ForgeTransferShapeAudit` pins a SHA-256 of seven Forge/NeoForge classes and refuses transfer writes for any shape
it has not reviewed. `tools/fingerprint/` reproduces that hash standalone, without a running kernel.

**The self-check is the only reason its output is usable:** run against 26.2's `forge-runtime.jar` it must reproduce
the pinned values. Measured: **7 of 7 reproduced, 0 mismatches.** Only after that did the 1.21.1 numbers mean
anything - one skipped step in the normalisation chain gives a different hash and the difference is invisible.

Measured on 1.21.1 (`merged-1.21.1-collect.jar`): all six present classes differ. The seventh is the finding:

```
Forge 1.21.1 CapabilityProvider inner classes:  2   (CapabilityProvider, $AsField)
Forge 26.2    CapabilityProvider inner classes:  7   (+ $AsField$LevelChunks, $BlockEntities,
                                                       $Entities, $ItemStacks, $Levels)
```

So the audit's premise - that an ItemStack's capability provider is the `$ItemStacks` inner class - does not hold on
1.21.1. Re-pinning hashes cannot fix that; the audit needs reasoning about per version.

The other 18 fingerprint sites are **not assessed**.

---

## Stage 4 - build and launch: blocked at one proven root cause

### The pipeline, which is not where you would look

The staged artifacts are produced by **the installer jar**, not by the shell scripts:

```
java -jar <installer> --dir <instance> --mc <version>
```

- `ArtifactBuilder.build(mcDir, mcVersion, jvm)` is already version-parametric; it resolves vanilla from
  `mcDir/versions/<mcVersion>/<mcVersion>.jar` and produces all six artifacts.
- `Pins.java` is what locked the version: `MINECRAFT`, `FORGE`, `NEOFORGE`, `NFRT`, `NFRT_RESULT`.
  All three version pins now read a system property and default to their 26.2 values.
- `Pins.stamp()` composes the whole set into one line and that line is the artifact cache key, so a half-changed pin
  set is safe rather than silently wrong: the stamps stop matching and everything rebuilds.
- `forbric-loader/run/build-merged-base.sh` is a development side path. Its own comment says the kernel owns the
  tools and "this project keeps no copy that could drift from it" - the real source is
  `forbric-kernel/src/tools/`, which is a **different file** (1917 lines) from the one under `.firstgen-backup`
  (1945 lines). Use the former.
- `.pins` files in `.forbric-build/out/` split into two different `tools=` hashes, so those artifacts came from two
  different installer builds. There is no `provenance.json`.

### The root cause

Running the pipeline with `-Dforbric.pin.minecraft=1.21.1 -Dforbric.pin.forge=1.21.1-52.1.20` gets all the way to
`binarypatcher` and fails:

```
Patch expected net/minecraft/server/Main$1 to have the checksum f734ba89 but it was e6be7ea9
```

Disassembling the same class from both sides settles it:

```
Forge official output (patched-mc-forge-1.21.1.jar)   Main$1  942 bytes
  final net.minecraft.server.MinecraftServer val$dedicatedserver;      <- Mojmap
  instanceof net.minecraft.gametest.framework.GameTestServer           <- Mojmap

PatchedMcBuilder's clean.jar                          Main$1  644 bytes
  final apn a;                                                        <- obfuscated
  invokevirtual apn.a:(Z)V                                            <- obfuscated
```

**Forge's binpatches are built against the DEOBFUSCATED jar. `PatchedMcBuilder` applies them to the obfuscated one.**
Its nine steps are: BUNDLER_EXTRACT, mergetool `--merge --ann API`, extract binpatches, binarypatcher, overlay,
access transformers, covariant self(). **There is no deobfuscation step, and no renaming step either** - an earlier
guess that `ForgeAutoRenamingTool --reverse` was a no-op on 26.2 was wrong; that tool does not appear in this file.

No SRG mappings are available anywhere in this tree or in the userdev jar (`forge-userdev.jar` holds only
`config.json`, `joined.lzma`, and `patches/*.java.patch`), so adding the missing step means obtaining mappings.
Mojang's own `client-1.21.1.txt` is already present under `namespace-probe/`.

### What is already correct, and can be used as-is

Naming shapes measured across the whole jars:

```
forge-probe-1211/patched-mc-forge-1.21.1.jar              9,478 classes   obfuscated short names: 0
nfrt-probe-1211/.../neoforge-21.1.249-merged.jar          9,768 classes   obfuscated short names: 0
forbric-kernel/.dev/minecraft/versions/1.21.1/1.21.1.jar  8,269 classes   obfuscated short names: 5,344
```

Both patched bases are **pure Mojmap**, which is exactly what `MergedBaseBuilder` expects. The vanilla client is not,
which is the one piece missing for the shortcut path below.

### Two ways forward

**A. Shortcut (unproven).** Deobfuscate the 1.21.1 vanilla with the Mojang mappings already in the tree, then feed
all three Mojmap jars to `MergedBaseBuilder`. Gets a launch attempt without touching `PatchedMcBuilder`. It is a
WORKAROUND, and it leaves the build pipeline still unable to produce a 1.21.1 base on its own.

**B. Correct fix (unproven).** Add the deobfuscation step to `PatchedMcBuilder` so the Forge side is built the way
Forge builds it. Requires mappings and touches the build path shared with 26.2, so it needs the 26.2 baseline
re-verified afterwards.

Note from stage 0: a `CONFLICTS: methods 1036` figure for 1.21.1 versus `1119` for 26.2 **was reported earlier and is
withdrawn**. Both runs used inputs that do not reproduce the shipped base, and the 1.21.1 run used the wrong tool
copy, so the comparison had no baseline. Do not reuse those numbers.

---

## Things that are not verified

- 1.21.1 has **never been built into a kernel and never launched**.
- `tools/dev.py prepare` for 1.21.1 has **never completed**. It stops at the `binarypatcher` checksum.
- Paths A and B above are **reasoning, not tests**.
- The collect-mode jars are surveys, not usable bases.
- Only the user can launch the game, so any render or GUI outcome is unverified by definition.

# Addendum: the shortcut path measured, and where it stops

Written after the section above. Path A turned out to be further along than the earlier text claims, and the merge
was measured properly for the first time.

## The deobfuscation step is not needed if you use the right vanilla

`E:\project\forge-probe-1211\work\client-official.jar` is **already Mojmap**. Measured across whole jars:

```
forge-probe-1211/work/client-official.jar              8,269 classes   obfuscated short names:     0
forge-probe-1211/patched-mc-forge-1.21.1.jar           9,478 classes   obfuscated short names:     0
nfrt-probe-1211/.../neoforge-21.1.249-merged.jar       9,768 classes   obfuscated short names:     0
forbric-kernel/.dev/minecraft/versions/1.21.1/1.21.1.jar 8,269 classes obfuscated short names: 5,344
```

The last row is what `dev.py prepare` downloads, and it is **not** what a merge wants. The first three are. So the
shortcut needs no remapping at all - it needs the right vanilla file, which already exists on this machine.

## The conflicts, measured comparably

Same tool (`forbric-kernel/src/tools`, including the collect-mode patch), same mode, both sides pure Mojmap. **The
line that matters is the second one, not the `CONFLICT-COLLECTED` count** - a mistake worth recording because it was
made three times:

```
1.21.1  [merge] classes: vanilla=2 forge-only-ns=1200 forge=97 neo=9077 MERGED=600 (superclass-rebased-to-forge=1)
1.21.1  [merge] CONFLICTS: methods=1029 fields=2 STRUCTURAL(superclass/field)=26 collision-methods-dropped=2

26.2    [merge] classes: vanilla=0 forge-only-ns=3 forge=185 neo=10155 MERGED=628 (superclass-rebased-to-forge=2)
26.2    [merge] CONFLICTS: methods=1117 fields=8 STRUCTURAL(superclass/field)=27 collision-methods-dropped=3
```

Read those fields correctly:

- `CONFLICT-COLLECTED #N` lines are only the cases that **threw** in default mode and were caught by collect mode.
  On 1.21.1 that is 4 lines. It is **not** the conflict count.
- `CONFLICTS: methods=...` is the real count. `vanilla=2` means "only 2 classes came from vanilla alone", not "only 2
  vanilla classes were seen" - most classes come wholesale from one of the two patched jars.
- `MERGED=600` is how many classes existed on **both** sides and therefore needed actual merging.

**On every conflict metric 1.21.1 is slightly better than 26.2**, not worse:

```
                   1.21.1   26.2
methods              1029    1117
fields                  2       8
STRUCTURAL             26      27
MERGED                600     628
```

This is consistent with the `1036 vs 1119` figure that an earlier revision of this document withdrew. **That
withdrawal was itself the error**: the number had been produced with the wrong tool copy and unverifiable inputs, so
re-measuring was right, but the conclusion drawn from that ("the figure was meaningless") was not - the figure was
essentially correct and it is now reproduced from a run whose inputs are two pure-Mojmap patched jars.

## The real extra cost is `forge-only-ns`

```
1.21.1   1,200
26.2         3
```

That is a difference of about 1,197 classes: classes that exist only in Forge's namespace. 26.2 needs essentially
none of them. **This is more likely to be what actually costs work on 1.21.1 than the conflict counts are**, and it
had not been looked at before it showed up in the same report line that carried the conflict numbers.


## Why conflict #1 cannot be fixed by relaxing a check

`Entity.getMaxHeightFluidType()` exists in both patched jars with identical arguments and different return types:

```
Forge      public net.minecraftforge.fluids.FluidType        getMaxHeightFluidType()
NeoForge   public net.neoforged.neoforge.fluids.FluidType    getMaxHeightFluidType()
```

Neither `FluidType` is an ancestor of the other - they are two unrelated classes with the same simple name.

The throw comes from one place, `MergedBaseBuilder.java:1024`, inside the `bm == null` branch. That branch already
recognises the situation (its comment says these are the same conceptual method, retyped by each compiler to its own
extension type, and that it should "reconcile to whichever ancestor we ACTUALLY chose") but **the reconciliation is
not implemented**: `boolean handled = false;` is never assigned `true`, so the only outcomes are the throw or a
straight splice.

`compatibleReturnContracts` (line 599) is **not** the bug. It accepts only one narrow shape: every `ARETURN` must be
preceded by a read of a field of the owner class, whose type is assignable to the return type - i.e. a cached-field
accessor. `getMaxHeightFluidType()` walks a map to find the highest entry, so it is correctly rejected.

Which means fixing this requires a **design decision**, not a code fix: on 1.21.1, which `FluidType` should the merged
`Entity` expose, or does it need a bridging class? That decides which ecosystem's mods work, and it is a question
about Forbric's intent rather than about the merge tool.

`ChunkGenerator` ("Incompatible initializer dependency") and `ItemBlockRenderTypes` ("retyped return") are separate
branches and have not been examined.

## 26.2 still cannot be reproduced here

Recorded because it keeps being assumed otherwise: running the active tool on 26.2 in **default** mode aborts on
`ReloadableServerResources#getConditionalServerResources` and produces nothing. Collect mode succeeds with 2
conflicts. So a shipped 26.2 merged base exists that this machine cannot regenerate through that path, and any
statement about "the 26.2 baseline" has to name which of the two it means.

---

# DANGER: `dev.py prepare` for 1.21.1 overwrites 26.2's state

`ArtifactBuilder.build()` writes `.dev/minecraft/.forbric-build/out/forge-runtime.jar` **first**, at
`ArtifactBuilder.java:101`, before the merge and before the `binarypatcher` step that currently fails. That filename
carries no version, so both lines share it. **A 1.21.1 `prepare` run therefore replaces the 26.2 Forge carrier on its
way to failing**, and the failure means nothing regenerates it.

Anyone touching this should either back that file up first, or make the path version-qualified before running prepare
for a second version. This is recorded here because it was found by a subagent reading `ArtifactBuilder`, not by
running into it - and earlier rounds of this port did run 1.21.1 prepares, so the 26.2 carrier may already have been
overwritten on this machine.

---

# The real size of the port: 151 runtime sources, 810 compile errors

An earlier revision of this document said the 26.2 audit found "about 15 lines to rewrite". **That estimate was wrong
by roughly fifty times**, and the reason is worth keeping: it was based on how few places name the version string.
Naming a version and depending on a version's API are different questions, and the kernel does the second one
constantly.

Measured by compiling the kernel's runtime source set against staged 1.21.1 artifacts:

```
810 errors across 151 runtime sources
```

Roughly a hundred corners of it, along three independent version axes:

| Axis | What is gone | Example |
|---|---|---|
| **Minecraft 26.x -> 1.21.1** | classes renamed between the versions | `net/minecraft/resources/Identifier` is gone; 1.21.1 has `ResourceLocation`. **101 uses.** Also `PackFormat`, `FuelValues`, `TooltipDisplay`, `EntitySpawnReason`, `SubmitNodeCollector`. |
| **Forge/NeoForge 26.x -> 1.21.1** | EventBus 7 API vs 6.2.33 | `eventbus.api.bus.EventBus<T>`, `BusGroup`, `listener.SubscribeEvent` do not exist; 1.21.1 ships `IEventBus`. Also `neoforgespi.transformation.*` and `fml.classloading.transformation.*`. |
| **fabric-api transfer 0.155 -> 0.116** | the API was renamed | `ItemVariant`/`FluidVariant`/`Storage` are 26.2 names; 1.21.1 has `ItemResource`/`FluidResource`/`TransactionContext`, plus `ResourceHandler`, `EnergyHandler`, `Operation`. |

This is **not** a staging hole, and that was checked in both directions: `ResourceLocation` and `FMLPaths` are present
in the staged jars, so the classpath resolves, and the missing symbols are genuinely absent from 1.21.1.

## What assembling the artifacts did teach

Nine of the eleven staged artifacts can be produced from what is on this machine. The two that cannot are the canary
mods, whose sources do not compile against 1.21.1 for the same reasons as the kernel.

The stock `assemble-*.sh` scripts **hardcode 26.2 auxiliary library versions**. 1.21.1's FML wants eventbus 6.2.33 and
forgespi 7.1.5 where 26.2 wants 7.0.1 and 8.0.0; mixing them assembles cleanly and fails later. Versions have to come
from each line's own userdev `config.json`, not from those scripts.

50 of 1.21.1's 54 Minecraft libraries are absent from `.dev/minecraft/libraries`, which holds 26.2's versions, so
`ready()` stays false until `stage_minecraft()` runs for 1.21.1.

## So what remains

Porting 151 runtime sources along three API axes. That is the whole of stage 4. Nothing about launching can be
measured until it is done, because no kernel jar exists to launch.

---

# Axis 1 (Minecraft's own API) measured and reduced: 261 -> 99

Measured after the section above. **Axis 1 only**; the EventBus/NeoForge axis and the fabric-transfer axis were
deliberately not touched and their counts are byte-identical before and after.

```
                     before   after
TOTAL                   772     608
AXIS1-minecraft         261      99     <- this work
AXIS2-forge-neoforge    164     164     (untouched)
AXIS3-fabric-transfer   166     166     (untouched)
unattributed cascades   181     179
```

Both runs are the same javac (`-Xmaxerrs 10000`, `--release 21`, JDK 21) over the same 151 sources with the same
classpath. The 772 here is **not** the 810 this document recorded earlier; the 38-error gap could not be
reproduced and has no confirmed cause (removing the boot-side classes from the classpath gives 1,843, not 810,
so "missing boot output" is ruled out). Use 772 as the axis-1 baseline, since it is the number the procedure
below reproduces.

## The names are mutually exclusive, so there is no rename

Every symbol on this axis is present on exactly one line:

```
net.minecraft.resources.Identifier         26.2=PRESENT  1.21.1=absent
net.minecraft.resources.ResourceLocation   26.2=absent   1.21.1=PRESENT
```

Same shape for `EntitySpawnReason`/`MobSpawnType`, `EntityTypes`/`EntityType`,
`WeightedList`/`WeightedRandomList`, `AbstractBoat`/`Boat`, `TriState`, `Hud`, `ValueInput`. So a blanket
rename cannot work: whichever spelling the sources use, the other line stops compiling. And 118 of the 136
`Identifier` occurrences are **type positions** (declarations, generics, `.class` literals), which no wrapper or
helper can absorb, because every vanilla call takes and returns the real type.

## The mechanism: a declared rename table, applied at build time

`forbric-kernel/src/runtime/mc-line/1.21.1.tsv` holds `<26.2 name> TAB <1.21.1 name>`. The Gradle task
`normalizeRuntimeJava` copies `src/runtime/java` into `build/generated/mc-line/<line>/java`, applying the table
whole-token and longest-name-first, and the runtime source dir is pointed at that copy. This is the compile-time
twin of what `ForbricMappings` already does to bytecode at runtime.

**On 26.2 nothing happens.** The task, the `srcDir` swap and the dependency are all inside
`if (mcLine != '26.2')`, so a default build's source dir is still `src/runtime/java` and no extra task joins the
graph. The line is chosen by `-Pforbric.mcLine`, then `FORBRIC_MC_VERSION` (the variable `tools/dev.py` already
reads), then 26.2.

Two guards, both exercised rather than assumed:

- a rule that matches nothing **fails the build** (a stale entry would otherwise become a compile error on the
  other line, which reads as an engine difference);
- a line with no table **fails naming the file**, never silently building as 26.2.

The table is names only. Member-level counterparts that change the *shape* of an expression
(`Minecraft.gui.setScreen` -> `Minecraft.setScreen`, `WorldVersion.packVersion():PackFormat` ->
`getPackVersion():int`, `Gui.overlay()`) are written down in the table's tail as facts, not as rules.

## What is NOT a rename and still needs code

Measured absent from 1.21.1 with no same-named equivalent, so these need restructuring that also has to keep 26.2
correct — which is why they are **not** table entries:

`TooltipDisplay` (no pre-1.21.2 tooltip-display component) · `FuelValues` · `PackFormat` (int before 1.21.9) ·
`TriState` · `TypedInstance` · `ValueInput`/`ValueOutput` · `Hud` + `GuiGraphicsExtractor` + the whole
`net.minecraft.client.gui.render.pip` picture-in-picture pipeline · `WeightedList` (1.21.1 has no `Builder`) ·
`FluidModel` · `CuboidModel`.

## Two classpath findings that are not this axis but corrupt measurements

- **`build/transfer-api-compile/` is shared between lines.** `extractTransferApis` `Sync`s whichever fabric-api
  was asked for into one unversioned directory, so a default (26.2) build silently leaves
  `fabric-transfer-api-v1-8.0.11` where a 1.21.1 measurement expects `-0.116.17`. Then the transfer API looks
  *missing* rather than *different*, and 146 extra errors appear. Re-run `extractTransferApis` with the line's own
  `-Pforbric.fabricApi` before measuring. This is the same class of hazard as the `forge-runtime.jar` DANGER above.
- **`.dev/api/energy-5.0.0.jar` is class-file version 69 (Java 25).** A JDK-21 javac (`--release 21`) cannot read
  it at all: "class file has wrong version 69.0, should be 65.0". That is 19 of the axis-3 errors and it is a
  staging problem, not a fabric difference — the 1.21.1 line needs a Java-21 build of that jar.

---

# Axes 2 and 3 measured and reduced: 608 -> 582, and what the "fabric rename" turns out to be

Measured after the axis-1 section above, same javac, same 151 sources, same `-Xmaxerrs 10000 --release 21`
under JDK 21, same classpath (`.dev/staged-1211/run` staged base). **Two procedures were used and both are
reported, because one of them is a hazard rather than a variable of the port:**

- **M1** is a1ef107's procedure exactly: `.dev/api/energy-5.0.0.jar` as it is, transfer modules re-extracted
  with the 1.21.1 fabric-api first. This is the pair that is comparable with the numbers already in this
  document.
- **M2** is the same procedure with the energy jar's class-file version retargeted 69 -> 65 (see hazard 2),
  applied to both runs. It isolates the staging defect from the API work.

```
M1  (energy jar as shipped, class-file 69)
                     before   after
TOTAL                   608     582
AXIS1-minecraft          99      99
AXIS2-forge-neoforge    164     138     <- this work
AXIS3-fabric-transfer   166     166     (nothing here is a fabric rename; see below)
unattributed cascades   179     179

M2  (energy jar retargeted to class-file 65, both runs)
                     before   after
TOTAL                   587     561
AXIS1-minecraft          99      99
AXIS2-forge-neoforge    164     138
AXIS3-fabric-transfer   142     142
unattributed cascades   182     182
```

javac's own totals are 608 -> 582 (M1) and 588 -> 562 (M2). The one-error gap on M2 is a single
`error: cannot access ParametersAreNonnullByDefault` that carries no `file:line` prefix, so the splitter
cannot see it; it appears only once the energy jar is readable, and it is not attributable to either axis.

**Axis 3 did not move because nothing on it is a rename.** That is the finding, not an omission, and it is
measured below.

## Axis 2: what was a rename, and what was not

The one thing on this axis that a name table can express is EventBus 7's per-event static bus. 6.2.33 has no
such field; on 1.21.1 every one of those events is posted on the single game bus, whose declaration was read
from the staged 1.21.1 `forge-runtime.jar`:

```
public static final net.minecraftforge.eventbus.api.IEventBus EVENT_BUS;   (net.minecraftforge.common.MinecraftForge)
```

26 rules `X.BUS` -> `net.minecraftforge.common.MinecraftForge.EVENT_BUS` are now in the table, one per event
class, and they account for **26 of the 26 errors removed** (164 -> 138, exactly one per rule).

The interesting part is the six sites that look identical and got no rule:

- `SpawnPlacementRegisterEvent.BUS` and `RegisterClientReloadListenersEvent.BUS` both implement
  `net.minecraftforge.fml.event.IModBusEvent` (javap). They fire on the **mod** bus, which has no name at all
  in 6.2.33 — it is reached through `FMLJavaModLoadingContext.get().getModEventBus()`, an expression. A rule
  would compile and silently deliver the event to nobody.
- `EntityEvent.EnteringSection.BUS` and `LivingEvent.LivingVisibilityEvent.BUS` are called as
  `.BUS.hasListeners()`, and `IEventBus` in 6.2.33 has no `hasListeners()` at all (javap: register,
  addListener x4, addGenericListener x4, unregister, post x2, fire x2, shutdown, start). Rewriting the
  receiver would trade one error for another and *move it out of this axis's count*, which is not a fix.
- `AttachCapabilitiesEvent.{Levels,Entities,BlockEntities}.BUS` — those inner classes do not exist on 1.21.1,
  which is the same 26.x capability-provider split already recorded in stage 3 above.

The claim in each rule was measured two ways before it was written: the event does not carry the
`IModBusEvent` marker on 1.21.1, and where the Forge hook is reachable its bytecode posts the event with
`getstatic MinecraftForge.EVENT_BUS` (`ForgeEventFactory`, `ForgeHooks`, `ServerLifecycleHooks`,
`ClientCommandHandler`, `ForgeHooksClient`, `ForgeEventFactoryClient`).

Everything else on axis 2 is an API generation, not a spelling. Every one of the 12,505 classes in the staged
1.21.1 jars was indexed and searched by simple name for each failing 26.2 name; at most four candidates came
back per name and each was a different concept. The list, with evidence, is in the table's axis-2 tail:
`BusGroup` (EventBus 7's group lifecycle), `neoforgespi.transformation.*` /
`fml.classloading.transformation.*` (modlauncher is what 1.21.1 has), `JarContents`, the `common.tooltip.*`
rework, the keyed reload-listener registry, `common.util.Result`, `InFluidPredicate`, `RecipeContentPayload`,
`UnbakedModelParser`, the pip/`ForgeLayer` cluster.

Two cases worth naming because they are *nearly* renames:

- **`BreakBlockEvent` really is a rename.** `net.neoforged.neoforge.event.level.block.BreakBlockEvent` (26.2)
  and `net.neoforged.neoforge.event.level.BlockEvent$BreakEvent` (1.21.1) have the same constructor, the same
  `getPlayer()`/`setCanceled(boolean)` and the same superclass (javap). It is still not a rule: the target is
  a **nested** class, and the file using it already imports a *different* `BlockEvent` and writes
  `BlockEvent.BreakEvent` for the MinecraftForge event a few lines later. The only spelling that works is the
  `$` form, for three errors. Written down instead.
- **`AddClientReloadListenersEvent` / `AddServerReloadListenersEvent`.** The 1.21.1 events exist
  (`RegisterClientReloadListenersEvent`, `AddReloadListenerEvent`), but the kernel calls
  `SortedReloadListenerEvent.addListener(key, listener, order)`; 1.21.1's equivalents have no ordering. The
  event is a rename; the API the kernel uses is not.

## Axis 3: the fabric transfer names are the same on both lines

This contradicts the earlier assumption in this document, and it was measured rather than argued. The two
lines' transfer modules were extracted separately and their class lists diffed:

```
fabric-transfer-api-v1     26.2 (8.0.11): 131 classes     1.21.1 (0.116.17): 115 classes

present on BOTH: ItemVariant, FluidVariant, Storage, StorageView, Transaction, TransactionContext,
                 ContainerItemContext, FluidStorage, ItemStorage, PlayerInventoryStorage,
                 SingleVariantStorage, SnapshotParticipant, ResourceAmount, ...

renamed:         ContainerStorage (26.2) -> InventoryStorage (1.21.1), same package
                 net.fabricmc.fabric.api.transfer.v1.item, same shape: both extend
                 SlottedStorage<ItemVariant>, both have of(Container, Direction) + getSlots() +
                 getSlotCount() + getSlot(int)  (javap both)
```

So `ItemVariant`/`FluidVariant`/`Storage` are **not** 26.2-only names, and `ItemResource`/`FluidResource`/
`ResourceHandler`/`EnergyHandler` are **not fabric names at all** — every one of them is
`net.neoforged.neoforge.transfer.*`, NeoForge's own transfer API, which is why they appear in the axis-2
list. The 166 errors the splitter calls "axis 3" are NeoForge-transfer errors (about 130) plus Team Reborn
Energy errors (24, hazard 2) plus a handful of fabric ones.

**The single fabric rename is not a rule, on measurement.** `InventoryStorage.of` on 1.21.1 takes
`net.minecraft.class_1263, net.minecraft.class_2350` — **intermediary** names. The kernel calls
`ContainerStorage.of(container, Direction.UP)` with Mojmap types, so substituting the name turns three
`cannot find symbol ContainerStorage` into three `incompatible types: Container cannot be converted to
class_1263`: same total, different bucket, with the real gap hidden behind a rename that looks like a fix.
That gap is the same one producing the 20 `class_1937 / class_2248 / class_2338 / class_2350 / class_1792 /
class_1935 / class_3611 / class_9326` errors: **the 1.21.1 fabric-api modules are intermediary-remapped**,
and compiling Mojmap sources against them cannot work for any signature that mentions a Minecraft type. The
fix is to remap those three modules to Mojmap — `tools/namespace/` already builds the table — not to rename a
type. That is the next piece of work on this axis and it was not attempted here.

## Hazard 1: `build/transfer-api-compile/` is shared, measured live

After the 26.2 verification build further down, that directory holds:

```
fabric-api-base-2.0.4+ece063239e.jar
fabric-api-lookup-api-v1-2.0.17+11e1c9a39e.jar
fabric-transfer-api-v1-8.0.11+39b5a3929e.jar
```

A 1.21.1 measurement taken now would read the transfer API as *missing*. Every 1.21.1 run in this document
re-ran `extractTransferApis` with `-Pforbric.fabricApi=.dev/api/fabric-api-0.116.17+1.21.1.jar` first, and the
classpath then names `fabric-transfer-api-v1-0.116.17.jar`. Both modules were also copied out to
`build/x11/transfer-26.2/` and `build/x11/transfer-1.21.1/` for the diff above.

## Hazard 2: the energy jar, retargeted and ABI-checked

All 12 classes of `.dev/api/energy-5.0.0.jar` are class-file **69** (Java 25), and a JDK-21 `--release 21`
javac cannot read the jar at all:

```
error: bad class file: ...energy-5.0.0.jar(team/reborn/energy/api/EnergyStorage.class)
    class file has wrong version 69.0, should be 65.0
```

Every `team.reborn.energy.*` reference then fails as `cannot find symbol`. That is 24 of the axis-3 errors,
and it is a staging defect rather than a fabric difference.

Handled by rewriting only the two class-file version bytes (`build/x11/retarget-jar.py`, 12 classes, bytes 0-5
and 8-end compared byte-for-byte equal), then proving the ABI is unchanged with **the repository's own
check** rather than a claim:

```
gradlew verifyRebornEnergy -Pforbric.rebornEnergy=build/x11/energy-5.0.0-j21.jar
> Task :verifyRebornEnergy
API binary contract verified: ...\forbric-kernel\build\x11\energy-5.0.0-j21.jar
```

The retarget refuses on a class whose constant pool uses a tag newer than the target version, so it cannot
silently downgrade something that needs 69. The 1.21.1 line still needs a real Java-21 build of that jar; this
is a local staging repair, not a substitute. **It is not wired into the build** — `measure.sh` passes it with
`-Pforbric.rebornEnergy=`, and a 1.21.1 `verifyRebornEnergy` under JDK 21 still needs it supplied that way.

## 26.2 still builds: the three checks, with commands

All three were re-run after this work, under `JAVA_HOME=C:\Program Files\Java\jdk-25.0.2` (and JDK 21 where
stated), with `GRADLE_USER_HOME` pointed at the repository's `.gradle-home`:

1. **Clean build.**
   `gradlew.bat --no-daemon clean jar` -> `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 12s`, 10 tasks executed, `verifyRebornEnergy`
   and `compileRuntimeJava` included. Note what `compileRuntimeJava` compiled: the warnings it printed name
   `src\runtime\java\...`, i.e. the un-normalised source tree, so `normalizeRuntimeJava` was not in the graph.
2. **Fresh runtime compile.**
   `rm -rf build/classes/java/runtime` then `gradlew.bat --no-daemon compileRuntimeJava` ->
   `BUILD SUCCESSFUL`, **295 class files** under `build/classes/java/runtime`. `--dry-run` of the same task
   lists only `:extractTransferApis` (SKIPPED — up to date) and `:compileRuntimeJava`: no
   `:normalizeRuntimeJava`.
3. **The srcDir switch is not sticky.** `gradlew.bat --no-daemon compileRuntimeJava -Pforbric.mcLine=1.21.1`
   ran `:normalizeRuntimeJava` (logging `1x` per rule, all 26 matched) and then **failed** at
   `:compileRuntimeJava`, which is the expected result — that line does not compile yet. Immediately after,
   `rm -rf build/classes/java/runtime` and `gradlew.bat --no-daemon compileRuntimeJava` with no property ->
   `BUILD SUCCESSFUL`, 295 class files. 26.2 is unaffected by a failed 1.21.1 run in the same working tree.

## Reproducing the measurement

The harness lives in `forbric-kernel/build/x11/`, which is gitignored, so the procedure is what to keep:

```
export GRADLE_USER_HOME=<repo>/.gradle-home
export JAVA_HOME="C:/Program Files/Java/jdk-21"

# 1. the line's own transfer modules -- hazard 1, every time
./gradlew.bat --no-daemon extractTransferApis \
    -Pforbric.stagedRoot=.dev/staged-1211/run \
    -Pforbric.fabricApi=.dev/api/fabric-api-0.116.17+1.21.1.jar

# 2. normalise the runtime sources for the line, and dump the classpath
./gradlew.bat --no-daemon -I build/x11/dump-cp.gradle normalizeRuntimeJava dumpRuntimeCp \
    -Pforbric.mcLine=1.21.1 -Pforbric.stagedRoot=.dev/staged-1211/run \
    -Pforbric.fabricApi=.dev/api/fabric-api-0.116.17+1.21.1.jar

# 3. javac by hand -- Gradle caps diagnostics at 100 errors
<JDK21>/bin/javac -J-Duser.language=en -J-Duser.country=US -J-Dstdout.encoding=UTF-8 \
    -Xmaxerrs 10000 --release 21 -encoding UTF-8 \
    -d build/x11/classes -cp "@build/x11/runtime-cp.txt" @build/x11/sources-norm.txt
```

Two measurement traps found here, both of which produce a silently wrong number rather than an error:

- **javac localises its diagnostics.** On this machine, with no locale pin, a JDK-21 javac emitted
  **GBK-encoded Chinese**, in which `axis-split.py`'s `symbol:` and `package ... does not exist` anchors do not
  exist and the split reported `TOTAL: 0` for a run that had 608 errors in it. The English + UTF-8 pin above
  is what makes the numbers comparable with the ones already in this document.
- `build/x11/classes/` is javac's `-d` target. Without one, javac writes class files next to the generated
  sources and the next `normalizeRuntimeJava` `Sync` deletes them mid-run.

## What is still not verified

- **No 1.21.1 kernel jar has ever been built and no 1.21.1 launch has been attempted.** 138 axis-2 errors and
  166 axis-3 errors remain in M1 (142 in M2); a lower count is not a build.
- The **test suites were not run on either line.** Nothing here is a behaviour check.
- Every `X.BUS` rule was verified by type and bytecode, not at runtime: that `MinecraftForge.EVENT_BUS.post`
  delivers the event to the mods that expect it is a claim about Forge 1.21.1's own dispatch, supported by
  the bytecode evidence listed above, not by an executed event.
- The **energy retarget** proves the public ABI is unchanged via `ApiJarContract`; it does not prove the 12
  classes behave identically, and no test loads them.
- The **fabric modules are still intermediary-remapped**, so the 20 `class_*` errors and the three
  `ContainerStorage` ones are unresolved by design here.
- `docs/1.21.1-port.md` stage 4's `binarypatcher` blocker is untouched by this work.

# The fabric-api modules, remapped out of intermediary: 582 -> 571

The previous round recorded, with evidence and deliberately without a fix, that the 1.21.1 fabric-api modules
are **intermediary-remapped** (`class_1263`, `class_2350`, ...) and that this was the reason
`ContainerStorage` -> `InventoryStorage` could not be a rule. Both halves of that are now addressed: the modules
are rewritten to Mojmap at build time on the 1.21.1 line only, and the rule is a rule.

## What was remapped, and the modules that needed it - verified, not assumed

Measured over the three modules `extractTransferApis` pulls out of `fabric-api-0.116.17+1.21.1.jar`, counting
constant-pool references that point into `net/minecraft/`:

| module | class files | files naming net.minecraft | Minecraft classes (distinct) | member refs (distinct) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| fabric-api-base | 14 | 5 | 1 | 3 |
| fabric-api-lookup-api-v1 | 29 | 19 | 15 | 23 |
| fabric-transfer-api-v1 | 115 | 70 | 88 | 232 |
| the three together | 158 | 94 | **93** | **244** |

All three need it; the previous round's three-module guess was right. The whole affected surface is **93 distinct
Minecraft classes and 244 member references** (the per-module columns overlap; the last row is the union) -
small enough to enumerate, which is why the failure was measurable at all rather than diffuse.

The remap is `remapTransferApis` in `forbric-kernel/build.gradle`, registered only inside the existing
`if (mcLine != mcLineDefault)` guard, so the 26.2 build graph does not gain a task (verified: `--dry-run` of
`compileRuntimeJava` on 26.2 lists neither `normalizeRuntimeJava` nor `remapTransferApis`). It runs
`net.forbric.nsremap.NamespaceRemapper` (ASM 9.10.1 `ClassRemapper` + a hierarchy-walking `Remapper`) over
`build/transfer-api-compile/` and writes `build/transfer-api-mc/1.21.1/`. The runtime source set compiles
against the latter on that line and against the former on 26.2 - one variable, chosen by `mcLine`.

Three things it does beyond a `SimpleRemapper`:

- **Hierarchy walking.** A call site's owner is the static receiver type, not the declaring class.
  `class_1657.method_23317` is declared on `class_1297`; **38 of the 244 references resolve only through a
  supertype.** It also matters for declarations: `AbstractFurnaceBlockEntityMixin` extends a Minecraft class, so
  its own overriding declarations must be renamed with their target.
- **Entry names, not only bytes.** Output entry names come from the same name function, so an inner class moves
  with its outer (`class_2350$class_2351` -> `Direction$Axis` in both places). **No class in these three modules
  is Minecraft-named**, so the real run renames zero entries - the path is proved by a `--selftest` mode that
  builds a jar holding `net/minecraft/class_1799` and its inner class, remaps it, and asserts the entry names,
  the field descriptor and the method names moved (`SELFTEST PASS`, 2 of 2 entries renamed).
- **A verification pass against a real Mojmap jar.** Every class, descriptor, signature, annotation type, LDC
  type and member reference in the OUTPUT is resolved against the staged merged base, walking supertypes, and
  the task fails if one does not. Result: **742 references checked (275 class, 145 field, 322 method), 0
  unresolvable**; 1 member unchecked because its owner chain leaves the base (`Container.toString()`, inherited
  from `java/lang/Object`), and 87 non-Minecraft owners (other fabric modules, guava) which the remap does not
  touch. The remap itself is deterministic: two runs over the same inputs produce byte-identical jars, because
  the entry timestamps are fixed.

The table is `src/runtime/mc-line/1.21.1-fabric-ns.tsv` - a declared, reviewable file, like the rename table,
not something inferred inside a task. It covers the **closure** the modules need: 175 classes and 10,352
members, supertypes included. A name it lacks is reported by the task and fails the build.

## The join behind that table is not the one `emit-table.ps1` does, and the difference is a defect

`tools/namespace/emit-table.ps1` keys members by `(obfOwner, kind, obfName)`. That key is **lossy**: one
obfuscated class reuses a short obf name for many methods that differ only in descriptor - `bsr.a` alone covers
81 - so **8,744 of 70,511 obf keys in `client-1.21.1.txt` carry more than one Mojmap name**, and whichever the
join saw last wins. `tools/namespace/join-keyed.py` adds the descriptor *and* the obf member name to the key:

```
78,922 of 78,922 members joined (100.0%), 0 ambiguous       join-keyed.py
77,152 of 78,922 members joined ( 97.7%)                    the shipped pipeline
```

Two further traps had to be fixed to get there, both silent: **client.txt's obf column is a DOTTED class name**
for the 34 classes Minecraft does not obfuscate (`net.minecraft.server.MinecraftServer`), while tiny writes the
same class slashed - 454 members; and the `10:10:` line-range prefix on client.txt method lines must be stripped
before anything else, or the return type parses as `911:911:boolean` and 40,686 of 41,243 methods join with
nothing.

**This is not only a build-time matter.** `KernelMappingResolver` reads exactly the `C/M/F` format
`emit-table.ps1` writes, so the shipped runtime table carries the same defect. Measured against the merged base:

| property | shipped table | descriptor-keyed table |
|---|---|---|
| member records | 77,152 | 78,922 |
| records where the two tables disagree on the name | 27,074 | - |
| of those, the other table's name is the one present at that owner+signature | 11 | 22,410 |
| two intermediary members mapped onto ONE Mojmap member | **4,025 targets / 8,241 records** | **0** |

A correct join is one-to-one onto Mojmap's member set, so 4,025 colliding targets are impossible rather than
merely unlikely. The corrected full table is one command
(`python tools/namespace/join-keyed.py <out> --full`); it is **not** wired in here, because swapping the runtime
mapping table is a behaviour change on this line and belongs to its own change.

## The counts, identically measured

Same procedure as above (same javac, same flags, same 151 sources, same classpath except the one variable),
against `.dev/staged-1211/run`, fabric-api `0.116.17+1.21.1` re-extracted first every time.

| step | TOTAL | axis 1 | axis 2 | axis 3 | cascades/other |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| baseline, reproduced exactly | **582** | 99 | 138 | 166 | 179 |
| + fabric modules remapped | **574** | 99 | 138 | 166 | 171 |
| + `ContainerStorage` rule | **571** | 99 | 138 | 163 | 171 |

The baseline is not carried over from the previous round: it was re-measured on this tree before any change and
came back 582 with the same split (179/166/138/99), and the final transcript was reproduced byte-identically
twice from two different starting states. The two steps are one variable each, and the error diffs are exact:

- **remap alone**: 8 errors gone, **0 new** - exactly the eight `cannot access class_1937 / class_2248 /
  class_2338 / class_2350 / class_1792 / class_1935 / class_3611 / class_9326` errors, all in the transfer
  package.
- **rule alone**: 3 errors gone, 0 new - the three `cannot find symbol ContainerStorage`.

The energy staging defect is out of the way in a second pair of runs (the retargeted `energy-5.0.0-j21.jar` on
both sides), where the same two changes are worth **15 errors (561 -> 546)**, 0 new. Four of the extra ones are
because a readable energy jar lets three `incompatible types` and two `invalid method reference` errors surface
that the class-69 jar's earlier failures were masking - all fabric-signature errors the remap fixes:

| state | TOTAL | axis 1 | axis 2 | axis 3 | other |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| raw modules, no rule | 561 | 99 | 138 | 142 | 182 |
| remapped modules, no rule | **549** | 99 | 138 | 142 | 170 |
| raw modules, with rule | 560 | 99 | 138 | 139 | 184 |
| remapped modules, with rule | **546** | 99 | 138 | 139 | 170 |

That table also re-measures the previous round's prediction instead of repeating it. With the modules still
intermediary-remapped, the rule is 561 -> 560: **three "cannot find symbol" become one "incompatible types:
Hopper cannot be converted to class_1263" and one "cannot access class_1263"** - the predicted lateral move,
observed. After the remap, the same rule is 549 -> 546 and changes nothing else.

`javap` on the remapped 1.21.1 module is what makes it a rename rather than a swap:

```
public static net.fabricmc.fabric.api.transfer.v1.item.InventoryStorage
    of(net.minecraft.world.Container, net.minecraft.core.Direction);
```

## 26.2 still builds: the three checks

`JAVA_HOME=C:\Program Files\Java\jdk-25.0.2`, `GRADLE_USER_HOME=<repo>/.gradle-home`:

1. **Clean build.** `gradlew.bat --no-daemon clean jar` -> `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 12s`, 10 tasks executed, and the
   compiler warnings name `src\runtime\java\...` - the un-normalised tree - so `normalizeRuntimeJava` and
   `remapTransferApis` were not in that graph.
2. **Fresh runtime compile.** `rm -rf build/classes/java/runtime` then `compileRuntimeJava` -> `BUILD
   SUCCESSFUL`, **295 class files**. `--dry-run` of the same task lists `:compileJava :processResources
   :classes :extractTransferApis :verifyRebornEnergy :compileRuntimeJava` and nothing else.
3. **The line switch is not sticky.** A 1.21.1 `compileRuntimeJava` (which ran `:normalizeRuntimeJava`,
   `:remapTransferApis` and `:verifyRebornEnergy`, then failed at `:compileRuntimeJava` as expected), immediately
   followed by `rm -rf build/classes/java/runtime` and a plain `compileRuntimeJava` -> `BUILD SUCCESSFUL`, 295
   class files, no normalise or remap task in the graph.

The 1.21.1 measurement was then taken again **from the state a 26.2 build leaves behind** -
`build/transfer-api-compile/` holding 8.0.11 - and the transcript is byte-identical to the earlier one
(`md5 27c08df8...`), which is the procedure the shared-directory hazard prescribes, executed rather than
trusted.

## What is still not verified

- **The 1.21.1 line still does not compile.** 571 errors by the documented procedure, 546 with the energy jar
  retargeted. **No 1.21.1 kernel jar has ever been built and no 1.21.1 launch attempted.** A lower count is not
  a build.
- **The remapped modules are compile-time only, and that is load-bearing.** The game side still links against
  the fabric modules the pack installs. Nothing in `build/transfer-api-mc/` is packaged or loaded - and it must
  not be: the string-valued mixin selectors (`@Inject(method = "...")`, `@At(target = "...")`, `@Shadow` field
  names) are written in **yarn** names and are *not* rewritten by this pass. They are inert for javac and would
  be wrong at runtime. A `@Shadow` declaration whose name belongs to the mixin's target class rather than to its
  supertype chain (`method_17029` -> `getTotalCookTime`, declared on `class_2609`, not on the mixin's
  superclass) likewise keeps its intermediary name: that changes no javac outcome, and it is left for whoever
  makes these jars loadable.
- The verification proves **every Minecraft reference in the remapped jars resolves**; it does not prove the
  fabric modules behave correctly against 1.21.1, and nothing loads them.
- The test suites were not run on either line. Nothing here is a behaviour check.
- The **runtime mapping table defect** (4,025 colliding targets, 22,410 provably wrong member names) is measured
  and fixed in `tools/namespace/join-keyed.py`, but the shipped table was **not** replaced.
  `KernelMappingResolver` still reads the lossy one.
- The energy jar on the 1.21.1 line is still a locally retargeted class-69 jar passed with
  `-Pforbric.rebornEnergy=`, not a real Java-21 build, and it is still not wired into the build.

# The runtime mapping table, replaced: 22,861 verified-wrong records to 0

The previous section ended by naming a defect it had measured and deliberately did not fix: `emit-table.ps1` keys
members by `(obfOwner, kind, obfName)`, which is lossy, so the table that `KernelMappingResolver` reads at runtime
carried that defect too, and swapping it was a behaviour change that belonged to its own change. This is that
change, and it does not rest on the join's own counts. It rests on the two questions a runtime table has to answer:
**does the resolver hand back exactly what the file says**, and **does that name exist at that owner and signature
in the game that runs**.

## Three ways this could have failed silently, and what each is measured by

1. **The join.** `tools/namespace/join-keyed.py --full` emits 87,176 records (8,254 C, 41,243 M, 37,679 F) from
   78,922 of 78,922 members joined, 0 ambiguous. (`--full` is now explicitly the runtime mode; the closure mode
   that feeds `NamespaceRemapper` is byte-identical to the committed `1.21.1-fabric-ns.tsv`, md5 `676dce98…`,
   so the compile-time remap is untouched.)
2. **The resolver's parsing of it.** A record the resolver cannot use does not throw: member records with fewer
   than four fields were dropped by an `if (f.length >= 4)` guard, and a table keyed differently from the lookup
   just misses. `net.forbric.kernel.fabric.MappingTableAudit` loads the table through the resolver — system
   property, static table, the real code path — and replays **every** record.
3. **The names being true of the game.** `tools/namespace/verify-runtime-table.py` resolves each record's
   descriptor from Fabric's own tiny mapping (neither side invents one), converts it from obf to Mojmap, and asks
   the jars whether `(name, descriptor)` exists on that owner.

## The format WAS different, and that is the finding

The table was swapped and the resolver was left alone first, on purpose, to see what it would say. It said:

```
                            shipped table                       descriptor-keyed table
mapClassName(slashed in)    net.minecraft.client.Minecraft      net/minecraft/client/Minecraft
unmapClassName(dotted in)   net/minecraft/class_310             net.minecraft.client.Minecraft   <- the input
mapMethodName(class_310)    lambda$buildInitialScreens$8        run
```

Three separate silent failures on one screen:

- **The keyed table's official class names are INTERNAL (slashed)**, because `join-keyed.py` also writes the table
  `NamespaceRemapper` feeds to ASM, where slashes are the form. fabric-loader's `MappingResolverImpl` answers
  **binary** names and rejects a `/` in its input, so a naive swap would have given every mod a name it cannot
  `Class.forName` — silently, with no error anywhere.
- **The reverse lookup answered nothing at all** from the keyed table, because the reverse map was keyed on the
  slashed spelling while a caller passes the dotted one.
- **`method_1514` mapped to `lambda$buildInitialScreens$8`** from the shipped table — the lossy join, visible in
  one lookup — and to `run` from the keyed table.

So the separator is no longer load-bearing in either direction. `KernelMappingResolver` reduces table keys and
query class names to the internal form and answers class names in binary form, which is what fabric-loader's
implementation does; a dotted query, which is the form that implementation documents, used to miss on **both**
tables and now hits. Measured after the change: replays of the two tables agree on **8,220 of 8,220** shared class
records — 0 differences — so the swap changes member names and nothing about class names. A three-field member
record now fails the load instead of being skipped.

## The numbers, from the resolver's own answers

`MappingTableAudit` wrote out what the resolver answered for every record; the verifier adjudicated *those*, not
the file. Against `patched-mc-merged-1.21.1.jar` (the game that runs), with `clean-mojmap.jar` as the unpatched
jar it was built from:

| | shipped table | descriptor-keyed table |
|---|---|---|
| records | 85,372 | **87,176** |
| answered by the resolver as written | 85,372 | 87,176 |
| names that exist at that owner+signature in the merged base | 61,699 | **85,874** |
| names that exist only in the UNPATCHED jar (patching/merge) | 812 | 1,302 |
| names that exist in NEITHER jar — a wrong mapping | **22,861** | **0** |
| distinct intermediary members claiming one Mojmap member | **3,830 (7,849 records)** | **0** |

The 1,302 are not excused, they are enumerated: **1,238 are compiler-synthesised** (`lambda$…`, `val$…`) whose
name or signature NeoForge's patching rewrote — `Stitcher.lambda$stitch$3` is the classic, the base carries the
same code under `lambda$stitch$4` — and **64 are real members** of classes the client+server merge takes from the
server side (`Entity.playCombinationStepSounds`, `BlockBehaviour.isSticky`) or anonymous classes whose numbering
patching changes. They resolve in the unpatched jar at exactly the stated owner and signature, which is what makes
them a property of the patched artifact rather than of the join. The verifier fails if a record resolves in
neither jar, so nothing can hide in that bucket.

The previous section's differential is reproduced from these answers rather than restated: on the **77,152** keys
both tables carry, **27,074** disagree, and of those **22,410** name a member that is not at that owner+signature
where the keyed name is (**11** the other way, 4,137 both — patched classes carry both lambda names — and 516
neither).

## What produces it, and where it goes

`forbric-kernel/build.gradle`, inside the existing `if (mcLine != mcLineDefault)` guard so the 26.2 graph gains no
task:

```
gradlew verifyRuntimeMappings -Pforbric.mcLine=1.21.1 -Pforbric.stagedRoot=.dev/staged-1211/run
  emitRuntimeMappings    join-keyed.py <build/namespace/mappings-1.21.1.tsv> --full
  auditRuntimeMappings   MappingTableAudit: every record through the resolver, answers to build/namespace/
  verifyRuntimeMappings  verify-runtime-table.py: the answers against the merged base and its unpatched origin
```

The table is generated into `build/`, never committed: it is a derived view of Mojang mappings, which cannot be
redistributed, and the join inputs live outside the repository (`-Pforbric.namespaceProbe`, defaulting to
`E:\project\namespace-probe`). The task is not part of `check` for that reason — it fails with a message naming the
missing input rather than quietly passing. A launch supplies it the way the resolver has always taken it:
`-Dforbric.mappings=<repo>/forbric-kernel/build/namespace/mappings-1.21.1.tsv`.

Two guards exist because this repository has already been bitten by shared staging filenames: the verifier refuses
an origin jar whose class-file major is not the base's, and the Gradle task reads the merged base's `.pins` sidecar
and refuses it if it says `mc=` something other than the line being verified. Measured while writing this: under
`.dev/staged-1211/run`, `patched-mc-merged-1.21.1.jar` and `patched-mc-merged-26.2.jar` are *byte-identical* and
both carry `mc=1.21.1` pins — the unversioned-filename accident, caught by the pins rather than by the name.

`KernelMappingResolverTableTest` grows from 6 to 9 tests, all synthetic and machine-independent: a slashed-valued
and a dotted-valued table answer identically in binary form; a dotted caller is answered instead of silently
missed; a truncated member record refuses the whole table.

## 26.2 is untouched

No table exists on that line (`-Dforbric.mappings` is absent), so `getNamespaces()` is still
`[official]` alone, every lookup is still the identity, and the table is never even attempted. The new code is all inside the table-present branch or in `Table.read`.

`JAVA_HOME=C:\Program Files\Java\jdk-25.0.2`, `GRADLE_USER_HOME=<repo>/.gradle-home`: clean `jar` →
`BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 12s`, 10 tasks executed (that is where the game side was compiled); `--dry-run
compileRuntimeJava` lists exactly `compileJava`, `processResources`, `classes`, `extractTransferApis`,
`verifyRebornEnergy`, `compileRuntimeJava`; `test --tests "net.forbric.kernel.fabric.*"` → **101 tests, 0 failures,
0 skipped**, of which `KernelMappingResolverTableTest` 9/0 and `KernelMappingResolverTest` 3/0. No
`normalizeRuntimeJava` or `remapTransferApis` in either graph.

## What is still not verified

- **No 1.21.1 kernel jar has ever been built and no 1.21.1 launch attempted.** A verified mapping table is one
  layer; it says nothing about whether the loader runs.
- The 1,302 records that disagree with the *patched* base are correct as mappings and absent from the artifact that
  runs. Nothing a Mojmap table can do fixes that: the names in the patched jar are not in Mojang's mapping file.
- The string-valued mixin selectors (`@Inject(method=…)`, `@At(target=…)`, `@Shadow` field names) are still yarn
  names and still wrong at runtime; this change does not touch them.
- `unmapClassName` still answers the internal (slashed) spelling of an intermediary name where fabric-loader's
  implementation answers the binary one. Its input is canonicalised now, so either form queries it; its output was
  left alone deliberately — it is a behaviour change in the direction no mod in this tree calls, and it is not the
  table's doing.
- The remaining API axes (99 errors on axis 1 at the last measurement) and the two classes that never merged are
  untouched and unaffected.

# Axes 2 and 3: two more renames, the transfer API measured against the oracle, and what 546 -> 526 is worth

Written after a1ef107/ac59d21/c4947f2/9ba960b. **Axis 2 and axis 3 were both taken as far as evidence allows,
and the answer for each is different:** axis 2 had two more genuine renames in it, and axis 3 has none at all --
it is one absent subsystem, so the whole of it is documented rather than patched. Measured with the same
procedure before and after, and with the 26.2 line re-verified the same three ways.

## The harness was gone, and that is why this section describes it

`build/x11/` is gitignored and the last round's copy did not survive, so every tool below was rebuilt and the
procedure is written down here rather than referenced. Two traps ate a round each and are now encoded in
`build/x11/measure.sh`:

- **`gradlew clean` deletes the harness.** It lives under `build/`, so the `clean jar` verification in this very
  section removed the scripts and every log with them. Write logs outside `build/`, or copy them out first.
- the three traps already in this document still apply and are all in `measure.sh`: `extractTransferApis` with
  the line's OWN `-Pforbric.fabricApi` first (the shared directory), the locale pin on javac (or the transcript
  is Chinese and the splitter's anchors do not exist), and an explicit `-d` (or the next `normalizeRuntimeJava`
  Sync deletes javac's output mid-run).

The tools: `dump-cp.gradle` (an `-I` init script that writes the runtime classpath and source list out),
`axis-split.py` (the axis splitter, rules in its header), `rename-sweep.py` (the counterpart sweep),
`near-names.py`, `retarget-jar.py`, `measure.sh`.

## The counts, before and after, measured identically

Both procedures again, because the energy jar is still a class-69 staging defect rather than an API
difference. `before` is `git show HEAD:forbric-kernel/src/runtime/mc-line/1.21.1.tsv` in place; `after` is the
table committed here. Same javac (JDK 21, `-Xmaxerrs 10000 --release 21`, locale-pinned), same 151 sources, same
classpath except the one variable.

| M1 -- energy jar as shipped (class-file 69) | TOTAL | axis 1 | axis 2 | axis 3 | cascades |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| before | **571** | 106 | 124 | 210 | 131 |
| after | **549** | 102 | 117 | 210 | 120 |

| M2 -- energy jar retargeted to class-file 65, both sides | TOTAL | axis 1 | axis 2 | axis 3 | cascades |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| before | **546** | 106 | 124 | 187 | 129 |
| after | **524** | 102 | 117 | 187 | 118 |

The totals reproduce the previous round's numbers exactly (571 and 546), which is what makes the pair
comparable; javac's own count is 571/549 on M1 and 547/525 on M2, the one-error gap being the same
`cannot access ParametersAreNonnullByDefault` line with no `file:line` that the last round recorded.

**The split is a stated reconstruction and its boundary differs from the last round's.** That round recorded
axis 2 = 138 / axis 3 = 139 (M2); this one records 124 / 187 on the same tree. The totals agree to the error,
so the difference is only where the two buckets meet: `axis-split.py` assigns every error that resolves into
`net.neoforged.neoforge.transfer.*` (or a fabric/reborn type) to axis 3, and the earlier splitter left part of
that block in cascades. The splitter's rules are in its header; the DELTA below does not depend on the boundary
at all, because it is a set difference of the transcripts.

**The delta is 22 errors, and it is a set difference of the two transcripts: 19 blocks removed, 1 new, in one
file.** The new one is not a regression -- it is a real difference the rename UNCOVERED, explained below.

```
removed   10 x net.minecraftforge.common.util.Result      KernelGameBlockEvents, KernelGameWorldEvents
removed    6 x net.minecraft.util.TriState                KernelGameBlockEvents
removed    3 x net.neoforged...block.BreakBlockEvent      KernelGameBlockEvents
added      1 x BlockEvent.BreakEvent constructor arity    KernelGameBlockEvents   (see below)
```

## Axis 2: what was a rename

Two, both **mutually exclusive**, both found by a sweep rather than by eye.

**1. `net.minecraftforge.common.util.Result` -> `net.minecraftforge.eventbus.api.Event.Result`.**
26.2 pulled MinecraftForge's decision enum OUT of `Event`; 1.21.1 still has it nested. Identical shape (javap
both): `ALLOW/DEFAULT/DENY` and `isAllowed/isDefault/isDenied`. Verified through the method that consumes it:

```
26.2    PlayerInteractEvent$RightClickBlock.getUseBlock() : net.minecraftforge.common.util.Result
1.21.1  PlayerInteractEvent$RightClickBlock.getUseBlock() : net.minecraftforge.eventbus.api.Event$Result
```

**2. `net.minecraft.util.TriState` -> `net.neoforged.neoforge.common.util.TriState`.**
26.2 adopted NeoForge's tri-state into vanilla. `net.minecraft.util.TriState` exists on 26.2 and on none of the
39 1.21.1 jars; `net.neoforged.neoforge.common.util.TriState` exists on 1.21.1 (javap: `TRUE/DEFAULT/FALSE`,
`isTrue/isDefault/isFalse`) and not in 26.2's `neoforge-runtime.jar`. Same consumer-method check:

```
26.2    (NeoForge) PlayerInteractEvent$RightClickBlock.getUseBlock() : net.minecraft.util.TriState
1.21.1  (NeoForge) PlayerInteractEvent$RightClickBlock.getUseBlock() :
                     net.neoforged.neoforge.common.util.TriState
```

Both are written as **fully-qualified rules only**, and that is load-bearing: the simple names do not change, so
a bare `Result` rule would fire inside its own target (`...Event.Result`) and inside
`net.minecraft.world.InteractionResult`, and a bare `TriState` rule would fire inside
`net.fabricmc.fabric.api.util.TriState`. Longest-first ordering cannot save that, because the simple name
survives the substitution. Rewriting the import alone is enough; every bare use resolves through it.

That `TriState` entry also **corrects the previous round's table**, which listed `net.minecraft.util.TriState`
as absent-with-no-counterpart and said "1.21.1's common.util has 90 classes and neither Result nor TriState".
`net.neoforged.neoforge.common.util.TriState` is in that package on 1.21.1.

## Axis 2: what was NOT a rename, and how exhaustively that was checked

The old tail was built by searching the three staged 1.21.1 jars for each absent name by simple name. That
cannot see a rename into a nested class, which is exactly the kind this round found, so it was redone against
the **whole measurement class path (39 jars)** and by word overlap rather than by simple name:
`rename-sweep.py` splits the absent name into CamelCase words and lists every class whose own words contain all
of them.

Measured on the final transcript: **49 distinct absent names, 36 with no candidate at all, 13 with at least
one, and NONE of those 13 is the same concept.** The closest is refused with corrected evidence:

- **`BreakBlockEvent` -> `net.neoforged.neoforge.event.level.BlockEvent.BreakEvent`, RULED this round.** The
  previous round refused it because "the only spelling that works is the `$` form ... which does compile".
  **It does not compile.** Measured with the JDK-21 javac against the 1.21.1 class path:

  ```
  import net.neoforged.neoforge.event.level.BlockEvent$BreakEvent;
  error: cannot find symbol
    symbol:   class BlockEvent$BreakEvent
    location: package net.neoforged.neoforge.event.level
  ```

  The **canonical** nested spelling is what compiles (`import ...BlockEvent.BreakEvent;` + bare `BreakEvent`),
  so the refusal rested on a false premise and the rule is in the table. Using it exposed the second half of
  the story, which is why the block was worth three errors: once the parameter type resolves, javac
  type-checks the body for the first time and finds a **real, independent** difference in MinecraftForge's own
  event:

  ```
  26.2    BlockEvent$BreakEvent(Level, BlockPos, BlockState, Player, net.minecraftforge.common.util.Result)
  1.21.1  BlockEvent$BreakEvent(Level, BlockPos, BlockState, Player)                      (javap both)
  ```

  No name rule can carry a seeded result through that constructor; on 1.21.1 it is `Event.setResult` after
  construction. So the rule is 3 symbol errors out and 1 honestly-visible call-site error in, and the next
  round's job in that file is a two-line rewrite rather than a type hunt.

- **`ForgeLayer`.** The tail said "no overlay-layer API before 26.x", which is **false**: 1.21.1 has
  `net.minecraftforge.client.gui.overlay.ForgeLayeredDraw` (javap: extends `net.minecraft.client.gui.LayeredDraw`,
  the named-layer constants, `add`/`addAbove`/`addBelow`/`putAbove`/`putBelow`/`move`/`addWithCondition`/
  `resolveLayers`). What 1.21.1 does not have is the *interface*: 26.2 introduced
  `ForgeLayer { void extract(GuiGraphicsExtractor, DeltaTracker); }`, which `ForgeLayeredDraw` implements, so on
  26.2 a layer is a Forge type. On 1.21.1 the same argument position is `LayeredDraw$Layer` with
  `render(GuiGraphics, DeltaTracker)` -- different method name *and* a parameter type that is itself absent on
  axis 1. Refused with the corrected fact.

Everything else is a whole API generation, and the sweep is what makes that a measurement rather than an
assumption. `BusGroup` is the cleanest example of a counterpart that exists but is not a name: 1.21.1 HAS
`net.minecraftforge.eventbus.api.BusBuilder`, so building a standalone bus is an expression
(`BusBuilder.builder().build()`) whose result has to go into `FMLModContainer`'s `eventBusGroup` field -- typed
`BusGroup` on 26.2. Also verified absent-with-no-counterpart on the full class path:
`neoforgespi.transformation.*` / `fml.classloading.transformation.*` (modlauncher's `TransformingClassLoader`
and `ClassTransformer` exist and are a **different platform**), `FMLMixinClassProcessor`, the keyed reload
listener registry (`ListenerKey`, `NeoForgeReloadListeners`, the `Vanilla*Listeners`), the sorted-listener
events, the `common.tooltip` rework, `InFluidPredicate`, `RecipeContentPayload`, `UnbakedModelParser`, the pip
pipeline, and `AnvilCraftEvent`.

One more refusal is worth naming because it is a *shape* difference on an interface `KernelModFile` implements:

```
26.2    IModFile.getContents() : JarContents     IModFile.getId() : String
1.21.1  IModFile.getSecureJar() : SecureJar      (no getContents, no getId; getDiscoveryAttributes instead)
```

`cpw.mods.jarhandling.SecureJar` exists on both lines, so the counterpart is there -- but the interface members
differ, and a type substitution would leave `IModFile` unimplemented (same total, different bucket).

## Axis 3: no rename exists, and what 1.21.1 actually has

The 187 errors on this axis are **seven 26.2 types in 13 files**, every one of them
`net.neoforged.neoforge.transfer.*`, and the package is **absent from the 1.21.1 jars entirely** (zero entries
matching `neoforge/transfer` in `neoforge-runtime.jar` and in `forge-runtime.jar`; the same grep finds it in
26.2's). So the question was asked as a question: what does 1.21.1 offer for the same job? Answered by `javap`
against the measurement class path, which is the oracle:

| 26.2 type | 1.21.1 counterpart | why it is not a substitution |
|---|---|---|
| `transfer.ResourceHandler<T extends Resource>` | `net.neoforged.neoforge.items.IItemHandler` / `fluids.capability.IFluidHandler` | javap 1.21.1: `getSlots/getStackInSlot/insertItem(int,ItemStack,boolean)/extractItem(int,int,boolean)/getSlotLimit/isItemValid`. ItemStack instead of a typed resource, a `boolean simulate` instead of a `TransactionContext`, no `long` amounts. |
| `transfer.item.ItemResource` | `ItemStack` | the same stack MINUS the count |
| `transfer.fluid.FluidResource` | `net.neoforged.neoforge.fluids.FluidStack` | same |
| `transfer.energy.EnergyHandler` | `net.neoforged.neoforge.energy.IEnergyStorage` | present on **both** lines; different method set (`receiveEnergy`/`extractEnergy`/`getEnergyStored`/...) |
| `transfer.transaction.TransactionContext` | **none** | the only `TransactionContext` on the 1.21.1 class path is fabric's own, which is the fabric half of the bridge |
| `transfer.transaction.SnapshotJournal` | **none** | the word-overlap sweep returns 0 candidates in all 39 jars |
| `transfer.resource.Resource` | **none** | - |
| `capabilities.Capabilities.Item/Fluid/Energy` | `Capabilities.ItemHandler/FluidHandler/EnergyStorage` | **looks like the cleanest rename on the axis and is not.** The nested class was renamed, and the capability's value type changed with it: 26.2's `Capabilities.Item.BLOCK` is a `BlockCapability<ResourceHandler<ItemResource>, Direction>`, 1.21.1's `Capabilities.ItemHandler.BLOCK` is one over `IItemHandler`. Substituting turns "cannot find symbol" into "incompatible types: IItemHandler cannot be converted to ResourceHandler<ItemResource>" -- the same lateral move measured for `ContainerStorage` before the fabric modules were remapped, except that here no remap can make it right. |

**The blocker that outlives the types is the transaction.** The bridge relies on 26.2's
`TransactionContext`/`SnapshotJournal` to roll back an insert that half-succeeds across two APIs. The 1.21.1
handler API has no transaction at all -- `insertItem(..., simulate)` is the whole mechanism -- so a port has to
supply snapshot/rollback itself. That is code, not a name, which is why no rule was written and why the count on
this axis did not move.

**Positive finding, for whoever does it:** 26.2 still ships the legacy handler API (`items.IItemHandler`,
`ItemStackHandler`, `ItemHandlerHelper`, `fluids.capability.IFluidHandler`, `energy.IEnergyStorage` are all in
26.2's `neoforge-runtime.jar`, and 26.2 adds `items.ItemResourceHandlerAdapter`, which adapts a handler INTO the
new API). So one source tree that speaks handlers can compile against both lines -- the restructure is
available. What it is not free of is the behaviour question: on 26.2 a mod may register a native
`ResourceHandler`, and a bridge that only asks for handlers would stop seeing it. That decision belongs to
whoever does the port.

## Corrections this round makes to the table itself

- `net.minecraft.util.TriState` was listed as absent with no counterpart; the counterpart is
  `net.neoforged.neoforge.common.util.TriState` and it is now a rule.
- `net.minecraftforge.common.util.Result` was listed the same way; the counterpart is the nested
  `net.minecraftforge.eventbus.api.Event$Result` and it is now a rule.
- "the `$` form ... does compile" (under `BreakBlockEvent`) is wrong; it does not compile. The canonical nested
  spelling does, so `BreakBlockEvent` is a rule now too.
- "no overlay-layer API before 26.x" (under `ForgeLayer`) is wrong; `ForgeLayeredDraw` is on 1.21.1.

## 26.2 is untouched: the three checks, run again

`JAVA_HOME=C:\Program Files\Java\jdk-25.0.2`, `GRADLE_USER_HOME=<repo>/.gradle-home`, no property passed:

1. **Clean build.** `gradlew.bat --no-daemon clean jar` -> `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 27s`, 10 actionable tasks all
   executed, and the compiler warnings name `src\runtime\java\...` -- the un-normalised tree -- with zero

2. **Fresh runtime compile.** `rm -rf build/classes/java/runtime` then `compileRuntimeJava` ->

   `:processResources`, `:classes`, `:extractTransferApis`, `:verifyRebornEnergy`, `:compileRuntimeJava`.
3. **The line switch is not sticky.** A 1.21.1 `compileRuntimeJava` ran `:normalizeRuntimeJava` (46 rules
   matched on the final table) and `:remapTransferApis`, then **failed** at `:compileRuntimeJava` as expected;
   immediately after, `rm -rf build/classes/java/runtime` and a plain `compileRuntimeJava` -> `BUILD SUCCESSFUL

The full suite on 26.2, reproduced this round rather than quoted: **5,627 tests, 79 failures, 842 ignored**
(`BUILD FAILED in 28m 1s`), and the 79 decompose exactly as the baseline says -- **77
`java.io.IOException at TempDirectory.java:350`** plus exactly two `AssertionFailedError`, at
`ForgeRegistrationA2ContractTest.java:143` (`clientGateSeedsF6AndBothScriptsParse`) and
`KernelRuntimeClassesTest.java:93` (`everyGameSideClassTheBootSideNamesIsInTheRegistry`). No regression, and
neither of those two was "fixed". Two notes on running it here: it needs ~28 minutes, and a run killed

`java.io.EOFException` -- delete `build/test-results` and `build/tmp/test` before retrying.

## What is still not verified

- **The 1.21.1 line still does not compile**, and no 1.21.1 kernel jar has ever been built or launched. 524
  errors (M2) is a count, not a build.
- **The two new rules are verified by compilation and by javap on both lines, not at runtime.** That
  `Result.DENY` and `TriState.FALSE` do what their 1.21.1 namesakes do is an argument from the enum constants

- **The measured effect of axis 2's work is 22 errors out of 546.** The remaining 187 on axis 3 need the
  handler-side restructure described above; axis 1's 102 and the 118 cascades are untouched by this round.
- The **energy jar** is still a locally retargeted class-69 jar passed with `-Pforbric.rebornEnergy=`; the
  retarget was redone this round (`build/x11/retarget-jar.py`, 12 classes, version bytes only, every other byte

- The **remapped fabric modules are still compile-time only**; the string-valued mixin selectors are still yarn

- The **axis splitter's boundary is a reconstruction** (see above). The totals are exact; the per-axis columns
