# Stage 4: the 1.21.1 certificate — the removed provider IS the carrier, so the proof keys on `baseClass`

This is the stage the previous round handed over. Its refusals are the premise: the runtime half of the capability
subsystem compiles on 1.21.1 but nothing installs it, and `ForgeCapabilityProtocol.protocol()` returns false on this
line for a reason that is in the bytecode. **The decision is written here before the code**, and the code, the counts
and the verification are appended below the decision.

## Decision, in one line

`ForgeCapabilityProtocol` gets a **second certificate shape**, selected by what the merged base's own manifest calls
the removed superclass — not by a property, not by a version string. When the removed class *is*
`net/minecraftforge/common/capabilities/CapabilityProvider`, the certificate must prove that the **Class argument** is
what selects the `AttachCapabilitiesEvent` subtype, because on this line that argument is the *only* place the
per-root distinction still exists.

## Why `protocol()`'s preconditions cannot be met on 1.21.1 — five checks, each a bytecode fact

The manifest rows are the same for all three roots on 1.21.1 (`sourceSuperclass` = `CapabilityProvider`), where 26.2
names a different `$`-nested provider per root. `protocol()` was written against the 26.2 rows, so every one of its
carrier preconditions asks about a class that on 1.21.1 is something else:

| `protocol()` asks | 26.2 (`removed` = `CapabilityProvider$Entities`) | 1.21.1 (`removed` = `CapabilityProvider`) |
|---|---|---|
| `BASE.equals(nativeProvider.superName)` | true — the nested class extends `CapabilityProvider` | **false** — `super = java/lang/Object` |
| `nativeProvider.fields.isEmpty()` | true — the nested class adds no state | **false** — 8 instance fields **plus** `SUPPORTS_LAZY_CAPABILITIES` (`baseClass:Class`, `capabilities`, `valid`, `isLazy`, `lazyParentSupplier`, `lazyData`, `registryAccess`, `initialized`) |
| `AsField.<init>(L…ICapabilityProviderImpl;Z)V` | true | **false** — this line has `<init>(Ljava/lang/Class;L…ICapabilityProviderImpl;)V` and the three-argument `(Class, B, Z)`; there is no `(B, Z)` constructor |
| `kind = removed.substring(lastIndexOf('$')+1)` | `"Entities"` → `KernelForgeCapabilities$Entities` | **no `$`** → the whole internal name → `KernelForgeCapabilities$net/minecraftforge/…` → `null` |
| the two abstract hooks | `fireAttachCapabilitiesEvent(B)` and `shouldFireAttachCapabilitiesEvent()` are `protected abstract`, which is exactly what `KernelForgeCapabilities$Entities/$BlockEntities/$Levels` implement | **absent**. `CapabilityProvider` declares no abstract method at all (`javap -p`: `abstractMethods=[]`), and `gatherCapabilities()` is `protected final` on both lines |

So the 26.2 subclass model has **no shape to occupy** here: there is no abstract method to supply and no non-final
hook to override. That is not a defect to be worked around; it is the line saying where its per-root key lives.

## The measurement that turns that into a design, not a compromise

`doGatherCapabilities` on 1.21.1, disassembled:

```
aload_0
aload_0 ; getfield baseClass:Ljava/lang/Class                   <- the Class ARGUMENT
aload_0 ; invokevirtual getProvider()L…ICapabilityProviderImpl; <- AsField.getProvider() returns `owner`
aload_1                                                         <- the optional parent
invokestatic ForgeEventFactory.gatherCapabilities(Class, ICapabilityProvider, ICapabilityProvider)
putfield capabilities ; initialized = true
```

and `ForgeEventFactory.gatherCapabilities(Class<? extends T>, T, ICapabilityProvider)` builds
`new AttachCapabilitiesEvent<>(baseClass, provider)` and posts it on the **one generic bus**. On 26.2 the same job was
done by `$Entities.fireAttachCapabilitiesEvent` naming `AttachCapabilitiesEvent.Entities`. **The per-root key moved
from the class to the argument.** That is why one provider class for three roots is correct on this line and a loss
on the other — and it is why the certificate's obligation changes from *"the removed class is the per-root provider"*
to *"the removed class is the state carrier, and the Class the delegate supplies is what the carrier keys the event
on"*.

A second, load-bearing corollary, also measured rather than assumed: `AsField(Class, B)` delegates to
`CapabilityProvider(Class)` → `(Class, false)`, and `isLazy = SUPPORTS_LAZY_CAPABILITIES && eager` — a tautological
`false` on that path whatever the static's value (it is `true`, from `<clinit>`: `iconst_1; putstatic; return`). With
`isLazy == false`, `getCapability` reads `getCapabilities()`, which cannot gather, and `capabilities` is null until
something calls `initInternal()`. So **an un-initialised provider is empty forever** on this line, which is what makes
`Inert` a real state (never initialise) instead of an override answering false. `initInternal()`'s own three
instructions are `aload_0; invokevirtual gatherCapabilities; return` — pinning them is pinning the eager path.

## The routes, and why five of them are worse

- **A. RELAX THE PROOF: emit a certificate whose `protocol()` half is "the transform ran".** Refused. It is the shim
  the task forbids, and it is worse than refusing: the manifest (`META-INF/forbric/required-ancestor-compositions.tsv`)
  exists precisely so a stateful ancestor cannot disappear silently. A proof that asserts nothing makes
  `RequiredAncestorCompositions.verify` decorative, and the next real regression in the carrier would define broken
  classes with a clean log.
- **B. SYNTHESISE 1.21.1 ANALOGUES OF `$Entities/$BlockEntities/$Levels` (one subclass of the carrier per root) AND
  KEEP `protocol()` AS IS.** Refused. Each such class would carry a **second copy** of the carrier's eight fields, and
  the event key would be the *subclass's* `Class`, not the owner's — so `AttachCapabilitiesEvent<Entity>` would only
  be constructed correctly if the subclass hard-coded it, which is rebuilding the 26.2 model out of a line that
  deliberately deleted its hook. `gatherCapabilities()` being `final` means there is no seam to build it from.
- **C. REUSE ONE DELEGATE (e.g. `Entities`) FOR ALL THREE ROOTS.** Refused, and this one is not cosmetic: `baseClass`
  would be `Entity.class` for a `Level`, so every `AttachCapabilitiesEvent<Level>` listener would silently never fire.
  A type error in the single argument the whole model now depends on, and no compiler would see it.
- **D. PORT THE RUNTIME HALF ONLY, LEAVING THE CERTIFICATE NULL (the previous round's refusal, restated).** Refused
  again, same reason: a class that compiles and that nothing constructs. The count falls; the blocker stays.
- **E. DO NOTHING (the previous round's choice).** Untenable now, and in a different way than before: it was the right
  call when the shape was unknown. The shape is measured, and leaving it means every later stage reports its count
  against a base that cannot be defined.
- **F. A SECOND CERTIFICATE SHAPE, SELECTED BY THE MANIFEST'S OWN `sourceSuperclass` (chosen).** No property, no
  version string: the manifest already says which class the merge dropped, so it already says which shape applies.
  A `removed` naming a `$`-nested class is 26.2's shape; a `removed` that **is** the carrier is this line's.

## What the 1.21.1 certificate has to prove (the obligations, named before they are coded)

P1. **The removed class is the root's actual source superclass** — implied by the manifest row and enforced by
    `Certificate.proves` (`removed.equals(r.sourceSuperclass())`).
P2. **The carrier's superclass is `java/lang/Object` and its field set is exactly the pinned one**, so delegating to
    it loses nothing: on 26.2 this check was `fields.isEmpty()` on a subclass; on 1.21.1 the state is *in* the removed
    class and the delegate inherits it, so "no unaccounted state" becomes "the inherited state is exactly this".
P3. **The carrier's `<init>(Class)` and `<init>(Class, boolean)` token streams are the pinned ones** — the second pins
    the field initialisation order and `isLazy = SUPPORTS_LAZY_CAPABILITIES && eager`.
P4. **`AsField` extends the removed class, declares exactly `owner`, and its `(Class, B)` constructor / `initInternal`
    / `serializeInternal` / `deserializeInternal` are the pinned token streams** — those are the four access points
    the composition calls, and `initInternal`'s body is the eager gather.
P5. **`doGatherCapabilities`'s token stream is the pinned one**, proving the event key is the `Class` argument.
    Without this the "one provider, three roots" step would be an inference; with it, it is a proof.
P6. **The delegate for THIS root exists, chains `$<Root> extends $Composed extends $AsField`, and its constructor
    passes the pinned `Class` literal** — `ldc Entity.class` / `BlockEntity.class` / `Level.class`. That is the exact
    replacement for 26.2's per-root provider class: the per-root thing is now a constant, and the certificate pins it.
P7. **The carrier declares no abstract method**, so there is no override site to drop (contrast 26.2, where the two
    `protected abstract` hooks *are* the contract).

## What this stage does NOT decide, and says so before coding

The composition's **dispatch** half (the `ForgeCaps` save/load funnels, `ServerLevel.initCapabilities`,
`LevelChunk.capProvider`, the lost field initializers) is a different question from the **definition** blocker, and
its anchors are different classes on this line. It is ported where a 1.21.1 anchor exists and stands down — with its
existing warning, which names the anchor — where it does not. Every stand-down is listed by name in the verification
section; none of them is a silent pass, and none of them is blamed on the certificate.

The previous round measured that porting the runtime half alone removes **29** errors from
`KernelForgeCapabilities.java` (14 axis-2 + 6 axis-1 + 9 cascades). This stage does not chase those: they fall out of
doing the work honestly, in the other order — the certificate is written first and the runtime half is written to the
shape the certificate can prove. If the 29 fall, that is reported as a consequence.

## The port

Two files carry the decision, and one new file is the runtime half. Nothing is stubbed, and the three places that
could have been faked are named in the code.

### 1. `ForgeCapabilityProtocol` — two shapes, selected by the manifest

`protocol(removed, owner, reader)` now dispatches on the row it was handed:

```
BASE.equals(removed)  ->  selfProvider(...)     // 1.21.1: the removed class IS the carrier
else                  ->  nestedProvider(...)   // 26.2: the removed class is a $nested subclass
```

`nestedProvider` is the previous body, **moved and not otherwise touched** — same pinned token lists, same
`fireAttachCapabilitiesEvent` comparison, same "no additional source state" sweep. `selfProvider` is new and is the
obligations P1–P7 written as checks:

| check | what it pins, in tokens |
|---|---|
| carrier super | `java/lang/Object` |
| carrier state | the nine fields, `access:name:desc`, in declaration order — `SUPPORTS_LAZY_CAPABILITIES`, `baseClass`, `capabilities`, `valid`, `isLazy`, `lazyParentSupplier`, `lazyData`, `registryAccess`, `initialized` |
| carrier constructors | `<init>(Class)` and the eleven-store `<init>(Class, boolean)`, the second of which is where `isLazy = SUPPORTS_LAZY_CAPABILITIES && eager` lives |
| **the event key** | `doGatherCapabilities`: `getfield baseClass`, `getProvider()`, `ForgeEventFactory.gatherCapabilities(Class, ICapabilityProvider, ICapabilityProvider)`, `putfield capabilities`, `initialized = true` |
| carrier has no hooks | no `ACC_ABSTRACT` member — so nothing is being overridden away |
| `AsField` | `super = carrier`, exactly `owner`, `<init>(Class, B)` = `[v25:0, v25:1, carrier.<init>(Class), v25:0, v25:2, putfield owner, return]`, and `initInternal`/`serializeInternal`/`deserializeInternal` bodies |
| the delegate | `$<Kind> extends $Composed extends $AsField`, `$Composed.<init>(Class, Object)` = `[aload0, aload1, aload2, checkcast ICapabilityProviderImpl, invokespecial AsField.<init>(Class, B), return]`, and `dispatcher()` |
| **the per-root key** | the delegate's own constructor: `[aload0, ldc <Root>.class, aload1, invokespecial Composed.<init>(Class, Object), return]` — the Class literal, pinned |
| the factory | `KernelForgeCapabilities.create`: `aload0, invokevirtual AsField.initInternal, aload0, areturn` |
| the wiring | `KernelForgeCapabilities.entity/blockEntity/level`: one `ifnull`, one delegate, one `create` — so a root wired to another root's delegate fails |

`Certificate` grew one component, `accessorHelper`, and one check beside the existing field/delegate/seam checks: the
final definition's own `forbric$caps()` must reach the factory this certificate was issued for. It is `null` on the
nested shape (26.2 has no such link to pin) and it is `inert` when dispatch is off — so the switch itself is certified
rather than skipped.

**Two changes to shared machinery, both justified in place:**

- `token()` learned loadable constants: `c:<descriptor>`. Without it a `Class` literal is indistinguishable from any
  other `ldc` (`o18`), and P6 could not exist. The opcode is deliberately **not** in the token: `ldc` and `ldc_w` are
  the same constant and which one javac emits depends on the constant-pool index, i.e. on unrelated edits.
  None of 26.2's four pinned token lists contains an `ldc`, so nothing on that line changes — checked by the 26.2
  capability suite (below), not by inspection alone.
- `certificate(...)` takes `dispatch`, because the accessor's factory is where the switch shows up on this line.

### 2. `ForgeCapabilityCompositionTransformer` — one composition, a line's worth of differences

`compose()` is unchanged except for **one omission**: `serializeCaps(ValueOutput)` is not emitted on the SELF shape,
because `ValueInput`/`ValueOutput` do not exist on this line. Everything else the composition adds — the interface,
the field, the accessor, `getCapability`, `invalidateCaps`, `reviveCaps`, `gatherCapabilities`, `getCapabilities`,
`serializeCaps(HolderLookup.Provider)`, `deserializeCaps(HolderLookup.Provider, CompoundTag)` — has the same name,
descriptor and bytecode on both lines, so the accessor and seven of the eight delegates are literally the same
emission. That is worth stating because it is the strongest evidence that the per-root difference really did collapse
to one argument: **26.2's composed Entity and 1.21.1's composed Entity differ by exactly the class of one method.**

The dispatch half is where the two lines part, and each part is anchored on something that was verified:

- **`CompoundTag` save/load seam** (previous round's item 3). New: `saveBlockEntity`/`loadBlockEntity` on
  `saveAdditional/loadAdditional(CompoundTag, HolderLookup.Provider)` and `saveEntity`/`loadEntity` on
  `saveWithoutId(CompoundTag)`/`load(CompoundTag)`. `saveWithoutId` returns `CompoundTag`, so its funnel counts
  `ARETURN` and inserts **above** the return value — stack-neutral there, and no instruction has to be found relative
  to it. Each funnel raises `maxStack` to what the four pushes need.
- **`ServerLevel.initCapabilities`.** 26.2's anchor, `LevelAttachmentsSavedData.init`, is a NeoForge class that does
  not exist here. Measured: the method **survived** the merge on this base (it is declared, and it still calls
  `gatherCapabilities`, `getDataStorage` and `getCapabilities`); only Forge's call at the constructor's end was lost
  with the superclass. So the port restores the call site and nothing else. A first draft synthesised
  `initCapabilities()` as well; the measurement says that half is not needed on this base, so the synthesis was
  **removed** rather than shipped as code no run exercises.
- **`LevelChunk.capProvider`.** 26.2 anchors on a `PUTFIELD` of `unsavedListener`; that field does not exist on this
  line. The final field *did* survive with nothing writing it (`javap`: one declaration, zero stores). The 1.21.1
  anchor is the constructor's own `super(...)` call — the one statement that is certainly there and certainly
  precedes a legal write to a final field — and `initInternal()` goes before the constructor's single `RETURN`, which
  is where Forge put it. A first draft counted *any* `INVOKESPECIAL <init>` as the super call and bailed out because
  this constructor also allocates `Heightmap`s and sections; the anchor is now `owner == node.superName`.
- **Unchanged and working on both lines, measured rather than assumed:** `Entity.remove → invalidateCaps` and
  `Entity.revive → reviveCaps` already appear in the merged 1.21.1 base (so `insertAfterCall` stands down on its
  "already there" branch), `BlockEntity.setRemoved` calls NeoForge's `invalidateCapabilities()` once and gets the
  Forge call after it, and `replayLostInitializer` needs only `reviveCaps()` — which this line has
  (`LivingEntity.reviveCaps` = `[aload0, invokespecial Entity.reviveCaps, aload0, aload0, invokestatic
  EntityEquipmentInvWrapper.create, putfield handlers, return]`, a straight line starting `ALOAD 0`).

### 3. `src/runtime/mc-line/1.21.1/java/…/KernelForgeCapabilities.java` — the runtime half

A 23rd overlay file, replacing the shared one for this line only (the 26.2 file is untouched). `Composed` forwards the
root's `Class` to `AsField`; `Entities`/`BlockEntities`/`Levels` differ **only** in that literal; `Inert` is not an
override but the absence of `initInternal()`; the factories are unchanged in name and descriptor, so the synthesised
accessor is byte-identical across lines.

Two behaviours are stated in the code because they differ from 26.2 and both are load-bearing:

- `Inert` is a **state**: `AsField(Class, B)` reaches `CapabilityProvider(Class, false)`, so `isLazy` is false and
  `capabilities` stays null until someone calls `initInternal()`. `getCapability` therefore answers
  `LazyOptional.empty()` forever, `serializeCaps` returns null and `deserializeCaps` is a no-op — the same end state
  26.2 reaches by marking an eager provider initialised with no dispatcher, arrived at from the other side.
- `injectCapabilities` is an **instance** API here: `CapabilityManager.injectCapabilities(ModList)` and
  `ModList.get().getAllScanData()`. Same change stage 2 made in `KernelForgeDeclarationReaders`.

**The overlay compiles on its own, against the real carriers** — that is not a convenience, it is what makes the
certificate a test of real bytes:

```
build/x11/compile-1211-runtime.sh
  javac --release 21 -cp <.dev/staged-1211/run/merged-base/…-1.21.1.jar ; …/forge-runtime.jar ;
                       build/classes/java/main ; forgespi-7.1.5 ; asm-9.10.1>
  -> 6 class files: KernelForgeCapabilities{, $Composed, $Entities, $BlockEntities, $Levels, $Inert}
```

### 4. The proof: `ForgeCapabilityComposition1211Test`

Where the acceptance criterion now lives. It reads the real 1.21.1 merged base and the real 1.21.1 carrier, composes
the three roots, and calls the **same** method `ForbricClassLoader.define` calls:

```java
RequiredAncestorCompositions contracts = new RequiredAncestorCompositions();
contracts.register(new ForgeCapabilityCompositionTransformer(reader));
contracts.verify("net.minecraft.world.entity.Entity", emitted, reader);   // no LinkageError
```

and, as the control, the same call against a loader with **no** proof registered, which must throw. Both directions
are asserted for all three roots, so the test cannot pass by `verify` becoming a no-op.

It also asserts that the row this subsystem does **not** cover is not silently claimed: the base's fourth row,
`EnderDragonPart`, must still be refused by the loader after the capability proof is registered, because that is
`PartEntity`'s composition and nothing here touches it.

Four adversarial controls, each a one-line lie about one input:

| lie | must be refused because |
|---|---|
| `$Levels.class` replaced by `$Entities.class` | the Class literal is the per-root key; a `Level` composed from the Entity delegate posts a `Level` to `AttachCapabilitiesEvent<Entity>` and loses every listener |
| a `NOP` inserted into `doGatherCapabilities` | same meaning, different bytecode: the proof is a fingerprint, and P5 is what makes "one provider, three roots" a proof instead of an inference |
| a field added to the carrier | unknown inherited state cannot be dismissed because the delegates still compile |
| `initInternal()` emptied to a bare `RETURN` | it *is* the eager gather on this line |

and one non-adversarial one: a definition with the composed field removed must still be refused, which is what keeps
the certificate about the artifact and not about the transform having run.

## The counts, and exactly how far they can be trusted this round

The previous round measured the whole 151-source set by hand-javac (M2: **263** total, axis1 99 / axis2 87 / axis3 0 /
cascades 77). **That number is not reproduced here, and this section says why before it says anything else.**

The harness for it lives in `forbric-kernel/build/x11/`, which is gitignored — and the four acceptance checks below
begin with `gradlew.bat clean jar`, which deletes it. That is not a surprise (this document says so, twice), but this
round stashed only the parts it needed and **`axis-split.py`, `measure.sh`, `dump-cp.gradle` and the retargeted
`energy-5.0.0-j21.jar` were lost.** What could be rebuilt from the documented procedure was rebuilt: the classpath
dump (an init script that prints `sourceSets.runtime.compileClasspath`) and the 151-source list. The **axis splitter
was not**, because its boundary is "this round's rules" by construction — writing a second one would produce numbers
that look comparable and are not, which is the exact error an earlier revision of this document made and then
withdrew.

So the counts below are a **set difference under one fixed setup**, and nothing else:

| | TOTAL (javac's own) |
|---|---|
| before this round's source change | **581** |
| after | **552** |
| removed | **29** |
| added | **0** |

Same javac (`JDK 21`, `-Xmaxerrs 10000 --release 21 -encoding UTF-8`, `-J-Duser.language=en -J-Duser.country=US`,

(`22 file(s) REPLACED` before, `23` after). Keyed on `(file, message)`, line numbers excluded:

```
removed  18  KernelForgeCapabilities.java   cannot find symbol
          8  KernelForgeCapabilities.java   method does not override or implement a method from a supertype
          1  KernelForgeCapabilities.java   incompatible types: ICapabilityProviderImpl cannot be converted to Class

          1  KernelForgeCapabilities.java   non-static method getAllScanData() cannot be referenced from a static context
```

**That is the 29 the previous round predicted** (14 axis-2 + 6 axis-1 + 9 cascades), and it fell out of doing the

**What is deliberately NOT claimed:** the absolute 581/552 is *not* comparable to the document's 288/263 for the same

- **No per-axis breakdown**, so no axis-3 identity. Previous rounds used `axis3 == 0 on M2` as the control that the

- **M1, not M2.** The retargeted class-69 energy jar went with `clean`, so both runs read the shipped class-69 jar.

## The acceptance criterion, measured

`build/x11/compile-1211-runtime.sh` compiles the overlay against the real 1.21.1 carriers, then
`ForgeCapabilityComposition1211Test` reads the real 1.21.1 merged base and calls
`RequiredAncestorCompositions.verify` — the method `ForbricClassLoader.define` runs before defining a class.

```
./gradlew.bat --no-daemon test --tests '*ForgeCapabilityComposition1211Test*'
ForgeCapabilityComposition1211Test  8 tests  0 failed  0 skipped
```

| root | manifest row | before | after |
|---|---|---|---|
| `net/minecraft/world/entity/Entity` | `AttachmentHolder` ← `CapabilityProvider` | `LinkageError` (no protocol proved the definition) | **accepted** |
| `net/minecraft/world/level/Level` | `AttachmentHolder` ← `CapabilityProvider` | `LinkageError` | **accepted** |
| `net/minecraft/world/level/block/entity/BlockEntity` | `AttachmentHolder` ← `CapabilityProvider` | `LinkageError` | **accepted** |
| `net/minecraft/world/entity/boss/EnderDragonPart` | `PartEntity` ← `net/minecraftforge/entity/PartEntity` | `LinkageError` | **still `LinkageError`, on purpose** — not this subsystem's row |

The "before" column is measured in the same test, not quoted: the same emitted bytes are handed to a loader with no
composition registered and `verify` is asserted to throw. So the acceptance is two-sided: the proof is registered and
accepted, and without it the same bytes are refused. `theRealBaseIsProvedForTheThreeRootsItRequires` fails if either
direction stops holding.

The 26.2 capability suite is the other half of the acceptance — the shape change must not move that line:

```
--tests '*ForgeCapability*' --tests '*MergedBaseCapability*' --tests '*RequiredAncestorCompositions*'
--tests '*CapabilityUseAudit*' --tests '*KernelForgeCapabilitiesShape*'
50 tests, 0 failed
```

which includes `ForgeCapabilityProtocolTest.withDispatchOffEveryRowOfTheRealManifestIsStillProvedAndLostStateIsStillRejected`
and `ForgeCapabilityDispatchOffExecutionTest.withDispatchOffTheRequiredRootsDefineThroughTheRealLoader` — the two that
a careless `accessorHelper` check broke, and which is why the certificate pins `inert` rather than skipping the link
when the switch is off.

## One finding about the Gradle line switch, recorded because it will waste the next round's time

`-Pforbric.mcLine=1.21.1` on its own does **not** compile the runtime against the 1.21.1 carriers: `build.gradle:44`
names the merged base by its 26.2 filename, so the classpath is whatever `-Pforbric.stagedRoot` points at. The 1.21.1
tree sidesteps that by keeping a copy of its own base under the 26.2 filename (measured: the file is byte-length
identical to `patched-mc-merged-1.21.1.jar` and its manifest says `CapabilityProvider`, not `$Entities`). Passing
`-Pforbric.stagedRoot=.dev/staged-1211/run` therefore does *not* give a clean 1.21.1 Gradle compile either: it fails
one task earlier, at `:remapTransferApis`, which verifies the remapped modules against that same hardcoded path and
reports `30 reference(s) … do not resolve … first: class net/minecraft/resources/Identifier`. **The documented
measurement path is the hand-javac one**, and the Gradle line switch is check 3 above — a switch, not a compile.

## What is still not verified, stated plainly

- **No 1.21.1 kernel jar has ever been built and no 1.21.1 launch has been attempted.** A certificate that verifies
  is a proof about bytes, not a game. Nothing in this stage — or any stage before it — has executed on 1.21.1.
- **The overlay's six class files have never been loaded by a JVM as part of a kernel.** They were compiled against
  the real carriers and they are what the certificate fingerprints, which is why the proof is about real bytes; that
  is the whole of it.
- **The certificate is proved by a test that calls `RequiredAncestorCompositions.verify` directly**, not inside
  `ForbricClassLoader.define`. The loader path differs only by being reached through `define`; the test asserts both
  directions of `verify` (refused without a proof, accepted with one) and the manifest is the base's own file, so what
  is not exercised is the loader plumbing, not the proof.
- **Nothing here is a launch.** `ServerLevel.initCapabilities` is now called at the constructor's end and
  `LevelChunk.capProvider` is written, but no level has been created, no chunk loaded, no `AttachCapabilitiesEvent`
  posted, and no capability read. The two `ForgeCaps` funnels have never round-tripped a tag.
- **Two judgement calls are named in the code rather than tested**: `LevelChunk`'s provider is written immediately
  after `super(...)` where Forge wrote it three statements later (the value depends on `this` and a literal only), and
  `saveWithoutId`'s funnel is inserted above the return value rather than before the instruction that pushes it.
- **The dropped `serializeCaps(ValueOutput)` overload is a difference, not an equivalent**: on 1.21.1 nothing calls a
  `ValueOutput` shape because the class does not exist, so there is no seam to port — but it also means a
  1.21.1-specific save path is whatever `saveAdditional(CompoundTag, HolderLookup.Provider)` does, and only that.
- **`build/x11` was destroyed by the mandated `clean` and only partly rebuilt.** `axis-split.py`, `measure.sh`, the
  retargeted energy jar and the previous rounds' transcripts are gone from this machine. The next round that wants a
  per-axis number must reconstruct the splitter, and should know that its reconstruction will not be comparable to
  the numbers in this document unless the classpath is too.
- **The whole-set totals in this document were not re-derived.** The delta (29 removed, 0 added) is measured; the
  totals preceding it are the previous round's, and the 581/552 here belong to a different, incomplete reconstruction.

# Stage 5: the harness rebuilt and a baseline re-established, then three member-level groups ported — 234 -> 220

This stage had two jobs in a fixed order. First, the measurement harness that the previous round's mandated
`gradlew clean jar` destroyed had to be rebuilt, because a round that cannot measure must not report a number.
Second, three groups on axis 2 were taken. Everything below is measured on the rebuilt harness, and **the
absolute numbers here are a NEW BASELINE, not a continuation of the ones above** — that is stated first because
it is the thing a reader would otherwise get wrong.

## Task A — the harness, rebuilt, and said to be a rebuild

Lost to `clean`: `axis-split.py`, `measure.sh` and the retargeted `energy-5.0.0-j21.jar`. Surviving and reused
unchanged: `setdiff.py`, `dump-cp.gradle`, `overlay-rules.py`, `probe.sh`, `compile-1211-runtime.sh`. Rebuilt:

- **`retarget-jar.py` + `energy-5.0.0-j21.jar`** — a copy of `.dev/api/energy-5.0.0.jar` with the u2 major-version
  field of each class file rewritten 69 -> 65 and **nothing else**. 12 class files, 13 non-class entries copied
  untouched, and the claim is checked structurally after writing: bytes 0..5 equal, version field equals 65,
  **bytes 8.. equal** — 12 bytes differ in the whole jar, all of them inside that field (69 -> 65 is
  `0x0045` -> `0x0041`, so in practice only offset 7 moves; an assertion on "two bytes differ" would have been
  asserting the decimal spelling rather than the field, and that is how the first version of the check failed).
  The ABI check is reproduced, not quoted: `gradlew verifyRebornEnergy
  -Pforbric.rebornEnergy=build/x11/energy-5.0.0-j21.jar` prints `API binary contract verified`. It is still a
  locally retargeted class-69 artifact, not a Java-21 build.
- **`measure.sh`** — pins JDK 21, `-Xmaxerrs 10000 --release 21 -encoding UTF-8`, an explicit `-d` (emptied
  before every run), and the four locale pins
  (`-J-Duser.language=en -J-Duser.country=US -J-Dstdout.encoding=UTF-8 -J-Dstderr.encoding=UTF-8`), because
  without them the transcript is GBK Chinese and the splitter has no anchors to find. It re-runs
  `extractTransferApis` with the line's own `-Pforbric.fabricApi` before **every** 1.21.1 measurement and then
  **asserts** the result: `build/transfer-api-compile/` is shared between lines, and a 26.2 build leaves
  `fabric-transfer-api-v1-8.0.11` where this line needs `-0.116.17`, which shows up as +146 errors. It also
  asserts the classpath is really 1.21.1 before compiling (`ResourceLocation` resolves, `Identifier` does not,
  `net.neoforged.neoforge.transfer.EnergyHandler` does not) — reconstructing a classpath that quietly holds the
  other line's jars is the mistake that made one round's 552 incomparable with this document's 288, so it is
  checked rather than assumed.
- **`axis-split.py`** — **rebuilt from the classification rules the port commits describe, not recovered.** Its
  header says so. Two things are therefore true at once and are stated together: the **TOTALS are exact** (error
  blocks counted out of javac's transcript, with javac's own `N errors` line printed beside them so the one-error
  `cannot access ParametersAreNonnullByDefault` gap stays visible), and the **per-axis columns are this rebuild's
  rules** and are not comparable with any column printed before the harness was lost.

The reconstruction had to solve a problem the old column values conceal: **javac prints the symbol as an
unqualified simple name.** `symbol: class FuelValues` says nothing about which line's API it belongs to; the
qualification is in `location: package ...`, or in the file's own imports, or written out in the source line.
A splitter that reads only the `symbol:` line attributes almost nothing. This one resolves the name through
(a) `location: package P`, (b) the file's import list, (c) qualified tokens in the block, and reads only the
MESSAGE and the OFFENDING SOURCE LINE as evidence — not the whole block, because javac's own continuation lines
(`where T is a type-variable: ...`) mention enough unrelated names to collapse the cascade bucket to nothing
(first attempt: 117/109/0/**8**). It also keeps the parameter list of `symbol: method f(T)`, which names a type
that the source line does not (`setScreen(screen)` spells the variable, not `Screen`).

## The new baseline, and the convention it is measured under

Reproduced on this tree, in this round, by `measure.sh` with `--energy j21` (the M2 convention: the retargeted
class-file-65 energy jar), 151 sources, 39 classpath entries:

| | TOTAL | axis 1 | axis 2 | axis 3 | cascades |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **BEFORE (the new baseline)** | **234** | 102 | 108 | **0** | 24 |
| AFTER (this stage) | **220** | 99 | 100 | **0** | 21 |

javac's own count is 235 and 221; the one-error gap is the `ParametersAreNonnullByDefault` diagnostic with no
`file:line`, exactly as earlier rounds recorded, and it is why blocks and diagnostics are both printed.

**Convention, named.** *M2 / JDK-21 / `--release 21` / locale-pinned / 151 sources / 39-entry 1.21.1 classpath /
`build/x11/axis-split.py` rules as committed with this round.* The 234 is **not** the 263 recorded before the
harness was lost, and it must not be read as a 29-error improvement: 263 was measured with a splitter whose
boundary this one does not reproduce, and the previous round separately measured a 29-error removal in a
reconstruction of its own. That the two agree at 234 is a coincidence worth exactly one sentence, and this is it:
**it is consistent with, and is not evidence for, the earlier 29.** The honest statement is that the per-axis
boundary moved when the harness was rebuilt, that 234 is the new baseline, and that the instrument for judging
this round is the set difference below, which does not depend on the boundary at all.

The rebuild's shape is recognisably the documented one: axis 3 is the control and reads 0, axis 1 is the
vanilla-name axis, axis 2 is the forge/neoforge surface, cascades are the residue that resolves to no package.
What differs from the old columns is where the axis-2/cascade line is drawn — this splitter buckets a block by
the family of the name it actually names, and does not count a `cannot find symbol` as a cascade merely because
an earlier error in the same file is upstream of it.

## Task B — three groups, all member-level, all javap-proven

The four large groups named as non-mechanical were left alone (see the refusals). The three taken were chosen
because each is **a member difference on a class that exists on BOTH lines**, which is exactly the class of
difference the third sweep exists to see and the one a class-name sweep reports as "nothing".

### G1. `RegistryAccess.lookup` -> `registry`, `lookupOrThrow` -> `registryOrThrow` (worth 8)

```
javap net.minecraft.core.RegistryAccess
  26.2    abstract <E> Optional<Registry<E>> lookup(ResourceKey<? extends Registry<? extends E>>)
          default  <E> Registry<E>         lookupOrThrow(ResourceKey...)
  1.21.1  abstract <E> Optional<Registry<E>> registry(ResourceKey...)
          default  <E> Registry<E>         registryOrThrow(ResourceKey...)
```

Same parameter, same return type, both lines — two renames. What makes this a *trap* rather than a rename in
passing: **1.21.1 also has a `lookup`**, a different method (`default <T> Optional<RegistryLookup<T>> lookup(...)`
from `HolderLookup.Provider`'s side). So the symbol RESOLVES and javac reports a type error rather than a missing
method — `no instance(s) of type variable(s) T exist so that Optional<RegistryLookup<T>> conforms to
Optional<Registry<BiomeModifier>>` — which reads like a generics problem and is a spelling. `lookupOrThrow` could
have been a table row (the token exists only as this method); `lookup` could not, because the token `lookup`
occurs 23+ times in `src/runtime/java` as a local variable name (`MethodHandles.Lookup lookup`,
`RegistryInfoLookup lookup`), and a whole-token rule would rewrite them. Both are therefore carried by the two
overlay files, and the table's member-level comment block records the pair for the next round.

### G2. `DataMapLoader`'s constructor and `apply()` (worth 3)

The member sweep found this one by diffing a class that exists on both lines, 26 members against 28:

```
26.2    public DataMapLoader()                    public void apply(RegistryAccess)
1.21.1  public DataMapLoader(RegistryAccess)      public void apply()
        public DataMapLoader(ICondition$IContext, RegistryAccess)
        private final RegistryAccess registryAccess   <- why: this line KEEPS the accessor it is given
```

`injectContext(context, access)` does not exist on 1.21.1 at all; the two-argument constructor is what 26.2's
constructor-plus-`injectContext` pair became. So the port constructs once with the live condition context and
applies with no argument. The reflection on the private `load(ResourceManager, ProfilerFiller)` overload and on
the `results` field is unchanged because both exist on 1.21.1 — checked, not assumed.

### G3. `ConfigTracker` / `ModList` are instance APIs on 1.21.1 (worth 3)

The same adjudication stage 2 made for `net.minecraftforge.fml.ModList` in `KernelForgeDeclarationReaders`, at
three sites that sweep did not reach, all re-measured on both carriers this round:

```
net.minecraftforge.fml.config.ConfigTracker
  26.2    public static Map<ModConfig$Type, Set<ModConfig>> configSets()
          public static void loadConfigs(ModConfig$Type, Path)
  1.21.1  public static final ConfigTracker INSTANCE
          public        Map<...> configSets();   public void loadConfigs(ModConfig$Type, Path)
net.minecraftforge.fml.ModList
  26.2    public static Optional<? extends ModContainer> getModContainerById(String)
  1.21.1  public static ModList get();   public Optional<? extends ModContainer> getModContainerById(String)
```

Note what makes this a port and not a coin flip: `net.neoforged.fml.config.ConfigTracker` — the *other* flavour —
is ALREADY an instance API with `INSTANCE` on 26.2 (36 members, identical on both lines). The kernel uses the
`net.minecraftforge` one here, so only that one changed shape. And the complaint is
`non-static method ... cannot be referenced from a static context`, not `cannot find symbol`: the member is there
and the CALL is wrong, which is why a table row cannot express it — what is missing is a receiver (`.INSTANCE`,
`.get()`), not a spelling.

## The overlay-steals-rules trap, hit a second time

Overlaying `KernelGameServerAboutToStart` orphaned exactly one table row,
`net.minecraftforge.event.server.ServerAboutToStartEvent.BUS`, whose only call site is in that file. This is the
mechanism defect stage 2 discovered, reproduced here rather than read about: overlays are written verbatim with
no rule applied, so a row that matches only inside one matches nothing, and "matched nothing" fails the build.
It was caught by `overlay-rules.py` **before** the build, the row is commented out in place with the reason
beside it (following the `LivingExperienceDropEvent.BUS` precedent), and the substituted name was confirmed
present in the overlay (`MinecraftForge.EVENT_BUS.post(`, line 112) rather than assumed. The overlay list goes
23 -> 28 files.

## The delta is a set difference of the two transcripts: 14 removed, 0 added

Keyed on `(file, message)` — line numbers excluded, because several files keep errors whose line moved:

```
removed   6  KernelForgeWorldgen.java       (4 x lookup, 2 x lookupOrThrow)
          5  KernelNeoWorldgen.java         (ctor, apply, 2 x lookupOrThrow, 1 inference from the ctor)
          1  KernelGameServerAboutToStart   (ConfigTracker.loadConfigs)
          1  KernelForgeConfigLoad          (ConfigTracker.configSets)
          1  KernelModConfigScreens         (ModList.getModContainerById)
added     0  anywhere
```

Every one of the 14 is inside a file this round overlays, and nothing was added. That is the whole case for
"the errors went away because the code was ported".

**Cross-axis movement, named rather than hidden**, because a fix that resolves one block resolves the others on
the same statement:

- **axis 1: 102 -> 99 (-3).** Two are `lookupOrThrow` on `KernelForgeWorldgen` and one is the `RegistryLookup`
  conformance on `KernelNeoWorldgen`. These were *consequences* on the vanilla side of the same fix — the
  boundary had put them here because the statement names `Registries`, and they left when the call was
  corrected. Not a reclassification: the splitter's rules did not change between the two runs.
- **axis 2: 108 -> 100 (-8).** The four `RegistryAccess.lookup` type errors on `KernelForgeWorldgen`, the
  `DataMapLoader` constructor, and the three `ConfigTracker`/`ModList` sites. This is the target group.
- **cascades: 24 -> 21 (-3).** `apply(RegistryAccess)`, the constructor inference and one symbol on
  `KernelNeoWorldgen` — downstream of G2 in the same method.
- **axis 3: 0 -> 0, byte-identical. The control holds**, which is the evidence that the splitter's boundary did
  not move between the two runs.

## What was refused, with this round's own evidence

Each of these was checked on both carriers this round rather than quoted from the earlier adjudication, and each
is a package that has classes on 26.2 and **zero** on 1.21.1:

| group | 26.2 classes in the package | 1.21.1 | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| FML transformation platform | `neoforgespi.transformation` 22, `fml.classloading.transformation` 16 | **0, 0** | refuse |
| Tooltip rework | `neoforge.common.tooltip` 5 | **0** | refuse |
| Picture-in-picture pipeline | `client.gui.render.pip` 8 | **0** | refuse |
| `JarContents` | `fml.jarcontents` 15 | **0** | refuse |
| Keyed reload-listener registry | `NeoForgeReloadListeners` exists, keyed on `ListenerKey<T>` | **class not found** | refuse |
| `InFluidPredicate` | `neoforge.fluids` has it, `test(E, FluidType, double)` | package exists (24 classes), **not in it**, no equivalent | refuse |
| `ForgeLayer` / `Hud` | `forge.client.gui.overlay` 4 | 2, and neither is `ForgeLayer`; `Hud` is 26.x vanilla | refuse |
| `UnbakedModelParser` | `neoforge.client.model` 19 | 31, none of them a parser; `client.resources.model.cuboid` absent | refuse |
| `RecipeContentPayload` | `neoforge.network.payload` | **0 candidates** | refuse |

Two refusals deserve their evidence written out, because each *looks* like a rename until the second javap:

- **`InputEvent$Key.getKeyEvent()`** exists on 26.2 only; 1.21.1's has `getKey()/getScanCode()/getAction()` and no
  `getKeyEvent()`, because 26.2 bundles them into `net.minecraft.client.input.KeyEvent` — a 26.x vanilla record
  that does not exist on this line (the transcript says `package net.minecraft.client.input does not exist`).
  The consumer differs too. Adding the accessor would mean inventing the type.
- **`IModFile`/`JarContents` (7).** The interface exists on both lines and its methods are **mutually exclusive**:
  26.2 declares `String getId()` / `JarContents getContents()`; 1.21.1 declares `SecureJar getSecureJar()` /
  `void setSecurityStatus(SecureJar$Status)`. `KernelModFile` implements a different contract on each line, and
  the 1.21.1 one returns `cpw.mods.jarhandling.SecureJar` where 26.2's returns a type from a 15-class package
  with zero classes on this line. That is not a rename and not a small restructure; it is a different contract.

Nothing was shimmed, stubbed, or deleted to move a count. No file was removed from the source set.

# Stage 6: axis 1 — 220 -> 200, and the trap is a field whose type name is the same on both lines

Axis 1 was the last axis with real mass that five stages of evidence had not already refused, so this stage went
there. The before-number was **measured fresh, not inherited**: `measure.sh` on this tree reproduced the stage-5
baseline exactly (220 / 99 / 100 / 0 / 21), which is the only reason the 200 below is a delta and not a coincidence.

## The method, and a bug in the tool that implements it

The sweep that works is the third one: for every `cannot find symbol` / `cannot be applied to given types`, take the
class javac had **already resolved** and diff its `javap -p` member listing on both carriers. A class-name sweep
cannot see a member rename, which is why two early rounds concluded "almost nothing here is a rename".

**The tool written for this stage had the same class of defect it was built to find.** `build/x11/mdiff.py` read only
`javap`'s **stdout**, and `javap` reports `class not found` on **stderr**. The effect is not an error: an ABSENT class
came back as `present: yes, members 0`, which reads as a real finding and makes a refused group look portable. It is
the same shape as the locale trap `measure.sh` pins against, and it is why
`net.minecraft.util.random.WeightedList` was first recorded here as "present on both lines with no members" when it is
absent on 1.21.1 entirely. Reading both streams fixes it, and that is what the tool does now.

## The six groups taken

Each is a member difference on a class that exists on **both** lines — the class of difference a class-name sweep
reports as "nothing".

### G1. `Minecraft`'s client-state accessors moved off `Gui` (9: 6 axis 1 + 3 cascades)

The largest and the most dangerous finding of the stage, because **neither the field name nor its declared type
changed**:

```
Minecraft.gui   declared type, 26.2    net.minecraft.client.gui.Gui   public final
Minecraft.gui   declared type, 1.21.1  net.minecraft.client.gui.Gui   public final
                          ^ same field name, same qualified type, DIFFERENT ROLE
26.2    Gui extends net.minecraftforge.client.gui.overlay.ForgeLayerInstance
        public final Hud hud;  public Screen screen();  public void setScreen(Screen);  public Overlay overlay();
1.21.1  Gui                                                    <- the HUD itself
        (hotbar/sprites/overlays; no screen(), no setScreen(), no overlay())
```

So on 26.2 `minecraft.gui` is the **screen manager**; on 1.21.1 it is the **HUD**, and the screen manager is the
client itself. A member-name diff of the *field* finds nothing, because the field is identical on both lines; only
reading what each line's `Gui` actually is finds it. The ported calls, each checked against 1.21.1 `Minecraft`:

```
26.2                                   1.21.1
Gui.screen()                           ->  Minecraft.screen          (public FIELD, javap -p)
Gui.setScreen(Screen)                  ->  Minecraft.setScreen(Screen)
Gui.overlay()                          ->  Minecraft.getOverlay()
Minecraft.disconnectWithSavingScreen() ->  Minecraft.disconnect()    (evidence below)
```

`disconnect()` is not a poorer substitute, and that was checked rather than assumed: `javap -c` on 1.21.1 shows it
building `new ProgressScreen(true)` and calling `disconnect(Screen, Z)` with that screen — a saving progress screen.
The name changed; the behaviour did not.

One judgement call, written down because it is a shape change rather than a rename: the 26.2 guard
`minecraft.gui == null` was **dropped**, not translated. There is no separate manager object on 1.21.1 that can be
absent, and `isRunning()` already covers the client not being up, so translating it would have left a check on a field
that means something else.

### G2. `ReloadableResourceManager.getListeners()` is gone; the field it returned is not (2)

```
26.2    public List<PreparableReloadListener> getListeners();   private        List<...> listeners;
1.21.1  ABSENT                                                 private final  List<...> listeners;
```

The accessor's own bytecode is `getfield listeners; areturn`, so reading the field is the same read without the
public name; 1.21.1 makes it `final`, which is why the helper only reads it. The helper throws rather than returning
an empty list, because an empty capture reads as "Forge registered no listeners" — a wrong answer rather than a
failure. `KernelGameClientReload` calls the same helper on `KernelForgeClientInit`, so there is one implementation
rather than two that can drift; both files are this line's own.

### G3. `WorldVersion.packVersion` -> `getPackVersion`, and `PackFormat` is an `int` here (5)

```
26.2    PackFormat packVersion(PackType)     PackMetadataSection(Component, InclusiveRange<PackFormat>)
                                             Pack.readPackMetadata(location, resources, PackFormat, PackType)
1.21.1  int        getPackVersion(PackType)  PackMetadataSection(Component, int)
                                             Pack.readPackMetadata(location, resources, int)
```

This is what the table's tail called "a real branch, not a substitution", written as the branch. `PackFormat` has no
counterpart on 1.21.1 at all; the same number is simply not wrapped in a range, and the `PackType` is not passed to
`readPackMetadata`. `Pack.Metadata`'s member set is **identical** on both lines (15 vs 15), so the rest of the method
needed no change — checked with `javap -p`, not assumed.

### G4. `Minecraft.disconnectWithSavingScreen()` -> `disconnect()` at the soak harness (2)

Same evidence as G1. `ClientSoakController`'s third axis-1 error is left failing **on purpose**: 26.2's `teleportTo`
takes an eighth `boolean` that `javap -c` shows is only `if (flag) setCamera(this)` before delegating to the
7-argument `Player.teleportTo`, and 1.21.1 has the 7-argument overload and no camera flag. Dropping the argument would
compile and would quietly skip the camera retarget, so it stays an error, with the reason in place, rather than a
silent behaviour change in a test harness.

### G5. `Minecraft.getDeltaTracker()` -> `getTimer()` — a table row (1)

```
26.2    public DeltaTracker getDeltaTracker();   and NO getTimer()
1.21.1  public DeltaTracker getTimer();          and NO getDeltaTracker()
```

The same return type name on both lines, and both of those interfaces declare `getRealtimeDeltaTicks()`, which is the
only member called. One call site, so a rule is the whole fix and no overlay is needed.

### G6. `Registry.getValue` -> `get` — a row that has to be written QUALIFIED (1)

The trap inside this one is that 26.2 shifted **both** names down one:

```
26.2    T getValue(ResourceKey<T>)   T getValue(Identifier)   Optional<Holder$Reference<T>> get(Identifier)
1.21.1  T get(ResourceKey)           T get(ResourceLocation)  Optional<Holder$Reference<T>> getHolder(...)
```

so `getValue` is the old `get`, and `get` is the old `getHolder`. A bare `getValue -> get` row would be wrong: the
token `getValue` occurs **49 times** in `src/runtime/java` and nearly all of them are `Map.Entry#getValue()`
(`KernelForgeOptions`, `KernelForgeSpawnPlacements`, `KernelCreativeSearch`'s `tab.getValue()`,
`IdentityValueBiMap`'s own override). The row is therefore written at the one qualified site that is a `Registry` —
`BuiltInRegistries.PARTICLE_TYPE.getValue` — which changes the member name and leaves the receiver expression alone.
The `.BUS` rows already in the table are the precedent for a qualified row.

## The overlay-steals-rules trap, hit a THIRD time — and the pre-build check is why it was not a build failure

Overlaying `KernelForgeClientInit` orphaned `net.minecraft.client.particle.ParticleResources`, whose only site was
that file's import. `overlay-rules.py` caught it **before** the build (`ORPHANED BY THIS OVERLAY SET: 1`), the row is
retired in place with the reason beside it, and the BARE `ParticleResources` row was kept **deliberately**: its
remaining site is a `{@code ParticleResources}` javadoc mention in `KernelParticleProviders`, which the normaliser
rewrites like any other text, so the ported line's documentation keeps this line's class name. Overlay list
28 -> 33 files; rules 46 -> 47 (two added, one retired).

| AFTER (this stage) | **200** | 82 | 100 | **0** | 18 |

`07443f72398c692a052fe81970e3cbbe`, after `cb05c258b36757f57106e3149ca026f0`.

Keyed on `(file, message, symbol)`; line numbers excluded because several files keep errors whose line moved.

```
removed   3  KernelClientPackSource.java      class PackFormat
          2  KernelClientPackSource.java      method packVersion(PackType)
          2  ClientSoakController.java        method disconnectWithSavingScreen()
```

Every one of the 20 is inside a file this stage touched — five overlays and two table rows, seven files. Nothing was
reclassified, shimmed or deleted.

**Cross-axis movement, named rather than hidden.** `axis 1` falls 99 -> 82 (-17) and `cascades` fall 21 -> 18 (-3).
The three cascades are `gui.overlay()`, `gui.screen()` and `gui.screen()` **in `KernelCompatibilityPrompts`** — the
same statements as G1's axis-1 blocks, and they sit in the cascade bucket for a splitter-boundary reason worth
recording: `Gui` is not imported in that file, so `location: variable gui of type Gui` resolves through no import to
no package and the block cannot be attributed to a line. They left when the call was corrected, which is a

# Stage 8: the energy jar becomes a per-line artifact the build produces (225 -> 200), and one subsystem gets a 1.21.1 edition (200 -> 195)

Stage 7 (commit `1299f5b`) attempted the first Gradle build and found the answer the count could not give: after two
wiring fixes, the 1.21.1 `jar` invocation fails at **exactly one task, `:compileRuntimeJava`**. Its record lives in
that commit message and in `forbric-kernel/build/x11/PORT-1.21.1-STAGE7.md` — a **gitignored** file, which is the same
hazard this document keeps recording, so the durable parts of it are restated here. Its map of what remained:
(ii) one wiring defect worth **+25**, (i) ~47 member/shape problems, (i) ~10 subsystem **editions**, (iii) an
unaudited boot half. This stage takes (ii) — the only minute-level item on the list — and then exactly one of the ten
editions.

## Task A — the energy jar: the DANGER's generalisation, which is NOT "one filename, two contents"

`.dev/api/energy-5.0.0.jar` holds 12 class files at version **69** (Java 25, Team Reborn's own release). The 1.21.1
line is built by a **JDK 21**, and that javac refuses a class file newer than the version it targets:

```
error: cannot access EnergyStorage
  bad class file: .dev/api/energy-5.0.0.jar(team/reborn/energy/api/EnergyStorage.class)
    class file has wrong version 69.0, should be 65.0
```

after which every `team.reborn.energy` reference fails as `cannot find symbol`: **25 errors**, all on axis 3.

**The shape is not the merged base's shape, and the difference is the useful finding.** The merged base's defect is
one unversioned *filename* holding two lines' *different bytes* — whichever line wrote last wins. Here one file holds
one line's bytes and **two readers disagree about whether they may read it**. Nothing was overwritten; the artifact is
correct for both lines; only one line's *compiler* can read it. Measured, not assumed: the same jar under a **JDK-25**
javac with the same `--release 21` is read and the compile proceeds to unrelated errors, while a **JDK-21** javac
refuses it. So the refusal is a property of the **compile JDK**, not of the release level and not of the line's name.

The generalisation, stated so the next shared input does not have to rediscover it:

> A shared, unversioned artifact is safe only if it satisfies the **weakest reader**. Where the lines differ in what
> their toolchain can read — which is exactly what "a line is built by a different JDK" means — the artifact is not
> shared: it is one line's artifact being borrowed by the other, and the borrow must be made explicit (a per-line
> edition) or the build must fail.

Applied to the three shared classpath inputs, the three cases are different and only one of them is a filename
problem:

| shared input | how the lines differ | how it is made per-line |
|---|---|---|
| merged base | different **bytes** per line | line-keyed filename, `patched-mc-merged-${mcLine}.jar` (stage 7) |
| fabric-api | different **release** per line | `fabric-api-*+${mcLine}.jar` glob (stage 7) |
| Team Reborn Energy | **same bytes**, different **reader capability** | cannot be line-keyed by name — it is a third-party mod's release — so the line's edition is **derived** by the build |

The corollary is the part worth keeping: **a filename is not the only way a line can be invisible.** The energy jar's
name carries no `+26.2` and never will, so no filename audit would ever have found it; it was found by *running the
build*. The detection question is "what can this line's compiler read", not "what does this filename say".

### What was wired

- `retargetRebornEnergy` (new Gradle task, `build.gradle`): writes `build/energy-api/<line>/energy-5.0.0-j21.jar` from
  whatever jar is resolved (property, or the dev default). **Only** the `u2` major-version field of the class entries
  that need lowering is rewritten; entry list, entry order and per-entry compression are preserved; non-class entries
  are copied untouched; a class file with preview features (`minor == 0xFFFF`) is **refused**, because a version stamp
  cannot lower preview features and the alternative is a `VerifyError` at load time. The claim is then re-proved from
  the two files, entry by entry, the same way the normaliser and remapper are held to it.
- The target version is **derived**, not written down: `44 + java.targetCompatibility` = 65 for this line, so the
  artifact cannot drift from the compiler's own `--release`.
- The task is registered **only when `mcLine != mcLineDefault`**. On 26.2 it does not exist (measured: Gradle answers
  `Task 'retargetRebornEnergy' not found`, and it appears **0** times in `gradlew tasks --all`).
- Both consumers now read **the same file by construction**: `sourceSets.runtime.compileClasspath`/`runtimeClasspath`
  use `energyForLine`, and `verifyRebornEnergy` runs `ApiJarContract` on `energyForLine` too — so "the bytes javac
  reads" and "the bytes the ABI contract verified" cannot diverge.

### The artifact itself, measured three ways

```
[forbric] energy 1.21.1: energy-5.0.0.jar (class 69) -> energy-5.0.0-j21.jar (class 65);
          12 class file(s) retargeted (12 byte(s) moved, all inside the u2 major-version field),
          0 already readable, 13 non-class entries copied untouched
```

1. **Byte level, independent of the writer** (python, re-reading both zips): entry lists identical (25 entries),
   12 entries differ, **12 differing bytes in total**, every one at **offset 7** — the low byte of the major-version
   field. 69 -> 65 is `0x0045 -> 0x0041`, so the honest count is *one byte per class*, not two; a verifier asserting
   "exactly 2 bytes differ" would reject a correct output. Output versions: `{65: 12}`.
2. **Against the harness artifact every earlier round measured with**: the build-produced jar is **entry-for-entry
   identical** to `build/x11/energy-5.0.0-j21.jar` (container md5 differs only because zip container metadata is not
   part of the data entries).
3. **Against the repository's own ABI check, under JDK 21** — the JDK that cannot read the input at all:
   `API binary contract verified: …\build\energy-api\1.21.1\energy-5.0.0-j21.jar`.

### The count movement, and the convention each number is under

`measure.sh` gained a third convention, `--energy build`, which passes **no** `-Pforbric.rebornEnergy` at all so the
build has to produce the artifact itself.

| convention | TOTAL | axis 1 | axis 2 | axis 3 | cascades | transcript md5 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **M2 before** (`--energy j21`, hand-made artifact) | 200 | 82 | 100 | 0 | 18 | `cb05c258b36757f57106e3149ca026f0` |
| **M1, reproduced this round** (class-69 shipped jar substituted into the dumped classpath) | **225** | 82 | 100 | **25** | 18 | `7bf3fabde9875cfa2390002690a303bf` |
| **M2 after** (build's own artifact, no property) | **200** | 82 | 100 | **0** | 18 | **`cb05c258b36757f57106e3149ca026f0`** |

The third row's md5 is **identical** to the first: supplying the build's artifact instead of the hand-made one changes
nothing about the errors, which is what makes the +25 the entire effect of the defect. M1 is no longer reproducible
through a build mode at all — the build retargets whatever it is handed — so it is reproduced by substituting the
shipped jar into the dumped classpath, which is exactly what M1 always was.

**26.2 uses the shipped jar unchanged, and this was verified rather than assumed:** with no properties,
`verifyRebornEnergy` prints `API binary contract verified: …\forbric-kernel\.dev\api\energy-5.0.0.jar` — the shipped
path, not a build artifact; the task is absent from the task list; and the task cannot write it, because its single
declared output is `build/energy-api/<line>/…`, with the input opened read-only (the shipped jar's md5 is
`5e82216d13b7ea90ce0d35ff26b6c8b0`).

## Task B — one subsystem: the client reload-listener bridge, 200 -> 195

Of the ten subsystems stage 7 named, nine are already refused with class-absence evidence in the table above and in
`.tsv`'s own refusal blocks. The smallest coherent one that is **not** a class-absence case is the reload-listener
bridge, and within it the **client half** is the only part that is both self-contained on this line and actually
installed on it: `GameEventMultiplexer.installClientReloadBridge` reaches the class by **reflection on
`install(Object)`**, so an edition that keeps that signature needs **no boot-half change**.

What an edition means here, measured on the line's own carriers (javap, both lines):

| fact | 26.2 | 1.21.1 |
|---|---|---|
| NeoForge's client event | `AddClientReloadListenersEvent(ReloadableResourceManager)`, extends `SortedReloadListenerEvent` — **keyed and ordered** | `RegisterClientReloadListenersEvent(ReloadableResourceManager)`, extends `Event implements IModBusEvent`; `registerReloadListener(PreparableReloadListener)` — **no key, no ordering** |
| MinecraftForge's client event | `final class … extends MutableEvent` with `public static final EventBus<…> BUS` | plain `IModBusEvent`, **no `BUS` field** |
| the two event hierarchies | one EventBus 7 | **separate**: `net.neoforged.bus.EventBus implements net.neoforged.bus.api.IEventBus` only; `net.minecraftforge.eventbus.api.IEventBus.post` is the only `post` that accepts a Forge `Event` |
| posting a Forge mod-bus event globally | the event's own `BUS` | the Forge loader's `ModLoader.get().postEvent(Event & IModBusEvent)` |

The edition, therefore, is three differences rather than a rename:

1. **The event name.** NeoForge renamed `Add…` to `Register…`, and this line's MinecraftForge event has the *same
   simple name*, so only one can be imported; NeoForge's is, and MinecraftForge's is written in full at its one use.
2. **The listener id is absent rather than skipped.** The 26.2 bridge synthesises an `Identifier` per listener because
   the 26.2 graph sorts by id. This line has no id, no key and no graph, so `register()` calls
   `registerReloadListener(listener)` and the derivation is *gone* — an id nobody reads is not a port of the code that
   needed one.
3. **The fallback posts through the loader, not a bus.** The first cut of this overlay posted the Forge event on the
   `net.neoforged.bus.api.IEventBus` it is handed, and javac refused it: `no suitable method found for post(…)`. That
   refusal *is* the finding — the two event hierarchies are separate types on this line — and the corrected call is the
   Forge loader's own global mod-bus dispatch.

**Cross-axis movement, named:** axis 2 falls 100 -> 95 (-5), axis 1 stays 82, **axis 3 stays 0 (the control)**,
cascades stay 18. The intermediate state is recorded because it shaped the work: the first cut measured **196**
(axis 2 -4) with one *new* error replacing the one it removed, at the `post(…)` line — which is why the fallback was
re-designed rather than cast around.

### The delta is a set difference of the two transcripts: 5 removed, 0 added

```
removed   5  KernelGameClientReload.java   cannot find symbol
             L32  import net.neoforged.neoforge.client.event.AddClientReloadListenersEvent
             L91  register(AddClientReloadListenersEvent event, …)
             L63  Consumer<AddClientReloadListenersEvent> bridge
             L78  RegisterClientReloadListenersEvent.BUS          <- the 26.2 per-event bus field
             L88  addListener(…, AddClientReloadListenersEvent.class, bridge)
added     0  anywhere
```

All five are inside the one file this stage overlaid, and that file now reports **0** errors.

### What was refused, with this round's own evidence

The rest of the cluster, measured this round rather than quoted:

| group | errs | evidence on 1.21.1 | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| `KernelForgeReload` (the server twin) | 3 | hook is `EventHooks.onResourceReload(ReloadableServerResources, RegistryAccess)` — 2 args, no `Map<ListenerKey<?>,…>`, and `ListenerKey` is **ABSENT**. The **installer** is descriptor-bound: `ForbricMergedBaseCompatTransformer.postMinecraftForgesReloadListenerEvent` matches a call site whose descriptor equals `RELOAD_HOOK_DESC` (the 3-arg 26.2 hook), so on this line it finds **0** call sites and stands down. An edition would compile and never be installed; making it installable is a `src/main` change, which would change 26.2's jar bytes | refuse — named as the next stage's first item |
| `KernelClientReloadNames` | 2 | `VanillaClientListeners` **ABSENT** (26.2: 22 `Identifier` fields + `getNameForClass`); its only call site is the boot-side redirect of `AddClientReloadListenersEvent.lookupName`, and that class is **ABSENT** here too — no table *and* no site | refuse |
| `KernelServerReloadNames` | 2 | `VanillaServerListeners` **ABSENT**; `ServerReloadListenerNamesInjector` targets `AddServerReloadListenersEvent`, **ABSENT** here — no table and no site | refuse |

Nothing was shimmed, stubbed or deleted to move a count, and no file left the source set.

## A measurement trap found in this stage's own work

**Gradle's own javac diagnostics are not locale-pinned.** The 1.21.1 build log says `错误: 程序包net.neoforged…
不存在` and `100 个错误` — so `grep 'error:'` finds nothing and, worse, **`grep 'wrong version'` on that log is a false
negative**: it would have "shown" the class-69 defect is gone when the message could never have appeared in English.
The evidence actually used for that claim: `verifyRebornEnergy` **passed under JDK 21** against the build-produced
class-65 artifact in the same invocation (impossible if javac were reading class 69), and the log contains **zero**
mentions of `69.0`, `65.0`, `错误的类文件` or `reborn`. `measure.sh`'s locale pins exist for exactly this reason and do not
cover this path; the hand-javac measurement remains the authoritative number.

`JAVA_HOME=C:\Program Files\Java\jdk-25.0.2` (and jdk-21 where a check is about the other line),
`GRADLE_USER_HOME=<repo>/.gradle-home`, no `-P` property except the ones named. `build/x11` was copied **outside the
repo** before the mandated `clean`, which is the fifth time that has been necessary.

1. **Clean build.** `clean jar` -> `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 32s`, **10 actionable tasks, 10 executed**, and `grep -c` for
   `normalizeRuntimeJava`/`remapTransferApis`/`retargetRebornEnergy` -> **0**. Both jars produced. **Strong form:** a
   build from `git stash`ed HEAD produced both jars **entry-and-CRC identical and file-md5 identical** — boot
   `709f455ab3accdb60d720891ae7b5b5b` (2,623 entries), runtime `7d2c9f4d093a4307a5c03281b8d96b0d` (324 entries) on

3. **The line switch is not sticky.** A 1.21.1 `compileRuntimeJava` logged `33 file(s) REPLACED` with **no "rule(s)
   matched nothing"**, ran `:retargetRebornEnergy` and `:verifyRebornEnergy`, and **`:compileRuntimeJava FAILED`** as

4. **The suite**, counted from `build/test-results/test/*.xml` (906 suites) rather than Gradle's console row, which
   said `5629 tests completed, 79 failed, 836 skipped` and is **wrong**: **5,635 tests, 79 failures, 0 errors, 842
   skipped** — identical to the baseline. The 79 decompose exactly as before: **77 `java.io.IOException` at

   mandated `clean` deletes it; **without that restore this tree skips 850**, which is stage 7's defect and the 8-test
   difference is exactly `ForgeCapabilityComposition1211Test` (it runs 8/0/0 instead of skipping). **The defect is

## What is still not verified, stated plainly

- **The 1.21.1 line still does not compile** (195 errors by the M2 convention). **No 1.21.1 kernel jar exists and no
  launch has been attempted.** Nothing after `:compileRuntimeJava` has ever run on this line.
- **Not one line of any overlay has ever executed**, across eight stages. The reload-bridge edition is verified by
  `javap` on both carriers and by compilation against the line's own class path; that is a proof about bytes.
- **The committed runtime test for this class proves the 26.2 bridge, not the edition.** `KernelForgeClientInitRuntimeTest`
  asserts `AddClientReloadListenersEvent`, the id-keyed `graph:add` and the per-event `BUS` — all 26.2 shapes, compiled
  from the shared source. The edition has **no behavioural test**; writing one needs a 1.21.1-shaped spy fixture, which
  is a stage of its own.
- **Three claims in the edition are reasoned, not observed**: that the NeoForge mod bus is what
  `GameEventMultiplexer` passes as `modBus`; that `ModLoader.get().postEvent(…)` reaches Forge listeners on the merged
  base; and that the merged base leaves the Forge loader with any mods registered there at all. The last one is a
  property of the merge, not of this method.
- **Two boot-half wiring facts are now known and unfixed**, both category (iii): the server-reload redirect is
  descriptor-bound to 26.2's 3-arg hook, and `ServerReloadListenerNamesInjector` declares a **REQUIRED** anchor on
  `AddServerReloadListenersEvent`, a class that does not exist on this line. Neither is fixable without changing
  `src/main`, i.e. 26.2's jar bytes.
- The energy artifact is a **class-version stamp, not a Java-21 build** of the library: the same bytes under a lower
  version. `verifyRebornEnergy` proves the public ABI, not the behaviour, and no test loads those 12 classes.
- The remapped fabric modules remain compile-time only, and the capability certificate is still proved by calling
  `RequiredAncestorCompositions.verify` directly rather than through `ForbricClassLoader.define`.

# Stage 9: the data-map watch — 195 → 190, and reachability chose the subsystem, not size

Stage 8 gave one subsystem a 1.21.1 edition and refused three others **because their installer was
descriptor-bound to a 26.2 hook**, so an edition would compile and be installed nowhere. This stage takes one more
edition out of the same cluster, and the property that selected it is exactly that one: **the 1.21.1 line reaches
it**. It is also the smallest candidate left (5 errors of 195), which is the second reason — a subsystem whose
reachability is measured and whose counterpart is measured is worth more than three whose reachability is a guess.

| | |
|---|---|
| subsystem | NeoForge's **own data-map reload path**, watched so `KernelNeoWorldgen`'s about-to-start fallback can stand down |
| file | `KernelNeoDataMapWatch.java`, overlay #34 |
| reachable on 1.21.1? | **yes, twice** — a boot-side reflection entry with a line-neutral signature, and a call site inside the line's own overlay (both below) |
| what an edition means | three differences in *where NeoForge posts the two signals*; no rename, and no `ListenerKey`/`Map` anywhere in it |
| errors | **5 occurrences, 1 file**, all axis 2, all `cannot find symbol` |

## Why this class and not its server twin, measured

Stage 8 refused `KernelForgeReload` because `ForbricMergedBaseCompatTransformer.postMinecraftForgesReloadListenerEvent`
matches a call site **whose descriptor equals** the 26.2 hook's. That is the failure shape to avoid, and it is
avoided here by construction, not by preference:

```
GameEventMultiplexer.installDataMapWatch(ClassLoader)                    boot half, src/main
  355  Class.forName("net.forbric.kernel.runtime.KernelNeoDataMapWatch", true, cl)
       .getMethod("installDataMapWatch", Object.class).invoke(null, neoBus)
KernelLifecycle.startGameBuses(ClassLoader, Side)                        caller, unconditional
  594  GameEventMultiplexer.installDataMapWatch(cl);            (both sides, before the bus start)
KernelRuntimeClasses                                                     boot seam registry
  462  new Entry(Origin.COMPILED, List.of(new Call("installDataMapWatch", void.class, Object.class)))
```

Every parameter is `Object`, so there is no descriptor a line could fail to match; the edition's whole install
contract is *keeping that signature*, which it does. The **consumer** is on this line too, inside the line's own
overlay — `KernelNeoWorldgen.beforeServerStart` reads `KernelNeoDataMapWatch.appliedTotal()` before it may load
anything, and `src/runtime/mc-line/1.21.1/java/…/KernelNeoWorldgen.java` carries that call. So on this line
before this stage there was a **consumer with no producer**: the fallback could never stand down, because the
class that counts what NeoForge applied did not compile.

The rest of the cluster stays refused, re-measured this round rather than quoted:

```
26.2    EventHooks.onResourceReload(ReloadableServerResources, RegistryAccess,
                                    Map<ListenerKey<?>, PreparableReloadListener>)
1.21.1  EventHooks.onResourceReload(ReloadableServerResources, RegistryAccess)   -> List<PreparableReloadListener>
1.21.1  net/neoforged/neoforge/resource/ holds 5 classes: ContextAwareReloadListener, EmptyPackResources(+1),
        ResourcePackLoader, package-info — no ListenerKey, no NeoForgeReloadListeners, no VanillaServerListeners
```

## What an edition means here, from `javap` on both carriers

| | 26.2 (the shared file) | 1.21.1 (this overlay) |
|---|---|---|
| registration signal | `AddServerReloadListenersEvent extends SortedReloadListenerEvent`, with `getServerResources()` and `addRetainedListener(ListenerKey,T)` | `AddReloadListenerEvent extends net.neoforged.bus.api.Event`: `addListener(PreparableReloadListener)`, `getListeners()`, `getServerResources()`, `getConditionContext()`, `getRegistryAccess()` |
| "is the loader registered" | `event.getServerResources().getListener(NeoForgeReloadListeners.DATA_MAPS_KEY)` | `NeoForgeEventHandler.DATA_MAPS`, by identity against the previous reload |
| the data-load signal | `TagsUpdatedEvent.ServerDataLoad`, a nested `final` subclass carrying `getServerResources()` | `TagsUpdatedEvent`, filtered on `getUpdateCause() == UpdateCause.SERVER_DATA_LOAD` |

Two things are **identical on both lines** and are why the other two listeners needed no edition at all:

```
TagsUpdatedEvent$UpdateCause            SERVER_DATA_LOAD, CLIENT_PACKET_RECEIVED      both jars
DataMapsUpdatedEvent$UpdateCause        CLIENT_SYNC,     SERVER_RELOAD               both jars
net.neoforged.bus.api.IEventBus         addListener(EventPriority, boolean, Class<T>, Consumer<T>)   both
```

The 4-argument overload is the one 26.2's file uses; it exists here unaltered, so the "LOWEST and not
`receiveCancelled`" wiring is the same code on both lines — and LOWEST is load-bearing for a measured reason:
`NeoForgeEventHandler.onResourceReload` and `tagsUpdated` both carry `@SubscribeEvent` **with no declared
priority** (`javap -v`), i.e. NORMAL, so LOWEST always runs after them.

## The registration probe: identity, because there is no key

26.2 asks the resource manager for a listener under a key. There is no key on this line, so the faithful question
changes from *"is something registered under that name"* to *"did NeoForge register a loader for THIS reload"* —
and `NeoForgeEventHandler` answers it in its own bytecode:

```
onResourceReload(AddReloadListenerEvent):
  event.addListener(new LootModifierManager())
  new DataMapLoader ; event.getRegistryAccess() ; <init>(RegistryAccess)
  dup ; putstatic DATA_MAPS ; event.addListener(...)          <- a NEW loader per reload
tagsUpdated(TagsUpdatedEvent):
  if (getUpdateCause() == SERVER_DATA_LOAD) DATA_MAPS.apply()
```

A changed identity is therefore the same statement as 26.2's keyed lookup, and an unchanged one means the handler
did not run for this reload. Two properties are stated in the code rather than glossed:

- `DATA_MAPS` is **private** (`private static DataMapLoader DATA_MAPS`), so this is reflection on a carrier
  implementation detail — the same class of dependency as `ForgeLayeredDraw.namedLayers` in
  `KernelForgeOverlayLayers`. If the field cannot be read, the edition **says so** (`UNREADABLE`) instead of
  claiming the loader is missing; a probe that cannot answer is not an answer.
- It is **stronger** than the 26.2 probe in one respect worth naming: `DATA_MAPS` is the exact object this line's
  `tagsUpdated` applies, so "registered" and "will be applied" are the same object here, where 26.2's key only
  promised a listener under a name.

## The two ends are posted on this line, measured rather than assumed

A watch over a path is worth nothing if the path is not driven here. Both ends are, and the merged base says so:

```
EventHooks.onResourceReload(ReloadableServerResources, RegistryAccess)        javap -c, 1.21.1
  new AddReloadListenerEvent ; NeoForge.EVENT_BUS.post ; return event.getListeners()
DataMapLoader.apply()                                                          javap -c, 1.21.1
  ... NeoForge.EVENT_BUS.post(new DataMapsUpdatedEvent(access, registry, UpdateCause.SERVER_RELOAD))
```

constant-pool scan of the two merged bases, for the classes that name each event:

```
                                  AddReloadListenerEvent   DataMapsUpdatedEvent
1.21.1 patched-mc-merged           4 classes (EventHooks…)   7 classes (DataMapLoader…)
26.2   patched-mc-merged           0                        0        (posts the 26.2 pair instead)
```

`EventHooks` posting it is also what makes the event reachable from the bus the kernel is handed:
`installDataMapWatch(Object)` receives `NeoForge.EVENT_BUS` from `GameEventMultiplexer`.
