## The committed test pins the 26.2 shapes, so the edition has none

`KernelNeoWorldgenTest.theWatchRegistersThreeLowestPriorityListenersThroughTheFourArgumentOverload` drives
`installDataMapWatch(Object)` through a recording `IEventBus` proxy and asserts, verbatim:

```java
assertTrue(calls.get(0).contains("net.neoforged.neoforge.event.AddServerReloadListenersEvent"));
assertTrue(calls.get(2).contains("net.neoforged.neoforge.event.TagsUpdatedEvent$ServerDataLoad"));
```

and it loads the class from `build/classes/java/runtime` — the **26.2** compilation of the shared source. So it
proves the shared file and **cannot be pointed at the edition**, whose third call is `TagsUpdatedEvent` with no
`$ServerDataLoad` and whose first is `AddReloadListenerEvent`. Both of the edition's other shapes are asserted
there too (`3` calls, each `4:LOWEST|false|`), and those two halves *do* hold on both lines. This is the same gap
stage 8 reported for the client reload bridge, and it is not fixed here: a behavioural test for the edition needs
a 1.21.1-shaped spy fixture, which is a stage of its own.

What the edition has instead: it **compiles against the real 1.21.1 carriers** (standalone, the stage-4 method),
and the compiled bytecode names this line's types:

```
javac --release 21 -cp <the 39-entry 1.21.1 classpath>  …/KernelNeoDataMapWatch.java  ->  1 class file, 0 errors
javap -p -c  (bytecode offsets, installDataMapWatch unless stated)
  10  ldc            // class net/neoforged/neoforge/event/AddReloadListenerEvent
  17  invokeinterface IEventBus.addListener:(EventPriority;ZLjava/lang/Class;Ljava/util/function/Consumer;)V
  27  ldc            // class net/neoforged/neoforge/registries/datamaps/DataMapsUpdatedEvent
  34  invokeinterface (same descriptor)
  44  ldc            // class net/neoforged/neoforge/event/TagsUpdatedEvent
  51  invokeinterface (same descriptor)
  lambda$installDataMapWatch$2 (TagsUpdatedEvent):
   1  invokevirtual  TagsUpdatedEvent.getUpdateCause:()…$UpdateCause;
   4  getstatic      …$UpdateCause.SERVER_DATA_LOAD:…$UpdateCause;
   7  if_acmpeq 11   (the cause filter; NeoForge's own handler spells the same test if_acmpne)
  dataMapsField:
   2  ldc            // String DATA_MAPS
```

(The listing is `build/x11/datamap-javap.txt`, produced by hand-javac against the line's own class path; the class
files it reads are `build/x11/classes-datamap/`.)

## What was refused this round, with this round's own measurements

Each re-measured on the 1.21.1 classpath in this round, not inherited:

| group | errs | evidence on 1.21.1 | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| `KernelForgeReload` (server reload twin) | 3 | `EventHooks.onResourceReload` is **2-arg** here (`(ReloadableServerResources, RegistryAccess)`) against 26.2's 3-arg; `ListenerKey` absent (the whole `neoforge/resource` package is 5 classes and none of them is it). Its **installer is descriptor-bound** to the 3-arg hook, so an edition would compile and never install | refuse, unchanged from stage 8 |
| `KernelNeoTooltips` | 20 | **the biggest boot-reachable candidate**, and it *is* reached (`init()` is in `KernelRuntimeClasses` and called unconditionally by `KernelLifecycle`) — refused because there is nothing to build: `neoforge/common/tooltip` **0 classes**, no `TooltipAppender`/`ItemTooltipHandler`/`RegisterTooltipAppendersEvent`, no `TooltipDisplay`. The 52 tooltip-named classes on the classpath are vanilla's `RenderTooltipEvent`/`ItemTooltipEvent`/`TooltipFlagExtension` family. An "edition" would remove 20 errors by removing the appender pipeline, not by giving it a 1.21.1 form | refuse |
| pip renderers | 31 | `client/gui/render/pip` **0**, `client/renderer/state/gui/pip` **0** | refuse |
| FML transformation view | 23 | `fml/classloading/transformation` **0**, `neoforgespi/transformation` **0**; its only consumer is a guest mod's cast this repository cannot see | refuse |
| `IModFile`/`JarContents` | 9 | `fml/jarcontents` **0**, and the interface's members are mutually exclusive | refuse |

Nothing was shimmed, stubbed or deleted to move a count, and no file left the source set.

## 26.2 still builds and its tests do not regress: four checks, reproduced

`JAVA_HOME=C:\Program Files\Java\jdk-25.0.2` (and jdk-21 where a check is about the other line),
`GRADLE_USER_HOME=<repo>/.gradle-home`, no `-P` property except the ones named. `build/x11` was copied **outside
the repository** before the mandated `clean` (the sixth time that has been necessary), including
`classes1211-runtime`.

1. **Clean build.** `clean jar` → `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 53s`, **10 actionable tasks, 10 executed**; `grep -c` for
   `normalizeRuntimeJava`/`remapTransferApis`/`retargetRebornEnergy` → **0**; the only two warnings name
   `src\runtime\java`. **Strong form:** a build from `git stash`ed HEAD (the overlay stashed with `-u`) produced
   both jars **entry-and-CRC identical** — the CRC listings `diff` to **0 lines** on both, and the file md5s are
   equal on both sides: boot `709f455ab3accdb60d720891ae7b5b5b` (2,623 entries), runtime
   `7d2c9f4d093a4307a5c03281b8d96b0d` (324 entries). This commit provably does not change 26.2 by a byte.
2. **Fresh runtime compile.** `rm -rf build/classes/java/runtime` then `compileRuntimeJava` → `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in
   22s`, **295 class files**; `--dry-run` lists exactly `:compileJava :processResources :classes
   :extractTransferApis :verifyRebornEnergy :compileRuntimeJava` — unchanged.
3. **The line switch is not sticky.** A 1.21.1 `compileRuntimeJava` (JDK 21) logged **`34 file(s) REPLACED`** —
   the 33 of stage 8 plus this round's one — with **no "rule(s) matched nothing"**, ran `:remapTransferApis`,
   `:retargetRebornEnergy`, `:verifyRebornEnergy`, then **`:compileRuntimeJava FAILED`** as expected. Immediately
   after, `rm -rf build/classes/java/runtime` and a plain `compileRuntimeJava` → `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 28s`,
   **295 class files**, zero line-task mentions.
4. **The suite**, counted from `build/test-results/test/*.xml` (906 suites) rather than Gradle's console row, which
   said `5629 tests, 836 skipped, 79 failed` and is **wrong**: **5,635 tests, 79 failures, 0 errors, 842 skipped** —
   identical to the baseline. The 79 decompose exactly as before: **77 `java.io.IOException` at
   `TempDirectory.java:350`** plus exactly two `AssertionFailedError`, at
   `ForgeRegistrationA2ContractTest.clientGateSeedsF6AndBothScriptsParse` and
   `KernelRuntimeClassesTest.everyGameSideClassTheBootSideNamesIsInTheRegistry`. **Neither was touched.**
   `build/x11` — including `classes1211-runtime` — was restored before the run, because the mandated `clean`
   deletes it; **without that restore this tree skips 850**, which is the known defect recorded twice and still
   not fixed.

## What is still not verified, stated plainly

- **The 1.21.1 line still does not compile** (190 errors by the M2 convention). **No 1.21.1 kernel jar exists and
  no launch has been attempted.** A lower count is not a build.
- **Not one line of any overlay has ever executed, across nine stages.** This edition is verified by `javap` on
  both carriers, by compilation against the line's real class path, and by the carriers' own bytecode for every
  behavioural claim it makes: that `EventHooks` posts the event on `NeoForge.EVENT_BUS`, that
  `onResourceReload` constructs and stores a new `DataMapLoader` per reload, that `tagsUpdated` applies it only on
  `SERVER_DATA_LOAD`, and that `DataMapLoader.apply()` posts `DataMapsUpdatedEvent` with `SERVER_RELOAD`. No reload
  has run through it, no `DataMapsUpdatedEvent` has been counted, and the stand-down has never been taken.
- **The probe reads a private carrier field.** `DATA_MAPS` is measured, and its failure mode is a warning that
  says it could not tell — not a crash, and not a false "registered". Nothing watches the watcher: if a future
  NeoForge build renames that field, the kernel loses the diagnostic and keeps the counts.
- **The edition has no behavioural test** — the committed one pins the 26.2 event names, as shown above.
- The **server reload twin** (`KernelForgeReload`) and the two name tables are still refused, for the reason
  stage 8 measured and this stage re-measured: their installers are descriptor-bound to 26.2 shapes, and fixing
  that is `src/main` work, i.e. 26.2's jar bytes.
- The energy artifact is still a **class-version stamp, not a Java-21 build**, and the remapped fabric modules
  remain compile-time only, so nothing in `build/transfer-api-mc/` may be loaded.


# Stage 10: the boot half is 26.2-keyed in STRINGS — 7,250 dead references measured, 10 fixed through a new gated mechanism, everything else refused with its count

Stage 7 classified the remaining distance into three categories and named one of them without touching it:

> (iii) an unaudited boot half: 31 source files and 125 lines across six carrier tables name 26.2-only types in
> STRINGS, which compiles on both lines and has therefore never been audited.

This is the most dangerous category left, for a reason that has nothing to do with size: **a compile error announces
itself, and a string does not.** `src/main` has no Minecraft types by construction — that is what makes it the boot
half — so javac never resolves a single one of the names it spells. A string that names a class or member which does
not exist on 1.21.1 compiles cleanly, ships, and fails only at the moment of use. Ten stages of verification could
not see it: hand-javac sees a source file with no line-keyed tokens, `javap` sees the two carriers agreeing on
everything the *sources* mention, and the four mandated 26.2 checks compare 26.2 to itself.

This stage does four things: **re-derives the count instead of trusting it**, classifies everything it finds,
**decides the mechanism before writing code**, and then fixes the ten sites where a 1.21.1 value is proven and
refuses the rest with their measurements.

## The two failures this category has already produced, restated because they are the shape of the whole finding

Both are recorded elsewhere in this document and both are still present in the tree:

1. `ForgeCreativeTabsInjector` declares its hook by the 26.2 **4-argument** descriptor while 1.21.1's carrier method
   is **5-argument** (stage 2, transformer axis). A REQUIRED anchor that cannot match.
2. `ServerReloadListenerNamesInjector` declares a REQUIRED anchor on `net.neoforged.neoforge.event.
   AddServerReloadListenersEvent`, which exists on 26.2 and **not** on 1.21.1 (stage 8, measured again below:
   `AddServerReloadListenersEvent` = 1 on 26.2, 0 on 1.21.1).

Neither is a crash. Both are a **silent no-op with a ledger entry**, which is exactly why fifteen other instances of
the same shape could sit in the tree unremarked. This stage is the whole set, not the examples.

## The instrument, and why it had to be built rather than borrowed

The question is "does this NAME exist on this LINE", asked thousands of times. Three instruments were available and
none of them answers it:

- **javac** cannot: the names are inside string literals, which are not symbols.
- **javap** answers it per class per line, at one process each (~150 ms against a 39-entry, 89 MB classpath). A
  census of a few thousand names would take minutes per line and would be re-run after every correction.
- **ASM** refuses class files newer than its own release — that is the energy-jar failure from stage 8 wearing a
  different hat, and a 26.2 class file is version 69.

So `tools/line-audit/classindex.py` reads the constant pool itself. The layout it depends on (magic, minor, major,
cp_count, cp[], access, this, super, interfaces, fields[], methods[]) has not changed since class file version 45
and does not know what version 69 is. It indexes each classpath once (33,180 entries for 1.21.1, 36,158 for 26.2)
and then answers thousands of queries in a second.

**It was validated against javap rather than trusted**, because a reader that silently desyncs the constant pool
returns *confidently wrong* answers and every count below rests on it. `tools/line-audit/validate-index.py` compares its
member list against `javap -p -s` for 40 random classes per line: **0 mismatches on 1.21.1, 0 on 26.2**. The first
version of the reader advanced four bytes over a `MethodHandle` (tag 15) entry, which is three, and therefore
answered *absent* for `ResourceLocation.fromNamespaceAndPath(String, String)` — a member javap shows. That is the
kind of error this stage is about, made by the audit of the stage itself, and it is why the validator is committed
to `build/x11` rather than remembered.

**All six scripts are COMMITTED, under `tools/line-audit/`**, and write their transcripts into
`forbric-kernel/build/x11/` — which `gradlew clean` deletes. That split is deliberate and is the answer to the
hazard this document has recorded four times: the *method* survives a clean, the *transcripts* do not, and the
transcripts that matter are restated in this section with their md5s. Re-running them from the tracked copies
reproduced this stage's numbers exactly (25,854 tokens, the same five buckets, 7,250 dead occurrences), which is
what makes them an instrument rather than a note.

The six scripts, each doing one thing:

| script | what it is for |
|---|---|
| `classindex.py` | does this class / member(+descriptor) exist on this line's real classpath |
| `bootstrings.py` | extracts every line-keyed name from `src/main` — a real Java lexer for the literals (a `//` inside a URL literal must not swallow a line; a text block is a literal), a name parser for the nine tables |
| `audit-strings.py` | asks both classpaths, buckets every token, prints per-carrier counts |
| `table-liveness.py` | for the five tables whose rows *rewrite*, is the row's KEY alive on 1.21.1 while its TARGET is dead |
| `validate-index.py` | holds `classindex.py` to `javap -p -s`, 40 random classes per line |
| `make-boot-patches.py` | generates `src/main/mc-line/1.21.1.patches` from the source lines, counting each site |

The two classpaths are the ones the build actually stages, dumped through `build/x11/dump-cp-line.gradle` (added
this stage so a 26.2 dump cannot overwrite the 1.21.1 classpath the M2 measurement depends on), and **each dump is
proved to be its own line before use**:

```
                    1.21.1   26.2
ResourceLocation       1       0
Identifier             0       1
GuiGraphicsExtractor   0       1
GuiGraphics            1       0
util/Util              0       1      (26.2 moved net.minecraft.Util into net.minecraft.util)
Util                   1       0
event/AddServerReloadListenersEvent      0   1   (the stage-8 REQUIRED anchor)
```

The staged jars underneath them differ in md5 for all three carriers and for the merged base, so this is not one
root read twice: `.dev/staged/run` (26.2) vs `.dev/staged-1211/run` (1.21.1), `forge-runtime.jar`
`7674eb16…` vs `a403af05…`, `neoforge-runtime.jar` `8f048b7a…` vs `6ab4d490…`, merged base `3f764fbe…` vs
`92dc2e8f…`.

## The re-derived count

`bootstrings.py` scans **545 files** (536 `.java`, 9 tables) and extracts **25,854 distinct line-keyed token
occurrences**, being **3,378 distinct class names** plus their members. "Line-keyed" means the namespaces whose
spelling or existence varies by line: `net/minecraft/`, `com/mojang/`, `net/neoforged/`, `net/minecraftforge/`,
`net/fabricmc/`, `org/spongepowered/`, `team/reborn/`. `java/*` names are the JDK's and are not line property.

| bucket | occurrences | what it means |
|---|---|---|
| **ok-both** | **17,588** | class (and member, when the token carries one) exists on both lines |
| **keyed** | **5,404** | class exists on 26.2, absent on 1.21.1 |
| **member-keyed** | **1,026** | class on both, exact name+descriptor absent on 1.21.1 |
| **member-keyed?** | **820** | name-only token whose member is absent on 1.21.1 (weaker evidence: `*` cannot separate overloads) |
| **absent-both** | **995** | on neither line — 26.2 synthetic names (`ByteBufCodecs$29`), lambda names, `VALUES`, and names invented by a pin |
| **other-line-only** | **21** | present on 1.21.1 and absent on 26.2 (a boot string naming something only this line has) |

**Dead on 1.21.1 = 7,250 occurrences**, split by carrier in a way that matters more than the total:

| carrier | occurrences | dead | share |
|---|---|---|---|
| boot `.java` (536 files) | 2,275 | **253 in 71 files** | 11.1 % |
| the nine tables (5,045 data lines) | 23,579 | **6,997 in 8 tables** | 29.7 % |

**1,422 distinct class names are dead.** The dead table rows are 2,460 lines:

| table | lines | dead rows |
|---|---|---|
| `transform/vanilla-early-returns.txt` | 3,269 | 1,516 |
| `mixin/native-only-methods.txt` | 1,014 | 438 |
| `mixin/carrier-renames.txt` | 396 | 243 |
| `mixin/uncalled-methods.txt` | 209 | 169 |
| `mixin/carrier-stubs.txt` | 85 | 55 |
| `mixin/carrier-helpers.txt` | 41 | 31 |
| `mixin/lambda-permutations.txt` | 10 | 5 |
| `mixin/carrier-twins.txt` | 12 | 3 |
| `interop/protocol/energy.api-contract.txt` | 9 | 0 |

### The count stage 7 reported, and where the difference comes from

Stage 7 reported **31 files, 125 lines, "six carrier tables"**. Re-derived here: **71 files, 253 occurrences, and
eight tables carrying dead rows**. Neither number is a correction of the other's arithmetic; they measure different
things, and it is worth being exact about which.

1. **"Six carrier tables" is seven.** The seven files under `mixin/` that stage 7 was naming total exactly
   **1,767 data lines** — the figure it reported — and there are **seven** of them, not six. With the transform
   table and the energy contract the boot side has **nine** committed tables and **5,045** data lines. The 1,767
   was right; the file count was not. (Counting was checked by summing `wc -l` over the seven: 41 + 396 + 85 + 12 +
   10 + 1,014 + 209 = 1,767.)
2. **31 files → 71 files is the cost of a hand list.** Stage 7 named a specific set of 26.2-only types
   (`Identifier`, `PackFormat`, `TooltipDisplay`, `FuelValues`, `WeightedList`, …) and counted the files that
   mention one of them. This stage names nothing in advance: every token is measured against both classpaths, so a
   dead name nobody thought to list is counted too. That is why the number more than doubles, and it is the whole
   argument for measuring rather than listing.
3. **The one number both stages measured the same way agrees.** Stage 7: "`Identifier` occurrences inside
   `src/main/java`: 21 (in 16 files)". This stage, independently: **20 occurrences in 16 files**. The single
   difference is a `Identifier` that stage 7's regex caught in a context this stage does not treat as a name
   occurrence — the file count, which is the part that would change a decision, is identical.

## The classification

The taxonomy, stated before the numbers, because the numbers are only as useful as the definitions:

- **(a)** resolves on both lines — nothing to do.
- **(b)** wrong on 1.21.1 **and reached**: the code that uses the string runs on this line, and the string is handed
  to something that resolves it (`Class.forName`, `getMethod`, `getField`, `invokeStaticOn`, `startBus`). This is a
  runtime failure, caught or not.
- **(c)** wrong on 1.21.1 and **not reached as a resolution**: pin data, anchor selectors, census rows, and names
  behind a probe that already fails closed. The failure is a silent no-op — *worse than a crash in one respect*
  (nobody sees it) and *better in another* (nothing dies).
- **(d)** something else: names on neither line, names only 1.21.1 has, and names whose consumer cannot be
  attributed to an owner at all.

| class | count | where |
|---|---|---|
| (a) resolves on both | **17,588 occurrences** | — |
| (b) reached resolution sites on a dead name | **30 sites** in 10 boot files | 23 same-line reflective sites + 8 bare member-name sites, less one that cannot be attributed to an owner |
| (c) pin / anchor / census data | **222 java occurrences + 6,997 table occurrences** | 71 files; 8 tables |
| (d) other | **1,016 name occurrences + 1 site** | 995 absent-both, 21 other-line-only, and one `getMethod("getValue")` whose receiver is a runtime object named `box` that nothing in the string identifies |

The **(b) 30** is the actionable set, and it is *larger than the string census alone can see*, which is the first
thing this stage has to say about its own instrument:

> **A member name passed to `getMethod("…")` with no owner in the same literal is invisible to a name census.**
> `getMethod("identifier")` is the whole defect at five sites and appears in the audit only if the census is allowed
> to see it — and it is not, because the owner is a *runtime object*. `bootstrings.py` records a member only when
> the same literal names its owner. There are **332 bare member-name strings** (`187` distinct) in the boot half.
> Five of them are `getMethod("identifier")` and two are `getMethod("getValue", …)` on a `Registry`; both were found
> by grepping for the member after the census had finished, and both were dead on 1.21.1. **Every count in this
> section is therefore a lower bound**, and the gap is exactly the set of names whose owner is not in the string.

### The tables, which need a second question answered

A census row that names a 26.2-only class cannot fire — no name observed on 1.21.1 can equal it — and that is a
silent no-op. But five of the nine tables have rows that *rewrite* a selector (`->`), and for those there is a
severity above "inert": a row whose KEY resolves on 1.21.1 while its TARGET does not. `table-liveness.py` measured
**514 rewriting rows**:

| state | rows | consequence on 1.21.1 |
|---|---|---|
| inert: key itself dead | **319** | row can never match — a no-op |
| **live key, dead target** | **115** in 14 distinct owner classes | the rule can match and rewrite to a member that does not exist on this line |
| live key, live target | **80** | the rule fires and rewrites to a member that *does* exist — on a premise measured on 26.2 |

The middle row is the one no per-name count would have produced, and the lower row is the one that cannot be
adjudicated without a launch. Examples, all from `carrier-renames`: `KeyMapping#getTranslatedKeyMessage()` is alive
on 1.21.1 and its target `lambda$getTranslatedKeyMessage$0` is not; `ServerLevel#getSeed()` alive, target
`lambda$getSeed$0` dead; `ServerPlayer#startSleepInBed(BlockPos)` alive, target `lambda$startSleepInBed$0` dead.

## The mechanism — decided here, before any code

Every previous stage's mechanism has been one of two things, and **neither of them applies to a string in a shared
source file**:

1. `src/runtime/mc-line/<line>.tsv` — a **token rename table** applied to `src/runtime/java` at build time. It cannot
   be pointed at the boot half, and the reason is measurable: the *same token* is a resolution name in one file and
   pin data in another. `net.minecraft.resources.Identifier` occurs at **2 resolution sites** (`KernelForgeWrapperSync`,
   `KernelClientSmoke`) and at **18 pin/anchor sites** in 16 files — `MixinEquivalentImplementations` pairs its
   spelling with a **26.2 body hash**, so renaming the spelling while leaving the hash produces a pin that is
   internally inconsistent: a *worse lie* than a pin that does not match. A table cannot tell the two apart; a
   human can, per site.
2. `src/runtime/mc-line/<line>/java/…` — a **whole-file overlay**, verbatim. It would be honest and it is what the
   runtime half uses for the ten subsystems that are genuinely different subsystems. Applied here it means
   duplicating `KernelClientSmoke.java` (2,600 lines) to change two strings in it.

There is a third thing that looks tempting and is refused on principle:

3. **A per-line edition of a census table.** Every one of the eight tables is *measured output* of a census test
   over a specific merged base (`CarrierRenameCensusTest`, `NativeOnlyMethodsCensusTest`, `VanillaEarlyReturnsCensusTest`,
   …). Renaming the classes and members inside a table would leave its **descriptors, counts, hashes, ecosystems and
   ordering** exactly as 26.2 measured them, under a header claiming a line it was never run against. Every consumer
   of these tables argues from "the table describes the base"; a hand-made 1.21.1 edition would be *fabricated
   evidence*, and the honest count of dead rows (2,460) would be replaced by 2,460 rows that are wrong in a way
   nothing can detect. **The tables are therefore out of scope for any mechanism this stage builds**, and the
   refusal is the finding: they need a 1.21.1 *census run*, not an edition.

So the mechanism is a fourth thing, and it is chosen because it is the only one that is both surgical and
unambiguous:

> **`src/main/mc-line/<line>.patches` — a line-keyed list of EXACT source substitutions, applied by the build to a
> copy of `src/main/java` and only when `mcLine != mcLineDefault`.**
>
> ```
> <file relative to src/main/java> <TAB> <expected occurrences> <TAB> <exact old text> <TAB> <exact new text>
> ```
>
> - It is **not a token table**: each row is the full text of one site, so it can only ever change the site it was
>   written for. There is no rule to over-apply and no way to rewrite a pin's spelling by accident.
> - It is **not a file overlay**: one row changes one string, and the rest of the file keeps being maintained in
>   one place.
> - The **expected-occurrence count is an assertion**: the task fails if the file holds a different number of
>   copies, so a source edit that moves or duplicates a site turns into a build failure naming the row instead of a
>   patch that silently stops applying.
> - The task is registered **only when `mcLine != mcLineDefault`**. On 26.2 there is no task, no generated source
>   dir, no input, and therefore no byte — the same shape as `normalizeRuntimeJava` (stage 1) and
>   `retargetRebornEnergy` (stage 8), both of which are absent from the default line's task list.
> - It **cannot** touch the tables, because `src/main/resources` is not in its scope, and that is deliberate: the
>   refusal above is enforced by the mechanism's shape rather than by a promise.

What it cannot do, said now so it is not discovered later:

- **It cannot fix a shape difference.** `BusGroup` → 1.21.1's Forge bus model is a different API, not a different
  spelling; the input-event records `net.minecraft.client.input.*` do not exist at all on 1.21.1. Those are
  *subsystem editions* and stay refused.
- **It cannot make a pin true.** Renaming a class inside a hash-paired pin is not done, and no row does it.
- **It cannot verify a runtime behaviour.** It changes the name a `Class.forName` asks for; whether the code that
  follows that call does the right thing on 1.21.1 is not observable without a launch, and this stage does not have
  one.

With the mechanism decided, the ten sites that qualify are the ones where (i) the consumer **resolves** the name,
(ii) the 1.21.1 counterpart exists, and (iii) the counterpart is the *same concept with the same shape* — each proved
by the same instrument, not by the rename table's word:

| site | 26.2 spelling | 1.21.1 spelling | proof |
|---|---|---|---|
| `KernelForgeWrapperSync:106`, `:369`; `KernelLifecycle:2042`; `KernelRegistryAliases:130`; `KernelWrapperEntryEvents:97` | `ResourceKey#identifier()` | `ResourceKey#location()` | `M ResourceKey identifier` = 1/0, `location` = 0/1 (26.2/1.21.1); javap both: 26.2 has `identifier()`, 1.21.1 has `location()`, and neither has the other |
| `KernelForgeWrapperSync:171`; `KernelClientSmoke:2241` | `net.minecraft.resources.Identifier` | `net.minecraft.resources.ResourceLocation` | stage 1's rule, re-measured here: `C` = 0/1 and 1/0 |
| `KernelForgeWrapperSync:177`; `KernelClientSmoke:2243` | `Registry#getValue(Identifier)` | `Registry#get(ResourceLocation)` | 1.21.1 `get(LResourceLocation;)Ljava/lang/Object;` = 1, `getValue` = 0; 26.2 the reverse; both return the value `T` |
| `KernelClientSmoke:961` | `net.minecraft.world.entity.EntitySpawnReason` | `net.minecraft.world.entity.MobSpawnType` | stage 1's rule; `MobSpawnType.COMMAND` present; `EntityType.spawn(ServerLevel, BlockPos, MobSpawnType)` present and same-shaped on both lines |

Ten sites, five files. The repair that motivates the largest of them is a whole behaviour rather than a log line:
`KernelForgeWrapperSync.finishSnapshotApplication` runs at login on both lines (its injector is
`RegistrySyncParityInjector`), and on 1.21.1 it currently falls into its own `catch (Throwable)` and logs
"could not apply the server's ids to the Forge-wrapped registries — they keep their local ids". Every other
reflective lookup in that method resolves on 1.21.1 (`ForgeRegistry$Snapshot`, `GameData.injectSnapshot`,
`Registry.getKey`, `IdMap.getId`, `IdMapper.add`, `Block.getStateDefinition`, `StateDefinition.getPossibleStates`,
`BuiltInRegistries.BLOCK` — all measured), so those two names are the entire defect in that path.

## What this stage refuses, with the count that makes each refusal concrete

| refused | count | why |
|---|---|---|
| per-line editions of the eight census tables | **2,460 dead rows** (of which **115 can match and rewrite to a dead target**) | fabricated census cannot be distinguished from measured census; needs a 1.21.1 census run |
| the `BusGroup` sites | **5**, four of them inside the 30 (`KernelEventSubscribers:401`, `KernelForgeInternalSubscribers:146`, `KernelLifecycle:597`, `LateForgeRegistryDeclarations:29`, `KernelForgeModContext:60`, a constant-mediated fifth) | `net/minecraftforge/eventbus/api/bus/` = **0 classes** on 1.21.1; this line's Forge bus is `BusBuilder` + `IEventBus` (stage 3), a shape change |
| the 26.2 client input records | **9 sites** (`KeyEvent`, `MouseButtonInfo`, `InputWithModifiers`, `MouseButtonEvent`, `CharacterEvent`) | `net/minecraft/client/input/` = **0 classes** on 1.21.1 — a whole event model, not a name |
| the FML jar-contents SPI | **2 sites** (`JarContents`, `JarResource`) | `net/neoforged/fml/jarcontents/` = **0 classes** on 1.21.1 — the FML view stage 7 refused as a subsystem |
| FML loading internals | **3 sites** (`LoadingModListImpl` ×2, `ModSorter$State`) | different FML loading shape; both under catches that already report absence as ordinary |
| `GameRuleCategory` | **1 site** | `net/minecraft/world/level/gamerules/` = **0 classes**; 1.21.1 keeps `GameRules$Category` |
| `ClientNetworkRegistry` | **1 site** | `net/neoforged/neoforge/client/network/registration/` = **0 classes** on 1.21.1 |
| pins, anchors and selectors | **230 java occurrences** | changing a name inside a pin whose hash is 26.2's makes the data inconsistent rather than true |

## The mechanism, as wired

`build.gradle` gains one task inside the block that only exists when `mcLine != mcLineDefault`:

```
normalizeBootJava (Sync)   src/main/java  ->  build/generated/mc-line/<line>/boot-java
                           then applies src/main/mc-line/<line>.patches, exact substitutions
                           sourceSets.main.java.srcDirs := [that directory]
                           compileJava.dependsOn normalizeBootJava
```

On 26.2 the block is not entered: no task, no generated directory, no patch file read, `sourceSets.main` is still
the Gradle defaults. That is the same gating shape as `normalizeRuntimeJava` (stage 1) and `retargetRebornEnergy`
(stage 8), and it is the reason the four checks below can say "zero mentions of the line tasks".

The patch file, `src/main/mc-line/1.21.1.patches`, is generated from the source lines themselves by
`build/x11/make-boot-patches.py` so that the `old` text is exact by construction and the occurrence count is counted
rather than typed. Its rows and the proof for each are in the stage-10 section above; the file itself carries the
adjudication rules in its header so the next reader does not have to find this document to know what may be added.

Three failure modes are turned into build failures rather than silence, because a patch mechanism that fails
quietly would be a second instance of the defect it exists to fix:

- a row that does not split into exactly four TAB-separated fields;
- a row whose `old` and `new` are identical (a correction that did not happen);
- a row whose expected occurrence count does not match the file (`found 0, expected 1`) — this is the one that
  matters, because it is what a moved site looks like. Its message names the file, the counts and the row's text
  and says the row must be re-adjudicated against this line.

And one property that is a *shape* rather than a promise: **`src/main/resources` is not in the task's scope at all**,
so no row can reach a census table even if someone wrote one. The refusal of per-line table editions is enforced by
what the mechanism cannot touch.

### What the mechanism measured, on the tree it produces

The same audit, re-run against `build/generated/mc-line/1.21.1/boot-java` instead of `src/main/java`:

| bucket | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| keyed (class absent on 1.21.1) | 5,404 | **5,401** |
| member-keyed | 1,026 | 1,026 |
| dead occurrences | 7,250 | **7,247** |
| dead files | 79 | **78** |

**Three of the ten rows move a number, and that is the honest accounting of the instrument, not of the fix.** The
other seven rows are bare member-name sites (`getMethod("identifier")`, `getMethod("getValue", …)`) whose owner is a
runtime object and therefore not in the string — the census cannot see them, which is why they were found by
grepping for the member after the census had finished. Their effect is checked the only way it can be, by counting
the spelling in the generated tree:

```
getMethod("identifier")                          src/main: 5   generated: 0
getMethod("location")                            src/main: 0   generated: 5
getMethod("getValue", <Registry receiver>)        src/main: 2   generated: 0
```

So the fix's measured footprint is **10 sites**, of which the audit can see 3 and the other 7 are visible only to a
grep — and the difference between those two sentences is the size of the audit's blind spot, stated rather than
buried.

## What the four checks say

### (a) 26.2 builds, and is byte-identical to a build from `git stash -u`ed HEAD

`JAVA_HOME=jdk-25.0.2`, `gradlew.bat --no-daemon clean jar` → **BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 25s**, Gradle reporting `10 actionable tasks: 10 executed`, and
**zero** mentions of `mcLine`, `normalizeBootJava`, `normalizeRuntimeJava` or `retargetRebornEnergy` in the log.

```
boot     md5 709f455ab3accdb60d720891ae7b5b5b   2,623 entries
runtime  md5 7d2c9f4d093a4307a5c03281b8d96b0d     324 entries
```

Both `md5`s are the ones the previous stage recorded, and both were compared **entry-and-CRC** against a build made
from `git stash -u`ed HEAD in this session (`build/x11/jar-crc.py`: every entry's name, CRC32 and uncompressed size,
sorted): the diff is **empty for both jars**. The HEAD build reproduced `709f455…`/`7d2c9f4d…` exactly, so the
comparison is against a fresh baseline rather than against a possibly-stale file.

### (b) `compileRuntimeJava` from nothing

`rm -rf build/classes/java/runtime` then `compileRuntimeJava` → `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 11s`, **295 class files**.

### (c) a 1.21.1 attempt, then 26.2

The 1.21.1 `jar` invocation runs `:normalizeBootJava` (10 rows applied, one per site, logged), compiles the boot
half **successfully on 1.21.1** — the first time the boot half has ever been compiled under a line-specific spelling,
which is the whole point of the mechanism — then `:normalizeRuntimeJava` logs **34 file(s) REPLACED**, `:remapTransferApis`,
`:retargetRebornEnergy` and `:verifyRebornEnergy` pass, and the build fails at **`:compileRuntimeJava`, exactly one
task**, as stage 7 established. Immediately afterwards `rm -rf build/classes/java/runtime` and a plain 26.2
`compileRuntimeJava` → **BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 22s, 295 class files, zero line-task mentions**.

### (d) the suite, counted from the XML and not from the console row

`gradlew.bat --no-daemon test` on 26.2, with `build/x11` (including `classes1211-runtime`) restored after the
mandated `clean`: **906 suites, 5,635 tests, 79 failures, 0 errors, 842 skipped** — identical to the baseline.

The 79 decompose exactly as before: **77 `java.io.IOException` at `TempDirectory.java:350`** plus exactly two
`org.opentest4j.AssertionFailedError`, at `ForgeRegistrationA2ContractTest.clientGateSeedsF6AndBothScriptsParse` and
`KernelRuntimeClassesTest.everyGameSideClassTheBootSideNamesIsInTheRegistry`. **Neither was touched**, and neither is
in the boot half's string path — the second one is the registry test that is *supposed* to notice a boot-side name
without a game-side class, and it fails for the reason stage 4 recorded.

### The M2 count is unmoved, which is the point

The corrections are strings: they cannot change the number of compile errors, and they did not.

```
TOTAL 190   (javac says 191 errors)
  axis1-minecraft        82
  axis2-forge-neoforge   90
  axis3-fabric-transfer   0
  unattributed cascades  18
transcript md5 d6294b11f86b8a661a6d3d2ad4e2549f
```

`md5 d6294b11…` is **byte-identical** to the stage-9 baseline quoted at the head of this document, re-measured with
`measure.sh --line 1.21.1 --energy build` after the boot half was patched. That is the strongest statement available
that this stage moved nothing it was not supposed to move: 190 was and is the compile distance, and it is unchanged
by a change to ten strings.

## What is still not verified, stated plainly

- **No 1.21.1 kernel jar exists and no launch has been attempted.** Nothing in this stage executed. The ten
  corrections are verified as *name resolutions* — the class or member exists on 1.21.1 — and by nothing else.
  `ResourceKey#location()` is `ResourceKey#identifier()`'s counterpart because the project's own stage-1 table says
  so and `javap` shows neither class declares both; whether the code downstream of each call does the same thing on
  this line is a launch-time fact and there is no launch.
- **`KernelForgeWrapperSync.apply` is not proved repaired, only proved un-blocked at two names.** Everything else it
  resolves was measured present on 1.21.1, but it is reached from `RegistryManager` through an injector that has
  never run on this line, and its `catch (Throwable)` still exists for the reasons it was written.
- **The audit's (b) count is a lower bound, by construction, and the gap is measured but not closed.** 332 bare
  member-name strings (`187` distinct) name a member without naming its owner; the census cannot attribute them, so
  it cannot test them. Eight were examined by hand (five `identifier`, two `getValue` on a `Registry`, one
  unattributable) and all eight are reported here; the other 324 are **untested**. A stage that wants the whole set
  needs a *dataflow* pass over the boot bytecode, not a census of literals.
- **The tables are untouched and their 2,460 dead rows stand**, including the **115 rows whose key resolves on
  1.21.1 while their target does not**. Nothing in this stage changed a table, and no table row has ever been
  matched at runtime on either line.
- **The 80 `live key, live target` rows are the ones this stage cannot judge.** Both sides resolve on 1.21.1, so
  the rule can fire and retarget a selector; whether the retarget is right is a behavioural question that 26.2's
  census cannot answer for 1.21.1.
- **`Identifier` is still spelled 18 times in pins and anchors** in 16 boot files, deliberately: those occurrences
  are paired with 26.2 body hashes and 26.2 anchor premises, and renaming them would make inconsistent data instead
  of true data. They will fail closed on 1.21.1 exactly as they do today.
- **Nothing has been done about the two category-(iii) failures already recorded** — `ForgeCreativeTabsInjector`'s
  4-vs-5-argument `HOOK_DESC` and `ServerReloadListenerNamesInjector`'s absent `AddServerReloadListenersEvent`.
  The event name was re-measured here (**1** on 26.2, **0** on 1.21.1); the 4-vs-5-argument descriptor was **not**
  re-measured this stage and is taken from stage 2's record. Neither is a string a substitution can fix: both
  need the 1.21.1 *shape* of the carrier call.
- **The absence claims are against the staged artifacts on this machine.** `.dev/staged/run` and
  `.dev/staged-1211/run` are the only carriers this stage read; the namespace coverage was checked (FML **259** classes on 26.2 vs **248** on 1.21.1, all loader
  roots **1,541** vs **1,648**) so a missing *namespace* is not masquerading as a missing *name*, but a
  carrier that is itself incomplete on this machine would still read as absence.
- **The instrument has the weaknesses of its own method**, stated so the next round can attack them: names are
  extracted by pattern (a name built by string concatenation across literals is not reconstructed — the boot half
  does this in several places, e.g. the `"Lnet/minecraftforge/"` + descriptor prefixes); `member-keyed?` buckets
  (820) are name-only and cannot separate overloads; classes are indexed by their jar entry name, so a
  multi-release jar would be mis-indexed (none in the classpath is one); and `bootstrings.py`'s Java lexer is a
  lexer, not a parser — a literal split across a `+` with a non-literal operand is recorded as its own fragment.
- **`dev.py prepare` was never run, and `E:\project\Forbric_tweaked test` was untouched.**

# Stage 11: the census run — what produces the eight tables, which of them this line can be measured on, and the 4 of 8 that were re-run while the other 4 are refused with their counts

Stage 10 closed category (iii) as a mechanism and left exactly one item open, in its own words:

> **The tables need a 1.21.1 census run**, not edits. That is a new work item (8 tables, 2,460 rows; and **115 rows are
> actively wrong-shaped, not merely dead**).

and it explained why editing cannot substitute for it:

> **a per-line edition of a census table is refused on principle — renaming inside one leaves 26.2's descriptors,
> counts, hashes and ecosystems under a header claiming a line it was never run against.**

This stage takes that item. It asks the three questions the item implies — *what produces each table, can that producer
be pointed at this line's artifacts, and can the run be gated so 26.2 keeps its tables byte-identical* — and it answers
the fourth one the item could not answer in advance: **what actually happens to the 115 rows.** Four tables are re-run.
Four are refused, each with the measurement that makes the refusal concrete rather than cautious.

Nothing in this stage is an edit to a row. Not one line of any census table was written by hand; the 1.21.1 tables are
the byte-for-byte output of the same producers the 26.2 suite runs, and the proof that they are is in "The control that
says this is a re-run, not an edition" below.

## What produces each table, named

Every one of the eight is a **JUnit test that re-derives its table from the staged jars with ASM and then asserts the
shipped file equals the derivation**. There is no generator executable and no separate tool: the producer *is* the test,
and the write switch (`FORBRIC_WRITE_*`) is the same code path with the comparison turned into a file write. The first
column is the shipped table, the second the test that produces it, the third what it reads.

| table | producer | what it reads |
|---|---|---|
| `transform/vanilla-early-returns.txt` | `VanillaEarlyReturnsCensusTest` | vanilla's jar + the merged base, the latter through `ForbricMergedBaseCompatTransformer` |
| `mixin/native-only-methods.txt` | `NativeOnlyMethodsCensusTest` | the merged base, the three platforms' jars, the merge's `.pins`, both carriers' manifests, vanilla's version JSON **and every library it lists** |
| `mixin/carrier-renames.txt` | `CarrierRenameCensusTest` | the merged base + vanilla's jar + MinecraftForge's patched jar + NeoForge's patched jar |
| `mixin/uncalled-methods.txt` | `UncalledMethodCensusTest` | the merged base, `forge-runtime-interop.jar`, both runtimes, **and the kernel's own boot *and* game-side classes** |
| `mixin/carrier-stubs.txt` | `CarrierStubCensusTest` | the merged base, both carriers, interop, and **the kernel's boot *and* game-side classes** |
| `mixin/carrier-helpers.txt` | `CarrierHelperCensusTest` | the merged base + the three platforms' jars |
| `mixin/lambda-permutations.txt` | `LambdaPermutationCensusTest` | the merged base + vanilla's jar |
| `mixin/carrier-twins.txt` | `CarrierTwinCensusTest` | the merged base + **the shipped `uncalled-methods.txt`** |

Two consequences follow from that table and are worth stating before the verdicts:

1. **The producers are not a tool that can be aimed; they are tests that can be *run* on a classpath.** Pointing them at
   1.21.1 means running them in a JVM whose fixture paths resolve to this line's jars.
2. **They are not independent.** `carrier-twins` is derived from `uncalled-methods`, so a refusal of the second is a
   refusal of the eighth as well. That is a property of the census, not of this stage's tooling.

## The finding that made a run possible at all: the test half already compiles on this line

The runtime half does not compile on 1.21.1 (190 errors, `:compileRuntimeJava` the single failing task) and never has.
The **test** half is a different story, and it is measurable rather than arguable: **not one source file under
`src/test/java` names a Minecraft type** (`grep -rl '^import net\.minecraft' src/test/java` → 0 files). The census tests
read Minecraft's *bytes* through ASM and name its classes only in strings, which is the same discipline that makes the
boot half portable. So:

```
./gradlew.bat --no-daemon compileTestJava -Pforbric.mcLine=1.21.1
        -Pforbric.stagedRoot=…/forbric-kernel/.dev/staged-1211/run
  ->  BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 45s   (compileJava = the boot half, compileToolsJava, compileTestJava)
```

The whole test source set — including all eight producers — compiles on this line. What does **not** work is the `test`
task, and for a reason that has nothing to do with the tests: `test` declares
`dependsOn tasks.named('compileRuntimeJava')`, and that task fails on this line. So the producers can run here, but only
through a task of their own.

## The two traps this line's staging directory sets, both closed before anything ran

Both were found by measuring the fixtures rather than reading them.

1. **`TestFixtures.vanillaJar()` is `versions/26.2/26.2.jar` as a literal**, and each producer hardcodes
   `merged-base/patched-mc-merged-26.2.jar`, `neoforge-patched/patched-mc-neoforge-26.2.jar` and `forgeMergeInput()`'s
   26.2 names. The producers' *paths* are line-blind; their *logic* is not.
2. **`.dev/staged-1211/run/merged-base/patched-mc-merged-26.2.jar` exists and is a byte-identical copy of the 1.21.1
   base** — `md5 92dc2e8f7599882adcc2b78bb9b6f668` under both names, same size, one inode. A 1.21.1 build run today with
   `-Pforbric.stagedRoot=.dev/staged-1211/run` therefore opens **1.21.1 bytes under a 26.2 filename**: the producers pass
   their fixture guards, derive a *1.21.1* census, and compare it against the shipped 26.2 table — a run that reads as a
   failure of the port and is in fact a correctly-shaped answer under a wrong label. Any mechanism here has to resolve a
   table's line from its **content**, never from a file name, which is the property a census needs anyway.

## Whether the producer can be re-run here: per table, with the premise that decides it

| table | dead rows (stage 10) | this line's premise | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| `vanilla-early-returns` | 1,516 | vanilla 1.21.1 + the 1.21.1 merged base, both staged | **re-run** |
| `carrier-renames` | 243 | merged base + vanilla + both carriers, all four staged | **re-run** |
| `carrier-helpers` | 31 | merged base + vanilla + both carriers | **re-run** |
| `lambda-permutations` | 5 | merged base + vanilla | **re-run** |
| `uncalled-methods` | 169 | needs the kernel's **game-side classes**, which this line cannot compile | refused |
| `carrier-stubs` | 55 | the same | refused |
| `carrier-twins` | 3 | derived from `uncalled-methods` | refused by chain |
| `native-only-methods` | 438 | needs vanilla's **library tree**; **92 of the 97** jars 1.21.1's version JSON lists are absent from this machine | refused |

The refusals are two different measurements, not one caution:

- **The kernel's game side (3 tables, 227 rows).** `uncalled-methods` and `carrier-stubs` add
  `build/classes/java/runtime` to "the merged game" before deciding what nothing calls. On this line that directory
  cannot be produced. The trap is that it **exists anyway**, left by the last 26.2 build: the tests'
  `requireCompiledJava` guard would read it, find classes, and pass, and the census would then scan 26.2's kernel classes
  as if they were this line's. That is a silent wrong answer of exactly the shape stage 10 was written about, so off the
  default line the fixture resolves to a path that does not exist and those producers fail closed.
- **The library tree (1 table, 438 rows).** `native-only-methods` walks vanilla's version JSON and writes a `# library`
  row for each listed jar that ships classes in vanilla's packages, and by its own contract a listed jar that is missing
  is a missing fixture. Measured: of 1.21.1's 97 listed libraries, **5 are on disk and 92 are not** (`libraries/` holds
  26.2's). Three of the five present ship vanilla-package classes (`com.mojang:blocklist`, `brigadier`, `patchy`), which
  is why the absent 92 are load-bearing rather than decorative.

## The decision, taken before the code

> **The producers are the tests. This stage therefore does not write a second producer — it gives the existing one a
> line, and runs it.**
>
> 1. `TestFixtures` gains the line: `-Dforbric.censusLine`, defaulting to `26.2`. Every jar a census reads is resolved
>    through it (`vanillaJar()`, `mergedBase()`, `neoForgePatched()`, `forgeMergeInput()`, `kernelGameSide()`). On the
>    default line every one of those resolves to **exactly the path string it resolves to today**, so the 26.2 run is
>    the same run it was.
> 2. A `Test` task, `lineCensus`, is registered **only when `mcLine != mcLineDefault`**. It runs the producer tests on
>    this line's classpath and does **not** depend on `compileRuntimeJava`. On 26.2 there is no such task.
> 3. A run writes into `src/main/mc-line/<line>-tables/` — the line's committed census — and a gated resources overlay
>    makes the line's jar carry it. `src/main/resources` is untouched by that mechanism, so no 26.2 table can be reached
>    by it: the refusal is enforced by shape, not by promise.
> 4. **A table says which line it was measured on.** On a non-default line the header carries `# census-line <line>` and
>    the md5 of every jar the derivation read. The 26.2 files cannot gain that line — they must stay byte-identical — and
>    the asymmetry is stated rather than hidden: 26.2's tables say "26.2" by being the ones only 26.2 ships.
>
> What the mechanism refuses, by construction:
>
> - a per-line table that was not measured (the overlay only ever carries a file a run wrote);
> - a hand-edited row (the `lineCensus` task re-derives and compares, and fails on one differing row);
> - a 26.2 byte (no task, no srcDir, no resource on the default line).

## The run, and the first thing it caught: the vanilla reference was obfuscated

The first 1.21.1 run produced a table set that looked plausible and was **wrong**, and the way it was wrong is the
strongest argument for running rather than editing:

```
carrier-renames rows 204 — and not ONE of them tagged FABRIC
lambda-permutations  0 rows
vanilla-early-returns 7 folded methods (26.2: 3,263 rows)
```

All three are the same defect. `TestFixtures.vanillaJar()` is `versions/<line>/<line>.jar`, and **1.21.1's client jar is
obfuscated**: 8,269 classes, not one named `net/minecraft/client/Minecraft`, `ResourceLocation` absent. 26.2 ships
unobfuscated, so on the default line "the launcher's jar" and "the game in the names the merged base uses" are the same
file, and the distinction had never been needed. Here it is the whole answer: every comparison's left-hand side was
anonymous, so everything the FABRIC column asks came back empty.

The line's game **in Mojmap** does exist on this machine and the pipeline made it:
`.dev/minecraft/.forbric-build/clean-mojmap.jar` — 8,272 classes, class file **65**, `ResourceLocation` present, the
unpatched jar the merged base was merged from. `TestFixtures.mojmapGame()` resolves it (with a
`net/minecraft/client/Minecraft` probe and a class-file-major check against the merged base, because that file's name
carries no version and its directory is shared with 26.2), and every name census now reads it. On the default line it
returns `versions/26.2/26.2.jar` — the same file as before, so 26.2 is unchanged by construction.

## The control that says this is a re-run, not an edition

Two measurements, both of which a hand-edited table fails.

1. **The producer reproduces 26.2 byte-for-byte.** Every one of the eight write switches was turned on and the whole
   census re-run against the 26.2 tree the tables were measured from (`.dev/staged/run`, the one that carries both
   carriers): **19 tests, 0 skipped, 0 failed**, all eight tables rewritten on disk at 18:14–18:15 — and
   `git status src/main/resources` is **empty**. Not "equivalent": the same bytes, header and rows, with the default
   line's stamp empty. The refactor that gave the producers a line changed no table.
2. **The 1.21.1 edition is what the 1.21.1 jars say, checked by re-deriving it.** `:lineCensus` without the write switch
   re-runs the producers and compares them to the committed files: **3 passed, 5 skipped, 0 failed** (the skips are the
   refusals below, each with its reason). It also caught a stale table during this stage: the pass after a write fails on
   exactly the table that was just rewritten, because the comparison reads the copy the line's resources carry.

## What the run produced

```
src/main/mc-line/1.21.1-tables/net/forbric/kernel/…
```

| table | 26.2 rows | 1.21.1 rows | md5 (1.21.1 edition) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `transform/vanilla-early-returns.txt` | 3,263 | **2,689** | `7ee935951ab725d1ef2636aa2a9dcf93` |
| `mixin/carrier-renames.txt` | 385 | **292** | `5a6fa89eb5a1c4ace3f38d2f70b66fbd` |
| `mixin/carrier-helpers.txt` | 37 | **56** | `a58ecb06fc80108327028178de6d14ff` |

Each begins with the derivation's own stamp, so the file cannot be mistaken for another line's:

```
# census-line 1.21.1
# input patched-mc-merged-1.21.1.jar 92dc2e8f7599882adcc2b78bb9b6f668
# input clean-mojmap.jar 6be8e895f47bb6e4f91847751f102055
```

The rows are not a renamed set of 26.2's. Of the 33 `(owner, method)` keys the 26.2 `carrier-renames` table carries
against the 37 this line's does, **28 exist only on 26.2 and 32 only on 1.21.1**; **five** are on both lines, **three
with the same target** and **two retargeted**:

```
26.2   KeyMapping#getTranslatedKeyMessage()Component  -> lambda$getTranslatedKeyMessage$0()Component
1.21.1 KeyMapping#getTranslatedKeyMessage()Component  -> lambda$getTranslatedKeyMessage$2()Component

26.2   ServerPlayer#startSleepInBed(BlockPos)Either -> lambda$startSleepInBed$0(BlockPos)Either
1.21.1 ServerPlayer#startSleepInBed(BlockPos)Either -> lambda$startSleepInBed$13(BlockPos)Either
```

That is the hazard stage 10 described, measured from both ends: `javap` on the 1.21.1 base shows the sleep lambda is
`$13` and `KeyMapping`'s is `$2`, so the 26.2 rows could match the key and rewrite to a method this line does not
declare — while the correct target was one number away. The ecosystem column comes back too: **209 of the 292 rows are
FABRIC-tagged** (85 FABRIC, 122 FABRIC+FORGE, 2 FABRIC+NEOFORGE), which is the column the obfuscated-vanilla run had
emptied.

## What happened to the 115 wrong-shaped rows

None of them was edited, and none is "gone" as if deleted. Each of the 115 was classified against what the 1.21.1
derivation says about the same key:

| fate | rows | what it means |
|---|---|---|
| **retargeted to a target this line has** | **37** | the key still names a carrier-split body here, under another name: 35 `ServerPlayer#startSleepInBed` rows now point at `lambda$startSleepInBed$13`, 1 `KeyMapping` row at `lambda$getTranslatedKeyMessage$2`, 1 `carrier-helpers` row at this line's helper |
| **dropped, because the premise is false here** | **70** | no row exists for that key on this line: 63 `AnvilMenu#createResult` (1.21.1's merged base declares `createResult` and **no** `createResultInternal` — `javap`), 4 `ServerLevel#getSeed`, 3 `carrier-helpers` keys whose call did not move |
| **still 26.2's** | **8** | all in `carrier-stubs`, which this line cannot run |

The honest sentence is therefore: **37 of the 115 now name the member this line actually declares, 70 describe something
this line's carriers did not do, and 8 are still standing.** The mechanism is structural rather than editorial — a row
only exists if the merged base declares the target, so a derivation over this line's base *cannot* emit a row whose
target is absent. That is the same property the independent measurement below checks.

The 115 was re-measured from the other direction, by a new instrument (`tools/line-audit/table-resolve.py`) asking the
stage-10 `classindex.py` about every key and every target of the four rewriting tables against the **1.21.1** classpath:

| rows asked of the 1.21.1 classpath | rows | dead key | dead target | **live key AND dead target** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| the shipped 26.2 tables | 507 | 312 | 417 | **115** |
| the 1.21.1 editions | 348 | **0** | **0** | **0** |

The 115 reproduces stage 10's number exactly, from the other end, which is what makes the second line a measurement of
the same property rather than of a different one.

The 72 evidence members of the re-run tables that a *declaration-only* index cannot find are the instrument's known
limit, not dead names: sampled and confirmed with `javap`, they are JDK types (`java/lang/Math`, `java/lang/String`) or
members declared in a supertype (`Player#getHealth` is `LivingEntity`'s; `invulnerableTime` is `Entity`'s).

## The five refusals, each with the measurement that decides it

Three tables were re-run and five refused. The refusals are not caution; each is a premise that is absent, and the
producer now fails closed on it rather than answering.

| refused | dead rows still 26.2's | the measurement |
|---|---|---|
| `uncalled-methods` | 169 | its "merged game" includes the kernel's own **game-side** classes, which this line cannot compile (190 errors). `TestFixtures.kernelGameSide()` returns a path that does not exist off the default line, because `build/classes/java/runtime` holds the last 26.2 build's classes and scanning it would produce a plausible table about the wrong kernel |
| `carrier-stubs` | 55 | the same premise, and the run **proved it is load-bearing**: with the obfuscated vanilla it never got past `original == null`; with the Mojmap game it reaches a computed stub and fails closed on the kernel fixture. 1.21.1's merged base does have delegating stubs that work an argument out |
| `carrier-twins` | 3 | derived from `uncalled-methods`, so refused by chain, with the reason named in the test |
| `native-only-methods` | 438 | walks vanilla's version JSON and needs every library it lists: **5 of 1.21.1's 97 are on disk, 92 are not** (`libraries/` holds 26.2's). Second, independent reason: this line's launcher jar is obfuscated, so its member lists would need the Mojmap jar while its `version.json`/library premise needs the launcher one — on 26.2 those are one file and here they are two |
| `lambda-permutations` | 5 | its proof is that each vanilla parameter matches the merged one by local variable **name** and type. On this line the two artifacts name parameters differently **by construction**: `$$0/$$1` in `clean-mojmap.jar`, `p_331067_/p_333112_` in the merged base, because the base is merged from MinecraftForge's patched jar, which carries MCP-era names. Measured: **9,907** vanilla lambdas are found once in the merged base and **356** of them have a different descriptor — the permutations exist — and the proof accepts **none**. An empty table would be a false "no permutations" |

So the 1.21.1 jar carries **1,790 re-run rows and 670 that are still 26.2's** (169 + 55 + 3 + 438 + 5), named here so
the next round starts from the list rather than from a guess. The counts fall where they can be measured and are
unchanged where they cannot.

## What the mechanism is, as wired

- `src/test/java/net/forbric/kernel/CensusTable.java` — the `census-table` tag, so the eight producers can be selected
  where they live. Only the `theShippedTableIsExactlyWhatTheArtifactsSay` method of each carries it: the other methods
  in those classes assert **26.2** facts (`lambda$startSleepInBed$0`, `Camera#update`'s two keys) and must not be asked
  whether this line agrees with 26.2's answer.
- `TestFixtures` — `censusLine()` (`-Dforbric.censusLine`, default `26.2`), one resolver per jar a census reads
  (`mergedBase`, `mojmapGame`, `neoForgePatched`, `forgeMergeInput`, `forgeRuntimeInterop`, `kernelGameSide`), the
  write root (`censusTableRoot`: `src/main/resources` on the default line, `src/main/mc-line/<line>-tables/` otherwise),
  the stamp, and `writeCensusTable`.
- `build.gradle`, inside `if (mcLine != mcLineDefault)`: `lineCensus` (a `Test` task filtered by the tag, no dependency
  on `compileRuntimeJava`), and `normalizeBootResources` (a `Sync` of `src/main/resources` with the line's edition
  overlaid, which becomes the only resources source dir for that line). `-Pforbric.censusWrite` turns the producers'
  writers on. On 26.2: no task, no source dir, no resource, no byte.
- `src/main/mc-line/1.21.1-tables/` — the line's committed census, one file per table, under the resource path the
  kernel reads it from.
- `tools/line-audit/table-resolve.py` — committed, like stage 10's six scripts and for the same reason: the stage's own
  measurement must survive a `clean`, and `build/x11` does not.

### The line endings, which cost one measurement to notice

The three committed tables were written with LF and came back **CRLF** after a `git stash pop` — this checkout has
`core.autocrlf=true` and `.gitattributes` had no rule for `*.txt`, so their md5s stopped matching the ones recorded here
and the next `-Pforbric.censusWrite` run would have looked like an edit. Both are now impossible for these files: the
working copies were normalised to LF and `.gitattributes` gained two narrow `eol=lf` rules — the two directories that
hold census tables, not `*.txt` everywhere. Nothing already committed changed bytes.

`JAVA_HOME=jdk-25.0.2`, `gradlew.bat --no-daemon clean jar` → **BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 29s**, `10 actionable tasks: 10
executed`, **zero** mentions of `mcLine`, `normalizeBootJava`, `normalizeBootResources`, `normalizeRuntimeJava`,
`retargetRebornEnergy` or `lineCensus` in the log.

boot    md5 709f455ab3accdb60d720891ae7b5b5b   2,623 entries
runtime md5 7d2c9f4d093a4307a5c03281b8d96b0d     324 entries

Both are the documented baseline. Then the same `clean jar` from a `git stash -u`ed HEAD reproduced **the same two md5s**,
and the two jars were compared **entry-and-CRC** (name, CRC32, uncompressed size, sorted): **identical, 2,623 and 324
entries, empty diff on both**.

### (b) `compileRuntimeJava` from nothing

`rm -rf build/classes/java/runtime` then `compileRuntimeJava` → BUILD SUCCESSFUL, **295 class files**.

The 1.21.1 `jar` invocation runs `:normalizeBootJava`, `:compileJava` (the boot half compiles on this line), then

in `build/resources/main` are byte-identical to `src/main/mc-line/1.21.1-tables/…`, and `carrier-stubs.txt` there is
byte-identical to `src/main/resources/…`, i.e. still 26.2's, which is the refusal being shipped as a refusal — then

## What is still not verified, stated plainly

- **No 1.21.1 kernel jar exists and no launch has been attempted.** Nothing in this stage executed. 1,790 table rows now
  name members this line declares *because the derivation read this line's jars* — the second half of that sentence is
  the only proof, and it is a proof about names, not about behaviour. Whether `MixinRetarget` firing on a retargeted row
  moves a selector to the right place is a launch-time fact.
- **The three tables carry `# census-line 1.21.1` and the md5s of the jars they came from; the five that could not be
  measured still carry 26.2's rows without any such stamp.** That asymmetry is deliberate (26.2's files must not change
  by one byte) and it means the line's jar is a *mixture*: a reader who wants to know which rows are this line's must
  consult this document or re-run `lineCensus`.
- **The 670 remaining 26.2 rows in those five tables are still wrong on this line**, including **8 rows that can match and
  rewrite to a dead target**. They are counted, named, and unimproved.
- **`mojmapGame()` is a derived artifact, not the game.** It is `.forbric-build/clean-mojmap.jar` — vanilla plus Mojang's
  mappings — and no version string ties it to a line: the guarantee is a class-file-major check against the merged base
  and a `net/minecraft/client/Minecraft` probe. A stale file that happened to be the same class-file generation would
  pass.
- **The census compares names and descriptors only.** A row whose key and target both exist can still be *wrong* in the
  sense that matters — `Kind`, the ecosystem column and the moved-member counts are recomputed from the same bytes, but
  nothing here checks that moving a selector along a row lands in the same behaviour.
- **`carrier-stubs`' kernel branch is refused by construction, not observed to be unused.** On this line it does reach a
  computed stub and skips; a future jar set in which it did not would produce a table with no kernel callers scanned, and
  the only guard is the fixture that fails closed.
- **The `carrier-twins` chain is refused rather than solved.** Its input table is 26.2's on this line, and nothing
  prevents a later round from running it anyway against that input — the guard is in the test, not in the table.
- **`dev.py prepare` was never run, and `E:\project\Forbric_tweaked test` was untouched.**
- **One measurement was lost and re-taken**: the mandated `clean` deleted `build/x11` before this stage's own comparison
  of the two jars' CRCs, so that comparison was re-run from scratch (build, record, stash, build, record, pop, diff) in a
  single invocation. The manifests it produced are quoted above; they are not kept.
