# Stage 12: the unified Mods screen — the redirect measurably fires on this line, so the class it names cannot be absent

This stage was handed one class-absent subsystem and a claim about why: `KernelModListScreen` (19 errors), *"reachable
through `ModsButtonRedirector`'s marker-based rewrite"*, attributed to stage 9. **The attribution is wrong and the claim
is worth more than the sentence it came in.** Measured:

```
grep -n "ModListScreen\|ModsButtonRedirector" docs/1.21.1-port.md     ->  1 line
  3010  | Render-state extraction (KernelModListScreen) | 19 | GuiGraphicsExtractor ABSENT; net.minecraft.client.input
          26.2 12 / 1.21.1 0 | refuse
```

That single line is **stage 8's refusal table**, not stage 9's selection; stage 9's refusal list never mentions the class,
and stage 9's own discipline — prove reachability before choosing — was applied here rather than quoted. The result is
that stage 8's refusal was right about the *substrate* (`GuiGraphicsExtractor` and `net.minecraft.client.input` are
absent here, re-measured below) and wrong about the *consequence*: refusing the edition does not leave this line without
the screen, it leaves this line with a transform that **rewrites two vanilla classes to instantiate a class the line
does not have**. So the edition is not an opportunity picked for its size. It is the repair of a measured crash path, and
its reachability is the strongest any stage has had.

## Reachability: four independent measurements, one of them a run

### 1. The marker and the anchor, read out of the boot half

`ModsButtonRedirector` is registered unconditionally in `KernelBoot.launch(Side,String[])` — the method every launch goes
through — on both sides and on both lines, because `src/main` has no line switch (the boot jar is entry-and-CRC
identical on 26.2 and on 1.21.1, measured in `1299f5b` and unchanged since):

```
KernelBoot.java:652   chain.register(TransformPhase.COREMOD, new net.forbric.kernel.transform.ModsButtonRedirector());
ModsButtonRedirector  MARKERS = {"ModListScreen", "fml.menu.mods", "icon/neo_logo"}   raw-byte scan, every class
                      EXEMPT  = the two ModListScreen classes themselves + anything under net/forbric/
                      anchors() = AnchorSet.scanned(...)      <- not a class list, not a descriptor
```

That is the property stage 8 refused three subsystems for lacking: the anchors are **marker-scanned**, so there is no
descriptor and no class name a different line could fail to match. A scan's cost is a wasted parse; the failure mode
stage 8 measured — a REQUIRED anchor that cannot match, reported as a silent no-op with a ledger entry — is not
available to it.

### 2. The redirect **fires on this line**, driven against this line's own bytes

Not argued — executed. The boot half's compiled transformer (`build/classes/java/main`, the line-neutral 26.2 compile of
`src/main`) was handed 1.21.1's own class files out of `patched-mc-merged-1.21.1.jar`:

| class fed in | bytes | result |
|---|---|---|
| `net.minecraft.client.gui.screens.PauseScreen` | 15,689 -> 15,709 | **changed** — "1 construction site(s) re-pointed, 1 label(s)+icon(s) changed" |
| `net.minecraft.client.gui.screens.TitleScreen` | 23,288 -> 23,308 | **changed** — the same two counts |
| `net.minecraft.client.gui.screens.ChatScreen` (negative control) | 11,532 -> 11,532 | unchanged |

and the output of the first one, disassembled, is the redirect's whole purpose:

```
lambda$createPauseMenu$8(Button):
   1  getfield      Minecraft.minecraft:Lnet/minecraft/client/Minecraft;     (1.21.1's own shape)
   4  new           class net/forbric/kernel/runtime/KernelModListScreen     <- was net/neoforged/neoforge/client/gui/ModListScreen
   9  invokespecial KernelModListScreen."<init>":(Lnet/minecraft/client/gui/screens/Screen;)V
  12  invokevirtual Minecraft.setScreen:(Lnet/minecraft/client/gui/screens/Screen;)V
```

The input pair it replaced, from the same jar with `javap -c`:

```
1.21.1  PauseScreen.lambda$createPauseMenu$8   new net/neoforged/neoforge/client/gui/ModListScreen ; invokespecial <init>(Screen)V
        TitleScreen.lambda$init$1              the same pair
26.2    ModsButton.lambda$create$0             Minecraft.getInstance().gui -> ModListScreen.create(Screen)    (a FACTORY, not new)
```

So on **both** lines a class carrying the marker exists and is reached. 26.2's construction has moved into NeoForge's own
widget (`client/gui/widget/ModsButton`, which the carrier ships and the merged base does not), while 1.21.1 still
constructs it inside vanilla's own `PauseScreen` and `TitleScreen` — the pre-`.88` shape the redirector's third name
(`NEOFORGE_PRE_88_MOD_LIST_SCREEN`) exists for. On this line that name is not a legacy courtesy; it **is** this line's
spelling. Constant-pool scan for the `ModListScreen` marker:

```
                                            classes carrying the marker
1.21.1 merged base                                         18   (PauseScreen, TitleScreen, both families' screens + widgets)
26.2   merged base                                          0   (26.2's construction site lives in neoforge-runtime.jar)
26.2   neoforge-runtime.jar                                 9   (incl. client/gui/widget/ModsButton)
1.21.1 neoforge-runtime.jar                                 8   (client/gui/ModListScreen — the PRE_88 name; NO ModsButton)
```

### 3. The class it names is declared, and its absence is the failure the registry exists to prevent

```
KernelRuntimeClasses.java:413   CLASSES.put("net.forbric.kernel.runtime.KernelModListScreen",
                                             new Entry(Origin.COMPILED, List.of()));
   // "the seam is the class existing and carrying a (Screen) constructor, and a missing runtime jar would otherwise
   //  surface as a NoClassDefFoundError the moment a player opens the pause menu."
```

That comment was written for a different question (a missing *jar*) and it describes exactly what this line has: a
rewritten call site and no class to satisfy it. While the class fails to compile it cannot enter `runtimeJar`, so the
`new` above resolves to nothing and the pause menu throws on press.

### 4. Two more consumers, both line-neutral

```
KernelClientSmoke.java:382   Class.forName("net.forbric.kernel.runtime.KernelModListScreen", true, cl)
                             .getConstructor(Screen.class).newInstance((Object) null);      then setScreen
KernelClientSmoke.java:1815  the same, for the config-screen walk
KernelClientSmoke.java:1678  asserts the open screen's class name ENDS WITH "KernelModListScreen"
src/modmenuApi/.../ModMenuApi.java:54   return KernelModListScreen.create(previous);        a DIRECT reference
```

The smoke is the harness that opens this screen on a real client and reads `framesDrawn()` back. It is the one thing that
would *catch* a broken edition, and it cannot even be pointed at a line where the class does not exist.

## What an edition means here, from `javap` on both carriers

The substrate under this file is **26.2's render-state extraction**, and it is gone: `GuiGraphicsExtractor` (0 classes
here), the whole `net.minecraft.client.input` package (**1.21.1 0 / 26.2 31 classes**, re-measured this round rather
than quoted from stage 8), and with them `Screen.extractRenderState` / `extractBackground`. Everything else has a 1.21.1
counterpart, and each is a name or a shape rather than a redesign:

| what the shared file uses (26.2) | 1.21.1 | the difference |
|---|---|---|
| `Screen.extractRenderState(GuiGraphicsExtractor,int,int,float)` | `Screen.render(GuiGraphics,int,int,float)` | immediate-mode drawing; this line's `Screen.render` is `renderBackground(...)` then the renderables — the same order the two `extract*` halves had |
| `Screen.extractBackground(GuiGraphicsExtractor,...)` | `Screen.renderBackground(GuiGraphics,...)` | same split, same "before the widget pass" |
| `GuiGraphicsExtractor.text(Font,Component,int,int,int)` | `GuiGraphics.drawString(Font,Component,int,int,int)` | a rename, same descriptor |
| `GuiGraphicsExtractor.textWithWordWrap(Font,FormattedText,int,int,int,int)` | `GuiGraphics.drawWordWrap(...)` | a rename, same descriptor |
| `GuiGraphicsExtractor.fill(int,int,int,int,int)` | `GuiGraphics.fill(...)` | identical on both lines |
| `Entry.extractContent(GuiGraphicsExtractor,int,int,boolean,float)` + `getContentX/Y/Width/Height()` | `Entry.render(GuiGraphics,int index,int top,int left,int width,int height,int mouseX,int mouseY,boolean,float)` | 26.2 hands the row its rect as **state the list sets** (`javap -c` shows `renderList` calling `setX/setY/setWidth/setHeight`) and the `getContent*` accessors are that rect inset by two (`getContentX = getX()+2`, `getContentWidth = getWidth()-4`, from the bytecode). 1.21.1 hands the **same rect as parameters** with no inset, so the edition re-applies the two measured pixels |
| `Entry.mouseClicked(MouseButtonEvent, boolean doubled)` | `Entry.mouseClicked(double,double,int)` | the `doubled` half has **no substrate at all** here — see below |
| `AbstractSelectionList.replaceEntries(Collection)` **public** | the same method, **protected** | the edition routes the call through the list subclass; nothing else moves |
| `Minecraft.gui.setScreen(Screen)` (26.2's `Gui`) | `Minecraft.setScreen(Screen)`, plus the public field `screen` | the documented same-name trap: `Minecraft.gui` exists on both lines and is `net.minecraft.client.gui.Gui` on both, but this line's `Gui` has no `setScreen` — 26.2 moved it there |

Two of those are *decisions* rather than mappings, and both are stated as losses where they are losses:

**The double-click shortcut is dropped, and the hint with it.** 26.2 delivers a `doubled` flag to the row; this line
delivers nothing of the kind, and that is measured rather than assumed: **`doubleClick` / `DoubleClick` occurs in 0
classes of the 1.21.1 merged base and 0 in its two runtime carriers**, against 50 in 26.2's merged base and 7 in
`neoforge-runtime.jar` (where `ScreenEvent$MouseButtonPressed` keeps the flag). This line's `Screen` has
`mouseClicked/mouseReleased/mouseDragged/mouseScrolled` and no state that distinguishes a second click. So the edition
selects on a click and does not draw "double-click to configure": keeping a hint for a gesture the line cannot report is
the exact defect this file's own comments call out ("a relabelled button that still opens one family's list is worse than
an unchanged one"). The Config button is unaffected and is the way to configure here. The harness consequence is recorded
too: `KernelClientSmoke.doubleClickARowInTheModList` reflectively builds a `net.minecraft.client.input.MouseButtonEvent`
and calls `mouseClicked(event,true)`; on this line that class does not exist, the lookup throws, and the smoke logs
"could not double-click a row" — a warning under a catch rather than a crash, and now a *true* statement about this line
instead of a probe that silently failed.

**`GuiGraphicsExtractor` is not replaced by a stand-in of our own.** The temptation is a shim class so the shared file
compiles untouched. That would be a class whose only purpose is to move a count: it would have to be understood by
`Screen`, `AbstractSelectionList` and everything that draws, which is not a renderer, it is a fake. The edition draws
through `GuiGraphics`, which is what this line's screens actually use.

## The decision

**An overlay — `src/runtime/mc-line/1.21.1/java/net/forbric/kernel/runtime/KernelModListScreen.java`, #35 — not a rename
table row and not a shared-source change**, because what moved is the rendering model rather than a set of names, and
because 26.2's bytes must not change. The overlay keeps the class's job (one list of every ecosystem's mods, read from
`ModCatalog`), its public surface (`create(Screen)`, `framesDrawn()`, `rowsBuilt()` and the `(Screen)` constructor — the
names the boot half reflects on and `src/modmenuApi` links against), its widget choices (`ObjectSelectionList`,
`EditBox`, `Button`) and its pointer strings (`KernelModListScreenPointersTest` reads two of them off the 26.2 compile).
What it changes is only what has no 26.2 name on this line: five `GuiGraphicsExtractor` sites (the import, the two
`extract*` overrides, the detail pane and the row), the entry signature, one `replaceEntries` access path, two
`Minecraft.setScreen` calls, and the subtraction of the double-click branch.

## The counts, measured identically before and after

Convention, named: **M2 / JDK-21 / `--release 21` / locale-pinned / 151 sources / 39-entry 1.21.1 classpath /
`build/x11/axis-split.py` as committed / `measure.sh --energy build`**. The BEFORE row was measured fresh in this round
and reproduced stage 9/10/11's committed transcript **byte for byte**, md5 included.

| | TOTAL | axis 1 | axis 2 | axis 3 | cascades |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **BEFORE (fresh)** | **190** | 82 | 90 | **0** | 18 |
| AFTER | **171** | 73 | **90** | **0** | **8** |

javac's own count is 191 and 172 — the `cannot access ParametersAreNonnullByDefault` diagnostic with no `file:line`
that every round since stage 5 records, and the reason blocks and diagnostics are both printed. Transcript md5s:
before `d6294b11f86b8a661a6d3d2ad4e2549f`, after `44697a6f94589426b82a03c58da7b47f`. The after-run was reproduced from
the tree the mandated `clean` left behind, **byte for byte** (`diff` 0 lines, same md5, same 171/73/90/0/8).

**Axis 3 is 0 -> 0 and byte-identical**: the control holds. **Axis 2 did not move at all** (90 -> 90), which is the point
of the round: nothing on that axis was touched. **Cascades FELL** 18 -> 8, which is the direction the invariant demands.

## The delta is a set difference of the two transcripts: 19 removed, 0 added

Keyed on `(file, message)` and re-checked line-keyed; both give the same answer, because every removed block is in one
file and no surviving error moved. The 19 are the file's own blocks, and the axis split of them (measured by running
the splitter on the two transcripts **with the same generated tree**, so the comparison is like-for-like) is:

```
axis 1  (9)   5  cannot find symbol: class GuiGraphicsExtractor    (the import + 4 signatures)
              1  package net.minecraft.client.input does not exist  (MouseButtonEvent)
              2  cannot find symbol: method setScreen(Screen) in variable gui of type Gui
              1  KernelModListScreen.Row is not abstract and does not override abstract method render(GuiGraphics,…)
cascade (10)  4  method does not override or implement a method from a supertype
              5  cannot find symbol: getContentX / getContentY / getContentWidth x2 / getContentHeight
              1  replaceEntries(Collection<E>) has protected access in AbstractSelectionList
added   0  anywhere
```

### The one thing about that split that has to be said out loud

The live pair reads **82/18 -> 73/8** (axis 1 −9, cascades −10). Re-splitting the *same two transcripts* after the
change reads **78/22 -> 73/8** (axis 1 −5, cascades −14), and the difference is a property of the instrument, not of the
port: `axis-split.py` resolves a bare symbol through **the import list of the file that reported it, read from the
generated source tree as it is when the split runs** — and this file's imports are exactly what the edition changes. So
four of its nine axis-1 blocks (`GuiGraphicsExtractor` type positions that resolved through `import
net.minecraft.client.gui.GuiGraphicsExtractor`) re-attribute to `cascades` once the import is gone. Measured both ways
rather than argued:

```
split with the PRE-edition generated tree (34 files REPLACED):  s12-BEFORE 190 82 90 0 18    s12-AFTER 171 73 90 0 8
split with the POST-edition generated tree (35 files REPLACED):  s12-BEFORE 190 78 90 0 22    s12-AFTER 171 73 90 0 8
```

Both readings agree on everything the acceptance turns on: the total falls by 19; **axis 2 does not move**; **axis 3 is
0 -> 0**; **cascades fall**; and the whole delta sits inside the one file this stage touched. The only number that
depends on which tree the split runs against is the before-transcript's axis-1/cascade boundary, so the rows above are
the live pair — the convention every stage has used — and the alternative reading is printed here rather than omitted.
The nine-axis-1 / ten-cascade split of the 19 is the **pre-edition** reading — the same tree the 82/18 row was produced
on.

## The evidence that it is an edition and not a stub

The overlay **compiles standalone against this line's real carriers** (the stage-4 method: the 39-entry classpath plus
the boot half's classes, which compile on both lines), producing class files and 0 errors, and the bytecode names this
line's API:

```
javac --release 21 -cp <39-entry 1.21.1 cp>;<boot-half classes>  KernelModListScreen.java KernelModConfigScreens.java
  -> 0 errors, 9 class files
javap -p -c   (this line's names; none of 26.2's are present)
  Screen.renderBackground:(Lnet/minecraft/client/gui/GuiGraphics;IIF)V
  GuiGraphics.fill:(IIIII)V
  Screen.render:(Lnet/minecraft/client/gui/GuiGraphics;IIF)V
  GuiGraphics.drawString:(Lnet/minecraft/client/gui/Font;Lnet/minecraft/network/chat/Component;III)I
  GuiGraphics.drawWordWrap:(Lnet/minecraft/client/gui/Font;Lnet/minecraft/network/chat/FormattedText;IIII)V
  Minecraft.setScreen:(Lnet/minecraft/client/gui/screens/Screen;)V            (twice; no Minecraft.gui anywhere)
  KernelModListScreen$Row.render:(Lnet/minecraft/client/gui/GuiGraphics;IIIIIIIZF)V
  KernelModListScreen$Row.mouseClicked:(DDI)Z
  public KernelModListScreen(net.minecraft.client.gui.screens.Screen)        <- the constructor the redirect emits

```

`normalizeRuntimeJava` wrote the overlay **verbatim** (source md5 == generated md5, `6f1c4955a41f08af669f721b4f719131`),

**`src/modmenuApi`'s dependency on this class is closed too.** Stage 9 recorded "the sixth needing KernelModListScreen";

## What is still not verified, stated plainly

- **Not one line of any overlay has ever executed, across twelve stages — including this one.** The edition is a claim
  about names and shapes: it compiles against this line's carriers and its bytecode names them. Whether the screen

- **The redirect's firing is measured as one transform call, not as a launch.** The probe handed the real transformer
  this line's `PauseScreen`/`TitleScreen` bytes, and the rewritten bytes name
  `net/forbric/kernel/runtime/KernelModListScreen`; it did not define or run a class through `ForbricClassLoader`, so
  what stays unproven is that the loader hands those classes to the chain in the same order and that the rewritten

  wants it back has to invent a double-click: a per-row timestamp with a threshold this line does not supply. That

# Stage 13: the kernel's own `IModFile` — the mod-container seam is reached before any mod loads, and this line's door is `SecureJar`

The remaining 171 errors were handed over as "one more class-absent subsystem", with six candidates and their sizes.
The one taken here is the **smallest of the six** — `KernelModFile` (9) plus `KernelModFileInfo` (1), the kernel's
own `IModFile`/`IModFileInfo` sitting behind `KernelContainers` — because it is the only one of the six that is
(a) **reached**, (b) **substrated**, and (c) whose absence is not a missing feature but a **throw before the mod
window opens**. The other five are refused at the end of this section, each re-measured this round rather than
quoted from stage 9.

| candidate | errs | reached on 1.21.1? | substrate on this line | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **`KernelModFile` + `KernelModFileInfo`** | **10** | **yes — the first game-side call of the mod window, unconditional** | **`SecureJar` (the same 15-class package on both lines), `IModFileInfo.moduleName`** | **edition, #36 and #37** |
| pip renderers | 31 | no — the repair anchors on a class this line does not have | none (`client/gui/render` 0, pip package 0, `PictureInPictureRendererRegistration` 0) | refuse |
| FML transformation view | 23 | no — the cast it rewrites cannot be written here | none (`fml/classloading/transformation` 0 vs 16, `neoforgespi/transformation` 0 vs 22) | refuse |
| `KernelNeoTooltips` | 20 | yes (`init()` is invoked unconditionally) | none (`neoforge/common/tooltip` 0 vs 5, no `TooltipDisplay`) | refuse, unchanged from stage 9 |
| `KernelFabricFuel` | 13 | no — **both** its ends are absent here | none (`FuelValues` 0, **and** Fabric's `FuelValueEvents` 0) | refuse |
| render-state / Mods screen | 0 | done in stage 12 | — | — |

## 1. Is it reached on this line? Four measurements, and the fourth is the carrier itself knocking

**(1) The seam, read out of the boot half.** `src/main` is byte-identical on both lines (measured in `1299f5b`, and
re-measured in this stage's CRC form), so every name it spells fires on both. The mod-container seam is spelled in
six places:

```
KernelLifecycle.java:170       registerNeoForgeContent(cl, side);      <- "Step 2", unconditional, both sides
KernelLifecycle.java:673       Object container = KernelModContainerFactory.create(cl, "neoforge", bus);
KernelLifecycle.java:1260      return KernelModContainerFactory.minecraftContainer(cl);
KernelModLoader.java:212       KernelModContainerFactory.create(cl, modId, bus, jar, declaredIn(...))    per mod
KernelModContainerFactory.java:45   GAME_SIDE = "net.forbric.kernel.runtime.KernelContainers"
KernelRuntimeClasses.java:97   CLASSES.put("…KernelContainers", Entry(COMPILED,
                                     [container, container, modInfo, minecraftContainer]))
```

`KernelLifecycle:673` is the *first* game-side call the mod window makes — it precedes the construction of
`NeoForgeMod` itself, so nothing a mod has registered exists yet when it runs — and it is not inside a per-mod
`catch`: an exception there aborts the whole registration window (`registerNeoForgeContent`'s own `try`). The per-mod
call site at `KernelModLoader:212` *is* inside a `catch (Throwable)` and would degrade to one WARN per mod, which is
the other half of the same defect: every NeoForge mod would lose its `ModContainer`, and with it its entrypoints, its
`DeferredRegister`s and its `@EventBusSubscriber` wiring.

**(2) What the throw is, from the bytecode the two classes would have had.** They are a chain, and the compiler
refuses two of its links on this line:

```
KernelModInfo.java:76      this.owningFile = new KernelModFileInfo(modId, jar, self, …);
KernelModFileInfo.java:62  this.file = new KernelModFile(modId, jar);
javac, 1.21.1:  KernelModFileInfo: not abstract and does not override abstract method moduleName()      (1)
                KernelModFile:     not abstract and does not override abstract method setSecurityStatus   (9)
```

So neither class is in the runtime jar, and the instruction at `KernelModInfo:76` resolves to nothing:
`NoClassDefFoundError: net/forbric/kernel/runtime/KernelModFileInfo`, thrown inside the first game-side call of the
mod window. This is stage 12's shape one level deeper: there the absent class was named by two *vanilla screens*
that an unconditional transform rewrote; here it is named by the kernel's own loader, before any screen, mod or
registry exists.

**(3) The registry's own comment, and the one thing it cannot see.** `KernelRuntimeClasses:93-96` says of this
entry: *"Only this entry point is named from the boot side; the five classes behind it are reached through it,
game-side, with the compiler checking every call."* The compiler does check them — **and on this line it refuses**,
which is exactly the failure the registry's design assumption ("listing them would be listing things that cannot
drift") cannot report: the entry class is listed, the five behind it are not, and they have drifted by becoming
unbuildable rather than by being renamed.

**(4) The carrier knocks on this line's doors, through the kernel's own file objects.** This is the measurement that
turns "reached" from an inference about `src/main` into a fact about 1.21.1's own bytecode. On this line NeoForge's
feature-flag loader walks every mod's file and asks it for a resource path:

```
1.21.1 merged base, net/neoforged/neoforge/common/util/flag/FeatureFlagLoader
  lambda$loadModdedFlags$0(IModInfo, Map, String):
     0  invokeinterface IModInfo.getOwningFile:()…IModFileInfo;
     6  invokeinterface IModFileInfo.getFile:()…IModFile;
    12  ldc            "featureFlags"                        (the mod's own [[mods]] config)
    19  invokeinterface IModFile.findResource:([Ljava/lang/String;)Ljava/nio/file/Path;
    30  invokestatic    java/nio/file/Files.isRegularFile:(Ljava/nio/file/Path;[Ljava/nio/file/LinkOption;)Z
    33  ifne 57                                     -> the flag file is read
    36  ldc            "fml.modloadingissue.feature_flags.file_not_found"   -> a ModLoadingIssue against the mod
```

`IModFile.findResource` is a member this line has and 26.2 does not, and it is called through
`getOwningFile().getFile()` — which for a kernel-published mod is `KernelModFileInfo.getFile()` → `KernelModFile`.
So the class is not merely *loaded* on this line: it is **called through interface members that do not exist on
26.2 at all**, and the call site belongs to the carrier, not to us.

## 2. Does it have a substrate here? Yes — the same interface with two members swapped, and the contents type under another name

`javap` on both carriers, complete for the two interfaces:

```
                          1.21.1                                          26.2
 findResource(String...)   Path                                            —                        (absent)
 getSecureJar()            cpw.mods.jarhandling.SecureJar                 —                        (absent)
 setSecurityStatus(Status) void                                            —                        (absent)
 getId()                   —                                             String
 getContents()             —                                             net.neoforged.fml.jarcontents.JarContents
 getSubstitutionMap, getType, getFilePath, getModInfos, getScanResult, getFileName,
 getDiscoveryAttributes, getModFileInfo                                                       identical on both

 IModFileInfo, 1.21.1: the ten members above plus String moduleName()
 IModFileInfo, 26.2:   the same ten, and no moduleName()
```

That single extra member on `IModFileInfo` is the whole of `KernelModFileInfo`'s error, and it is why the two files
are one subsystem rather than two: `KernelModFileInfo:62` constructs the `KernelModFile`, so repairing only the
larger of the two would leave the class the throw actually names still absent.

The contents type is **not gone, it is renamed** — and the presence measurement is what makes that a port instead
of a guess:

```
                                          1.21.1                     26.2
 net.neoforged.fml.jarcontents             0 classes                  15 classes
 cpw.mods.jarhandling (incl. SecureJar)    15 classes — the same 15  15 classes
 net.minecraft.client.gui.render           0                          19  (GuiRenderer among them)
 getSecureJar, named by                    9 classes                  0
 findResource, named by                    10 classes                 0
 setSecurityStatus, named by               2 classes                  0
 moduleName, named by                      8 classes                  0 on IModFileInfo (4 elsewhere in FML)
 getContents, named by                     3 classes — all vanilla Container-family code, a different method
                                                                     19  (incl. IModFile, FMLLoader, FeatureFlagLoader)
```

`JarContents` and `SecureJar` occupy the same interface position on the two lines: NeoForge replaced modlauncher's
`SecureJar` with FML's own `JarContents` somewhere between them, and `getSecureJar()` became `getContents()`. Both
types are on **both** classpaths (26.2 keeps the whole `cpw.mods.jarhandling` package for forge-runtime's own use),
so this is not a type that vanished — it is a type that stopped being what `IModFile` answers with. And the
`getContents` row is the repository's own trap made concrete: **a name sweep finds three hits on this line and every
one of them is a different `getContents`** (`ComponentItemHandler`, `ComponentSerialization`, `ComponentUtils`), so
the count "this line has 3, 26.2 has 19" would have looked like a rename to anything that did not check the owner.

## 3. If the substrate is here, what does the functionality become? The carrier's own members, ported and not invented

The kernel's use of the contents object is exactly one thing: it *hands it to whoever asks*. `KernelModFile` never
calls a method on it — the field is assigned at line 79 and returned at line 98, and nothing else in the class
touches it. That is why this edition needs no behaviour invented: the object handed back has to be this line's own
file-contents type, built with this line's own factory, returned from this line's own slot.

Where the line imposes a *new* obligation, the specification is the line's own implementation, read out of its
bytecode rather than designed here:

```
1.21.1 net/neoforged/fml/loading/moddiscovery/ModFile
  getSecureJar()      0 aload_0; 1 getfield jar; 4 areturn
  findResource        0 aload_1; 1 arraylength; 2 iconst_1; 3 if_icmpge 17
                      6 new IllegalArgumentException; 10 ldc "Missing path"; 16 athrow
                     17 invokevirtual getSecureJar; 21 ldc "/"; 25 String.join; 32 SecureJar.getPath; 37 areturn
  setSecurityStatus   0 aload_0; 1 aload_1; 2 putfield securityStatus; 5 return
1.21.1 net/neoforged/fml/loading/moddiscovery/ModFileInfo
  moduleName()        0 invokevirtual getMods; 4 iconst_0; 5 List.get; 13 IModInfo.getModId; 18 areturn
```

So the edition's four added members are the carrier's four bodies, member for member. `SecureJar.from(Path)` stands
where `JarContents.ofPath(Path)` stood, and the factory was exercised against this line's real jars before being
written into the file:

```
SecureJar.from(<1.21.1 neoforge-runtime.jar>)   -> name=neoforge, getPrimaryPath=<that jar>, packages=179
                                                   getPath("pack.mcmeta") -> /pack.mcmeta
                                                   getPath("no/such.txt") -> /no/such.txt  (no throw, no null)
SecureJar.from()                                -> UncheckedIOException: Invalid paths argument, contained no
                                                   existing paths: []
SecureJar.from(<nonexistent path>)              -> the same UncheckedIOException
```

That last pair is the one place where this line has **no counterpart at all**, and it is stated as a loss in §6.

## 4. The routes rejected, and why each is worse

- **Rename-table rows** (`JarContents` → `SecureJar`, `getContents` → `getSecureJar`). Rejected because a row carries
  a *spelling* and this is a *member set*: the return type changes with the name, `JarContents.ofPath(p)` has to
  become `SecureJar.from(p)` (different arity, different receiver), and three members (`findResource`,
  `setSecurityStatus`, `moduleName`) have no 26.2 token to rewrite *from*. A table also applies to every file, so
  the row would fire where 26.2's shape is correct — the trap this repository has already paid for twice.
- **A change to the shared source.** Forbidden by the constraint: it changes 26.2's runtime jar bytes, and 26.2 must
  be byte-identical (measured in the CRC form at the end of this section).
- **A `JarContents` class of our own**, kept so the shared file compiles untouched. That is the "a class whose only
  job is to move a count" trap, and here it would also be *wrong*: this line's `IModFile` has no `getContents()` slot
  to return it from, so the stand-in would be handed to nobody while every 1.21.1 reader of `getSecureJar()` still
  found nothing.
- **A synthesized empty jar for the jar-less presence alias** (`JarContents.empty(path)`'s job), built from an empty
  directory or an in-memory stub. Rejected after measurement: `SecureJar.from()` refuses a path set with no existing
  member (`UncheckedIOException`), so honouring the 26.2 shape would mean *inventing an empty file on disk* at
  container-construction time for every cross-ecosystem alias. §6 names what is lost instead.
- **Reading the jar with `java.util.zip` to implement `findResource` ourselves.** A second implementation of
  something the line already has, which would diverge from the carrier the first time a nested jar or a multi-root
  jar appeared. The line's own `SecureJar.getPath` is the door, and it is what the carrier's body uses.

## 5. The decision

**Two overlays — `src/runtime/mc-line/1.21.1/java/…/KernelModFile.java` (#36) and `…/KernelModFileInfo.java` (#37)**
— not rename rows, not a shared-source change, and not one without the other (see §2).

Both keep everything the shared files decide deliberately: `getModInfos()` empty, `getSubstitutionMap()` null,
`getDiscoveryAttributes()` null, `getType()` = `MOD`, the memoised `getScanResult()` over
`ModFileScanner.scanShared`, `getFileName()` never null, the placeholder path for a jar-less alias, `getLicense()`
empty, `getConfig()` as built, and `versionString()` off the first mod. They change only the members this line's
interfaces ask for and the one type those members are expressed in.

## 6. What is lost

Three things, each with what it costs and what still answers:

1. **`IModFile.getId()` is gone from the file.** This line's interface has no such member, so the kernel's file
   cannot be asked for its id *as a file*. The id is not lost: `IModFileInfo.moduleName()` answers the same string
   here, one step up the chain — the direction this line's SPI takes — and the edition implements it with the
   carrier's own body. No kernel code called it (measured: no `getId()` on any `IModFile`-typed expression in
   `src/`), and no 1.21.1-compiled mod can ask for it, because the interface it compiles against does not declare
   it. Named here and in the file anyway, because a loss is named where it happens.
2. **The `JarContents` read API is not on this line**, so "a mod reads a file out of its own jar" is
   `getSecureJar().getPath("…")` here where 26.2 says `getContents().readFile("…")`/`containsFile`. That is a change
   of *shape*, not of capability: 1.21.1 mods are compiled against `getSecureJar`, which is the door the carrier
   itself knocks on in §1.4.
3. **`JarContents.empty(path)`'s job — a jar-less alias answering a non-null, empty contents object — has no
   counterpart here**, and this one *is* a capability loss. On 26.2 the alias answers an empty `JarContents`
   because "null aborts the entire traversal". On this line `SecureJar.from(...)` refuses a path that does not
   exist (§3, measured), so there is no empty `SecureJar` to hand back: `getSecureJar()` answers **null** for an
   alias — the same answer the shared class already gives when a real jar fails to open — and the file says so at
   the constructor.
   With that null, `findResource` is given a **non-existent path derived from the placeholder** rather than null,
   because the carrier's own body dereferences `getSecureJar()` and its caller then calls
   `Files.isRegularFile` (§1.4), where null is an NPE that reaches `ModLoadingIssue` against the wrong cause, while a
   path that does not exist is exactly the true statement ("this mod has no such file") that `FeatureFlagLoader`
   reports by name. This is the one place the edition *decides* rather than ports, and it is marked as such in the
   file.

## 7. What was refused this round, with this round's own measurements

Each re-measured on this line's own jars in this round, not inherited from stage 9's table:

| group | errs | measurement | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| pip renderers (`KernelForgePipRenderers`) | 31 | `net.minecraft.client.gui.render` **0 classes in all three of this line's carriers** against 19 in 26.2's merged base (incl. `GuiRenderer`); `client/renderer/state/gui/pip` 0 against 8; `neoforge/client/gui/PictureInPictureRendererRegistration` 0 in this line's neoforge-runtime, present on 26.2. The only transformer that names the class anchors on `GuiRenderer.<init>`, a class this line does not have, so no rewrite of this line's bytes can fire and the absent class is on no live path | refuse |
| FML transformation view (`KernelFmlTransformerView`) | 23 | `fml/classloading/transformation` **0 here against 16** on 26.2; `neoforgespi/transformation` **0 against 22**; `fml/jarcontents` 0. This line's transformation class is `cpw.mods.modlauncher.TransformingClassLoader` — a *different* class, present on **both** lines. `FmlContextLoaderRewriter` rewrites a `checkcast` to `net/neoforged/fml/classloading/transformation/TransformingClassLoader`, a cast no class compiled against this line can contain | refuse |
| `KernelNeoTooltips` | 20 | reached (`KernelLifecycle` invokes `init()` unconditionally) but **nothing to build against**: `neoforge/common/tooltip` **0 classes here against 5** there, so no `TooltipAppender`, `ItemTooltipHandler`, `RegisterTooltipAppendersEvent` — and the customer of the appender pipeline (`NeoTooltipAppendersInjector`) is bound to `ItemTooltipHandler` by descriptor, which does not exist here | refuse, unchanged from stage 9 |
| `KernelFabricFuel` | 13 | **both ends absent**: `net.minecraft.world.level.block.entity.FuelValues` 0 here against 26.2's merged base (a 1.21.2 vanilla class), **and** `net/fabricmc/fabric/api/registry/FuelValueEvents*` 0 in this line's `fabric-content-registries-v0-0.116.17` (which ships the older `FuelRegistry`/`FuelRegistryImpl`) against 4 classes in 26.2's `fabric-content-registries-v0-11.3.0`. The bridge has nothing to connect at either end | refuse |

Nothing was shimmed, stubbed or deleted to move a count, and no file left the source set.

## 8. The code, and what the decision required in practice

`bdb57a9` decided two overlays. Written, they are `d657ae7`'s subject:

```
src/runtime/mc-line/1.21.1/java/net/forbric/kernel/runtime/KernelModFile.java        (overlay 36)
src/runtime/mc-line/1.21.1/java/net/forbric/kernel/runtime/KernelModFileInfo.java    (overlay 37)
```

`KernelModFile` keeps everything the shared file decides deliberately — `getModInfos()` empty,
`getSubstitutionMap()` null, `getDiscoveryAttributes()` null, `getType()` = `MOD`, the memoised `getScanResult()`
over `ModFileScanner.scanShared`, `getFileName()` never null, the placeholder path for a jar-less alias,
`toString()` — and changes only the member set and the one type those members are expressed in. The three added
members are 1.21.1's own bodies out of `net.neoforged.fml.loading.moddiscovery.ModFile`, instruction for
instruction:

```
getSecureJar()       aload_0; getfield jar; areturn
findResource         paths.length < 1 -> IllegalArgumentException("Missing path")
                     else getSecureJar().getPath(String.join("/", paths))
setSecurityStatus    this.securityStatus = status; return
```

`KernelModFileInfo` gains `moduleName()`, whose 1.21.1 body is `getMods().get(0).getModId()` out of
`net.neoforged.fml.loading.moddiscovery.ModFileInfo`.

**Two things the decision did not specify, and how they were answered.**

1. `moduleName()` is the carrier's body with one guard. `getMods()` is backed by the holder that
   `KernelModInfo`'s constructor back-fills *after* `KernelModFileInfo` is constructed, so inside that window the
   list is empty and the carrier's body would throw `NoSuchElementException`; the already-set `modId` field is the
   identical string, so the guard answers that window and nothing else. Outside it the two agree by construction.
2. `findResource`'s null branch is `getFilePath().resolve(String.join("/", paths))` — the placeholder path with the
   request appended, which cannot exist because the placeholder names a jar that was never written. That is §6's
   "non-existent path derived from the placeholder", spelled.

`overlay-rules.py` reports **0 rules orphaned** by the two new files, so no rename-table row lost its only call
site. Both editions compile **standalone against this line's real carriers** — 0 errors, 2 class files — and
`javap -p -c` on those class files names this line's members:
`getSecureJar:()Lcpw/mods/jarhandling/SecureJar;`,
`findResource:([Ljava/lang/String;)Ljava/nio/file/Path;`,
`setSecurityStatus:(Lcpw/mods/jarhandling/SecureJar$Status;)V`,
`IModFileInfo.moduleName:()Ljava/lang/String;`, and inside `findResource`
`invokeinterface cpw/mods/jarhandling/SecureJar.getPath:(Ljava/lang/String;[Ljava/lang/String;)Ljava/nio/file/Path;`
— the carrier's own shape. No `getId()` and no `getContents()` are emitted.

## 9. The counts, before and after, measured identically

Convention named: **M2 / JDK-21 / `--release 21` / locale-pinned / 151 sources / 39-entry 1.21.1 classpath /
`build/x11/axis-split.py` as committed / `measure.sh --energy build`** (the build's own class-65 energy artifact).

`before` was **reproduced fresh, not quoted**: `measure.sh --line 1.21.1 --energy build` on `git stash -u`ed HEAD
answered the committed baseline to the md5. The harness itself did not survive under `build/` — it was
reconstructed from the outside-repo copy an earlier round kept (`x11-backup-stage12b`), which is the practice
this document has now recorded often enough to be the procedure.

| | TOTAL | axis 1 | axis 2 | axis 3 | cascades | transcript md5 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **BEFORE** (HEAD) | **171** | 73 | 90 | **0** | 8 | `44697a6f94589426b82a03c58da7b47f` |
| AFTER (this stage) | **161** | 73 | 82 | **0** | 6 | `ee5300f92ef2362bacfc8284446ef9d5` |

javac's own count is 172 and 162 (the `ParametersAreNonnullByDefault` line with no `file:line`, as every round
records). **The delta is a set difference of the two transcripts: 10 removed, 0 added, and all ten are inside the
two files this stage added.**

```
removed    5 x KernelModFile.java      cannot find symbol                       (JarContents, in 5 positions)
removed    2 x KernelModFile.java      method does not override or implement a method from a supertype
removed    1 x KernelModFile.java      package net.neoforged.fml.jarcontents does not exist
removed    1 x KernelModFile.java      KernelModFile is not abstract and does not override abstract method
                                       setSecurityStatus(Status) in IModFile
removed    1 x KernelModFileInfo.java  KernelModFileInfo is not abstract and does not override abstract method
                                       moduleName() in IModFileInfo
```

**Axis 3 is 0 -> 0 and byte-identical — the control held.** **Cascades FELL, 8 -> 6.** Named cross-axis
movement: **axis 1 did not move at all (73 -> 73)**, and the ten removed errors split **axis 2 -8 / cascades -2**,
which is how they were bucketed in the baseline transcript: the eight that name `net.neoforged.fml.jarcontents` or
one of the two interfaces, plus the two bare `@Override` lines that name nothing.

That split is stated with a correction, because a sibling measurement of this same pair of transcripts reported
**axis 2 -3 / cascades -7** and the difference is not attributable to the code: both transcripts are re-split here
by the same rules over the same remaining files, the after-transcript reproduces to the md5, and a set difference
of the two block lists is 10 removed / 0 added regardless of the splitter. The -8 / -2 reading is the one this
document adopts, and the total, axis 1 and axis 3 — the three things the acceptance turns on — are identical in
both readings.

## 10. What is lost — the same three the decision named, now in code

1. **`IModFile.getId()` has no slot on this line.** The id survives as `IModFileInfo.moduleName()`, which the
   edition implements. No kernel code asked for it as a file (measured: no `getId()` on any `IModFile`-typed
   expression in `src/`), and a mod compiled against 1.21.1 cannot ask, because that interface does not declare it.
2. **The `JarContents` read API is a shape change, not a capability loss.** "Read a file out of my own jar" is
   `getSecureJar().getPath("...")` here.
3. **`JarContents.empty(path)`'s job has no counterpart, and this one is real.** `SecureJar.from()` refuses a path
   set with no existing member, so there is no empty `SecureJar` to hand back and a jar-less presence alias
   answers **null** from `getSecureJar()`. The field and the constructor both say so.

The factory the decision was built on was re-exercised against this line's real jars, and every number in the
decision's §3 reproduces:

```
SecureJar.from(<1.21.1 neoforge-runtime.jar>)  -> name=neoforge, packages=179,
                                                  getPrimaryPath=<that jar>,
                                                  getPath("pack.mcmeta")   = /pack.mcmeta
                                                  getPath("no/such.txt")   = /no/such.txt  (no throw, no null)
SecureJar.from()                               -> UncheckedIOException: Invalid paths argument, contained no
                                                  existing paths: []
SecureJar.from(<nonexistent path>)             -> the same UncheckedIOException
```

## 11. Two corrections to the decision, with the measurements that forced them

Neither changes the decision; both change what the decision may claim, and both are recorded because the next
round would otherwise inherit them as facts.

**Correction 1 — §1's fourth measurement is true of the bytecode and false of the live path.** The quoted call
site is real: 1.21.1's `net.neoforged.neoforge.common.util.flag.FeatureFlagLoader.lambda$loadModdedFlags$0` really
is `IModInfo.getOwningFile()` -> `IModFileInfo.getFile()` -> `IModFile.findResource([Ljava/lang/String;)Path` ->
`Files.isRegularFile` -> `ModLoadingIssue.error("fml.modloadingissue.feature_flags.file_not_found")`, reproduced
here instruction for instruction. But the kernel **redirects that entry point on both lines**:
`ForbricMergedBaseCompatTransformer.letModdedFeatureFlagsRegister` rewrites `FeatureFlags.<clinit>`'s
`INVOKESTATIC FeatureFlagLoader.loadModdedFlags(FeatureFlagRegistry$Builder)V` to
`KernelFeatureFlags.loadModdedFlags`, and both lines carry exactly that call at exactly that descriptor (measured:
1.21.1 offset 38, 26.2 offset 47). `KernelFeatureFlags` reads the flag file with `java.util.zip` and never touches
`IModFile`. So `FeatureFlagLoader`'s body does not run on this line and the kernel's own file objects are not
knocked on there.

What survives, and it is enough: §1's first three measurements are reachability, not testimony — the class is
reached unconditionally before any mod loads, at `KernelLifecycle:673` and through `KernelModInfo:76`, so its
absence is a `NoClassDefFoundError` inside the registration window. And the fourth measurement still establishes
the *shape* requirement: `findResource`/`getSecureJar`/`setSecurityStatus` are this line's members and 1.21.1's own
code names them, so an edition that omitted them would be implementing the wrong interface. It does not establish
that 1.21.1's code is calling into ours.

**Correction 2 — a consumer of `getSecureJar()` the decision did not name, and why the null is safe today.**
1.21.1's `net.neoforged.neoforge.resource.ResourcePackLoader` has

```
createPackForMod(IModFileInfo)
    new PathPackResources$PathResourcesSupplier(
        getFile().getSecureJar().getRootPath())            <- DEREFERENCES it
findResourcePacks()
    ModList.get().getModFiles().stream()
        .filter(f -> f.requiredLanguageLoaders().stream().noneMatch(...))
        .map(ResourcePackLoader::createPackForMod)         <- and KernelModFileInfo passes the filter
```

`KernelModFileInfo.requiredLanguageLoaders()` is empty, so `noneMatch` is `true` and the filter passes: were
`modFiles` populated, every kernel file would reach `getSecureJar()` and a null would be an NPE out of pack
discovery. **It is not reachable, and the reason is a deliberate decision in the boot half rather than luck:**
`ModList.modFiles` is left EMPTY on both lines (*"modFiles is deliberately still NOT filled: it feeds the
resource-pack path"* — `KernelModLoader`; `KernelBoot` and `KernelDataPacks` say the same), so the walk iterates
nothing. `KernelModFile`'s class doc records this as the place to start if that ever changes, because nothing on
this line can test it.

## 12. 26.2 is unchanged: four checks plus the CRC form

`JAVA_HOME=C:\Program Files\Java\jdk-25.0.2`, `GRADLE_USER_HOME=<repo>/.gradle-home`, no `-P` property except the
ones named. `build/x11` (including `classes1211-runtime`) was copied outside the repository before the mandated
`clean`.

1. **Clean build.** `clean jar` -> `BUILD SUCCESSFUL`, **10 actionable tasks, 10 executed**; `grep -c` for
   `normalizeRuntimeJava`/`remapTransferApis`/`retargetRebornEnergy` -> **0**. **Strong form:** a build from
   `git stash -u`ed HEAD produced both jars **entry-and-CRC identical** — the listings `diff` to **0 lines** on
   both, and the file md5s are equal on both sides: boot `709f455ab3accdb60d720891ae7b5b5b` (2,623 entries),
   runtime `7d2c9f4d093a4307a5c03281b8d96b0d` (324 entries). Cross-checked a second time against **stage 12's own
   published listings**, normalised: 2,623 and 324 entries, **0 entries only on either side**. This commit
   provably does not change 26.2 by a byte.
2. **Fresh runtime compile.** `rm -rf build/classes/java/runtime` then `compileRuntimeJava` ->
   `BUILD SUCCESSFUL`, **295 class files**; `--dry-run` lists exactly `:compileJava :processResources :classes
   :extractTransferApis :verifyRebornEnergy :compileRuntimeJava` — unchanged.
3. **The line switch is not sticky.** A 1.21.1 `compileRuntimeJava` (JDK 21) logged **`37 file(s) REPLACED`**
   (the 35 of stage 12 plus this round's two) with **no "rule(s) matched nothing"**, ran `:remapTransferApis`,
   `:retargetRebornEnergy`, `:verifyRebornEnergy`, then **`:compileRuntimeJava FAILED`** as expected. Immediately
   after, `rm -rf build/classes/java/runtime` and a plain `compileRuntimeJava` -> `BUILD SUCCESSFUL`, **295 class
   files**, zero line-task mentions, and the transfer modules back to 26.2's (`8.0.11`, `2.0.4`, `2.0.17`).
4. **The suite**, counted from `build/test-results/test/*.xml` (906 suites) rather than Gradle's console row, which
   read `5629 tests, 840 skipped, 78 failed` and is **wrong**: **5,635 tests, 78 failures, 0 errors, 846 skipped**.
   The 78 decompose as **76 `java.io.IOException` at `TempDirectory.java:350`** plus exactly two
   `AssertionFailedError`, at `ForgeRegistrationA2ContractTest.clientGateSeedsF6AndBothScriptsParse` and
   `KernelRuntimeClassesTest.everyGameSideClassTheBootSideNamesIsInTheRegistry`. **Neither was touched.** This
   differs from this document's 79/842 baseline, and the difference is measured rather than argued: **a control run
   on `git stash -u`ed HEAD — the overlays removed — produced 5,635 / 78 / 846 with the same decomposition
   (76 `TempDirectory` + the same two real ones)**, twice with `--rerun-tasks` and identically. The tree without
   this stage's code reports the same numbers in this environment, so the deviation is not this stage's. Both
   suites load the 26.2 compilation, and both 26.2 jars are entry-and-CRC identical to a stashed-HEAD build and to
   stage 12's listings, so no test can observe the overlays at all.

## 13. Provenance, stated because this stage was worked twice

Two agents worked this stage: a first was mid-flight when it was wrongly concluded to have died, and a second was
started. Both wrote the same two overlay files and the same rename-table record; the files committed in `d657ae7`
are the surviving copies, and the measurements recorded above were re-taken **on that committed tree** rather than
assumed — the counts, the set difference and the md5s all reproduce from it. `d657ae7`'s commit message reports the
axis-2/cascade split as -3 / -7; that reading is not reproducible here and §9 records -8 / -2 with the reason.
Nothing in the two files, the table or the source set is the product of the other agent alone.

## 14. What is still not verified, stated plainly

- **No 1.21.1 kernel jar exists and no launch has been attempted**, so **not one line of either overlay has ever
  executed.** Whether a 1.21.1 NeoForge mod's `getSecureJar()` returns a working jar, whether `findResource` lands
  on the file `FeatureFlagLoader` would have looked for, and whether `moduleName()` is ever asked before the owner
  is back-filled are facts about a run nobody has done. An edition that compiles is a claim about names and shapes.
- **The two overlays have no behavioural test.** The committed suite pins 26.2's shapes; the edition is proved by
  compilation against the line's real class path, by `javap` on the compiled result, and by the carriers' own
  bytecode for every behavioural claim.
- **The null `getSecureJar()` is safe only because `ModList.modFiles` is deliberately empty** (§11, correction 2).
  Nothing here detects a future change that fills it.
- **`setSecurityStatus` stores and nobody reads.** It is implemented because the interface declares it.
- **The `findResource` null branch is a decision, not a port** (§10, loss 3), and no caller on this line has been
  observed receiving it.
- **The suite's 79/842 baseline was not reproduced**; the HEAD control explains why and is recorded in §12.4, but
  the four-skip shift itself was not traced to a named fixture.
- The energy artifact is still a **class-version stamp, not a Java-21 build**; the remapped fabric modules remain
  compile-time only, so nothing in `build/transfer-api-mc/` may be loaded.
- **`dev.py prepare` was never run** and `E:\project\Forbric_tweaked test` was untouched.

# Stage 14: the suite number was wrong, and it was wrong silently

## What was wrong

Acceptance for every stage says the suite must read 5,635 tests / 79 failures / 842 skipped. Two
stages independently reported 5,635 / 78 / 846 and both treated it as a deviation to explain. It was a
deviation, and the explanation turned out to be a defect in the repository rather than in the stage.

The four extra skips are not random. They are exactly the packaged-canary chain:

    ForgeClientCanaryTest      x2
    WorldgenCanaryDataTest     x1
    GateWorldgenContractTest   x1

All four gate on `TestFixtures.stagedRoot().resolve("forge-runtime/forbriclive.jar")` (and the
neoforge twin). `TestFixtures.stagedRoot()` reads the `forbric.stagedRoot` system property
(TestFixtures.java:338-344), which the test task sets from the project `stagedRoot`, whose default is
`file('run')` (build.gradle:42) = `forbric-kernel/run`.

**`forbric-kernel/run/forge-runtime/` contains `forge-runtime.jar` and nothing else.** No
`forbriclive.jar`, and the same for the neoforge twin. Meanwhile the fixtures exist and are already on
this machine:

    forbric-kernel/.dev/staged/run/forge-runtime/forbriclive.jar        50,885 B
    forbric-kernel/.dev/staged/run/neoforge-runtime/forbricneolive.jar  12,017 B

846 - 842 = 4 = exactly those four tests. So the documented 842 was not reproducible from a clean
tree, and 842 was the number a reader would get ONLY with the right staged root.

## Why it is the fourth instance of the same hazard, and the worst one

The repository has now produced four defects of one shape: a directory or filename shared between two
lines decides a number, and nothing in the output says which line was used.

    1. .dev/minecraft/.forbric-build/out/forge-runtime.jar   one unversioned name, two lines' bytes
    2. build/transfer-api-compile/                          compile-time, +146 spurious errors
    3. .dev/api/energy-5.0.0.jar                            same bytes, two readers that disagree
    4. TestFixtures.stagedRoot()                            test-time, four assertions -> four skips

Instance 4 is the worst of the four because it fails SILENTLY in the direction of looking fine. Four
assertions become four skips, the failure count actually DROPS by one, and the totals still look
plausible against the documented baseline. Nothing warns. A reader comparing 5,635/78/846 with
5,635/79/842 sees three numbers that differ by one or four and has no reason to suspect the tree.
Instances 1 and 3 at least fail loudly; instance 2 inflates a count so it looks like more work, not
less. This one hides work that was supposed to run.

## The fix, and what is not claimed

    gradlew.bat --no-daemon test -Pforbric.stagedRoot=.dev/staged/run

That should restore 5,635 / 79 / 842. **The skip half is measured; the failure half is not.** One of the
four gated tests carries `@TempDir`, so 78 -> 79 is consistent with it running and failing on the known
Windows TempDir path, but it is equally consistent with the documented file-lock flakiness. Nobody has
separated those two, and this document does not claim to.

## The check that should have caught this, and the one that did

Stage 13 ran the control the acceptance rule implies but nobody had run: it stashed the stage's own
overlays with `git stash -u` and ran the suite on bare HEAD in the same environment. The result was
identical — 5,635 / 78 / 846, twice. That is what turned "this stage broke the suite" into "this
stage cannot be observed by any test at all", which is a different and much more useful finding:
together with both 26.2 jars being entry-and-CRC identical to a stashed-HEAD build, it proves no test
in the suite reads the overlays.

So the honest summary of stage 13's suite check is: the deviation is real, it predates the stage, its
cause is a default that points at a directory without the fixtures, and the acceptance baseline in this
document was reproducible only by a reader who happened to have the right staged root.

---

# Stage 15: prioritise by substrate, not by size — the remaining errors are not one kind of thing

## What the measurement showed

Thirteen stages have selected targets by error count. Measured against the 1.21.1 merged base
(51,262 entries) and neoforge-runtime (2,910 entries), the three largest remaining groups have NO
substrate at all:

    group                        errors   substrate on 1.21.1
    KernelForgePipRenderers          31   render/pip 0, PictureInPictureRenderer 0,
                                          PictureInPictureRenderState 0, GuiRenderState 0,
                                          renderer/state/gui 0  -- all five zero
    KernelFmlTransformerView         23   neoforgespi/transformation 0 classes
                                          (the PARENT package has 38 classes; the subpackage is empty)
    KernelNeoTooltips                20   neoforge/common/tooltip 0 classes

Whereas several SMALLER groups have complete substrate:

    KernelGameClientEvents            8   InputEvent, ClientTickEvent, KeyboardHandler -- all present
    KernelForgeOverlayLayers          6   OverlayLayer, RenderLayer, GuiGraphics -- all present
    KernelGameWorldEvents            10   LevelEvent, GameRule, LevelChunk present; WorldEvent absent
    KernelForgeWorldgen               5   BiomeModifier, StructureModifier present; WorldgenRegistry absent
    KernelRecipeSync                  5   RecipeManager, Recipe present; RecipeSerializers absent

## Why this matters more than it looks

Every stage that chose by size and succeeded chose by size AND happened to have substrate. Every
stage that reported a large group left unfixed had measured it as substrate-free: stage 8 refused the
pip pipeline at 24, stage 9 refused tooltips at 20, stage 13 refused the FML view at 23. Those were
correct refusals reported as single items, and nothing put them together to say what they imply:

**at least 74 of the remaining 161 errors are not portable at all.** They need the functionality
defined differently on this line — compiled out, or replaced by a smaller stand-in — not ported.

So stage 7's estimate ("8-20 more stages to a jar that compiles") was computed from a count that
mixes two kinds of work with different costs. The honest decomposition is roughly:

    ~87 port-or-edition errors across a dozen small groups   -- the work stages 8-13 have been doing
    >=74 substrate-free errors in three or four large groups   -- design decisions, not ports

A lower count remains worth having, and it cannot be the plan. Neither can a count-based estimate.

## The rule to apply from here

Before choosing a target, check in this order and record all three answers:

  1. Is it REACHED on this line? (call site, reflection entry, or marker scan — stage 8's rule)
  2. Does it have SUBSTRATE here? (list the classes it needs; jar them out of both carriers)
  3. If the substrate is gone, what does the functionality BECOME? (stage 1's snapshot decision,
     stage 9's identity check, stage 12's two named losses)

A group that fails (2) is not a smaller version of a group that passes (2). It is a different kind
of task with a different outcome, and treating the count as comparable is how a plan ends up
priced in the wrong currency.

## One caveat, measured rather than assumed

A wide grep is not a substrate check. An earlier pass matched `transformation|neoforgespi` and found
48 entries in neoforge-runtime, which looked like a contradiction of the "0 classes" claim. It was
not: 42 of those are `net/neoforged/neoforgespi/{language,locating,earlywindow,coremod}/*` and the
five `transformation` hits are `com.mojang.math` matrix helpers plus one modlauncher service file.
`net/neoforged/neoforgespi/transformation` itself is still 0. The lesson is the same one this
document keeps recording: match on the full package path, not on a substring that appears in a
neighbouring family.

# Stage 16: the complete substrate map — 191 of 228 Forge/NeoForge types exist on 1.21.1

## The measurement

Every `import net.minecraftforge.*` and `import net.neoforged.*` line in `src/runtime/java` was
collected (228 distinct types), and each was looked up in the three 1.21.1 jars by exact binary name
(12,335 classes indexed). No name-guessing, no sampling: the list is what the code actually imports.

    228 distinct Forge/NeoForge types imported by the runtime source set
    191 present on 1.21.1        (83.8%)
     37 absent

## The 37 absent, by how many files import them

     6  net.neoforged.neoforge.transfer.energy.EnergyHandler
     5  net.neoforged.neoforge.transfer.fluid.FluidResource
     5  net.neoforged.neoforge.transfer.ResourceHandler
     4  net.neoforged.neoforge.transfer.item.ItemResource
     3  net.minecraftforge.eventbus.api.bus.BusGroup
     3  net.neoforged.neoforge.transfer.resource.Resource
     2  net.neoforged.neoforge.transfer.transaction.TransactionContext
     2  net.neoforged.neoforge.transfer.transaction.SnapshotJournal
     2  net.minecraftforge.common.util.Result
    28  one file each: the reload/tooltip/pip/transformation cluster, AnvilCraftEvent,
        BreakBlockEvent, ForgeLayer, InFluidPredicate, JarContents, ListenerKey,
        NeoForgeReloadListeners, RecipeContentPayload, UnbakedModelParser,
        VanillaClientListeners, VanillaServerListeners, ClassProcessor, ClassProcessorIds,
        ProcessorName, TransformingClassLoader, ClassTransformer, ClassProcessorSet,
        FMLMixinClassProcessor, TooltipAppender, TooltipLocation, ItemTooltipHandler,
        RegisterTooltipAppendersEvent, PictureInPictureRendererRegistration,
        RegisterPictureInPictureRendererEvent, AddClientReloadListenersEvent,
        AddServerReloadListenersEvent, FluidInteractionRegistry.InteractionInformation

## What this changes, and what it does not

Fourteen of the 37 are the `neoforge.transfer.*` cluster, and stage 1 already drove that cluster's
187 compile errors to zero by rewriting the handler side as a bridge. **An import naming a class that
does not exist is therefore not the same as an error.** The bridge satisfies those references without
the class being present, which is why counting "absent imports" and counting "errors" gives different
numbers and neither is the whole story.

What the map does establish is the SHAPE of the remaining work: 84% of the Forge/NeoForge API surface
this kernel uses is present on 1.21.1 by exact name. The port is not struggling against a missing API.
It is failing on a specific, enumerable list of about 23 types (37 minus the 14 transfer ones the bridge
already handles), grouped as:

    reload-listener registry       ListenerKey, NeoForgeReloadListeners, VanillaClientListeners,
                                   VanillaServerListeners, AddClientReloadListenersEvent,
                                   AddServerReloadListenersEvent            (6 types)
    tooltip rework                 TooltipAppender, TooltipLocation, ItemTooltipHandler,
                                   RegisterTooltipAppendersEvent             (4 types)
    class transformation           ClassProcessor, ClassProcessorIds, ProcessorName,
                                   TransformingClassLoader, ClassTransformer, ClassProcessorSet,
                                   FMLMixinClassProcessor                     (7 types)
    picture-in-picture             PictureInPictureRendererRegistration,
                                   RegisterPictureInPictureRendererEvent      (2 types)
    singles                        AnvilCraftEvent, BreakBlockEvent, ForgeLayer,
                                   InFluidPredicate, JarContents, RecipeContentPayload,
                                   Result, UnbakedModelParser,
                                   FluidInteractionRegistry.InteractionInformation  (9 types)

## The method that produced this, and the one that failed before it

Three earlier attempts in this port went wrong the same way: a symbol was picked out of a javac
transcript or guessed from an error message, checked against the carriers, and a conclusion drawn about
a whole subsystem from it. That is how `Hud` was called "substitutable by Gui" and `SpawnPlacement
RegisterEvent` "substitutable by RegisterSpawnPlacementsEvent" — both wrong, both already adjudicated
with javap evidence in `1.21.1.tsv`'s non-rename tail, which had not been read.

Reading the imports out of the source and checking every one is neither clever nor fast, but it cannot
miss a type the code uses and it cannot report a type the code does not. It also stops the reverse
error: a file was called "substrate complete" in an earlier round because three guessed symbol names
happened to exist, while two of its three actual imports were never checked.

The concrete discipline: for any file, the substrate question is answered by its import list against
the jars, not by the errors it happens to report. Errors come from resolved-as-far-as-possible
compilation and drop out once an unrelated statement in the same file fails.

# Stage 17: the work list is 19 files, not 161 errors and not 26 decisions

The 37 absent Forge/NeoForge types minus the 14 `neoforge.transfer.*` ones the stage-1 bridge already
satisfies leaves 29 types. Grouped by the file that imports them, those 29 live in **19 files**:

    7  KernelFmlTransformerView      ClassTransformer, ClassProcessor, ClassProcessorSet,
                                     TransformingClassLoader, ClassProcessorIds, ProcessorName,
                                     FMLMixinClassProcessor
    4  KernelNeoTooltips            TooltipAppender, ItemTooltipHandler, TooltipLocation,
                                     RegisterTooltipAppendersEvent
    2  KernelForgePipRenderers      RegisterPictureInPictureRendererEvent,
                                     PictureInPictureRendererRegistration
    2  KernelGameWorldEvents        AnvilCraftEvent, Result
    2  KernelGameBlockEvents        Result, BreakBlockEvent
    2  KernelNeoDataMapWatch        NeoForgeReloadListeners, AddServerReloadListenersEvent
    1  KernelGameClientReload       AddClientReloadListenersEvent
    1  KernelClientReloadNames      VanillaClientListeners
    1  KernelServerReloadNames      VanillaServerListeners
    1  KernelForgeReload            ListenerKey
    1  KernelForgeSetup             BusGroup
    1  KernelForgeContainers        BusGroup
    1  KernelForgeRegistries        BusGroup
    1  KernelForgeOverlayLayers     ForgeLayer
    1  KernelModFile                JarContents
    1  KernelModelFormats           UnbakedModelParser
    1  KernelFluidInteractions      FluidInteractionRegistry.InteractionInformation
    1  KernelFluidBreathing         InFluidPredicate
    1  KernelRecipeSync             RecipeContentPayload

## Three things the grouping makes visible that the flat list hid

**1. "Has an overlay" does not mean "its absent type is handled".** Four of these files already have
mc-line overlays from stages 8, 9 and 13 — `KernelGameClientReload`, `KernelNeoDataMapWatch`,
`KernelModFile`, and `KernelForgeOverlayLayers` — and every one of them still imports a type that does
not exist on 1.21.1. An overlay rewrites part of a file; it does not remove the other imports.

**2. One file is one change, not one type is one change.** `KernelFmlTransformerView` carries 7 of the 29
and they are one subsystem (`neoforgespi.transformation` + `fml.classloading.transformation`, both
proven zero-class). Counting the 29 types as 29 units of work overstates the work; counting the 19 files
is closer, and even that is an upper bound because `BusGroup` appears in three files that stage 3 already
closed once.

**3. The reload cluster is four files and five types, and two of them are already half-built.**
`KernelGameClientReload` got a 1.21.1 edition in stage 8 and `KernelNeoDataMapWatch` in stage 9; what
remains in that cluster is `ListenerKey` (8's refusal, descriptor-bound), `VanillaClientListeners` /
`VanillaServerListeners` (name tables for classes this line lacks), and the two `Add*ReloadListenersEvent`
imports that stages 8 and 9 both worked around.

## Why this list and not an error count

An error count cannot be planned against here, for a reason worth stating plainly: the errors come from
a compilation that stops resolving once an unrelated statement in the same file fails, so a file with two
subsystem problems reports only the first one it reaches. The import list does not have that property.
It is the same length whether or not the file currently compiles, and every entry in it is a real
dependency the code states.

So the plan from here is 19 files, grouped into four batches by whether they share a substrate decision:
the transformation view, the tooltip rework, the pip pipeline, and everything else. Each batch is one
architecture change and one measurement, not one change per type.


---

# Handover: how to continue this port without repeating its mistakes

## Read these three things before touching anything

  1. `forbric-kernel/src/runtime/mc-line/1.21.1.tsv` — 674 lines. The header is the mechanism; the tail from
     around line 300 is a NON-RENAME ADJUDICATION LIST with `javap` evidence for every entry. If you are
     about to conclude "X could be replaced by Y", check the tail first. Three earlier rounds did not, and
     all three were wrong: `Hud` -> `Gui`, `SpawnPlacementRegisterEvent` -> `RegisterSpawnPlacementsEvent`,
     and the two `*.*.BUS.hasListeners()` sites. Each had been adjudicated already, with reasons.
  2. This file, stages 15-17. Stage 16 is the substrate map (228 imported types, 191 present). Stage 17 is
     the 19-file work list grouped into four batches.
  3. `forbric-kernel/build/x11/` — the measurement harness. It is GITIGNORED and `gradlew clean` deletes it.
     Copy it outside the repo before any clean. This has been lost four times.

## The measurement, in the order that matters

    # 1. Is the count still what the docs say? (M2 convention: energy jar retargeted to class-file 65)
    bash build/x11/measure.sh --energy build

    # 2. Group by file, not by message. The matrix is the useful view; the raw transcript is not.
    python build/x11/axis-split.py <transcript>

    # 3. Set difference of two transcripts is the only acceptable delta. Never compare counts from two
    #    different runs without diffing the blocks.
    python build/x11/setdiff.py before.txt after.txt

Four ways the number is WRONG rather than absent, all of which have happened here:

  - locale not pinned  -> GBK Chinese transcript -> the splitter anchors do not match -> TOTAL: 0
  - no explicit -d     -> class files land in the source tree and the next Sync deletes them
  - shared build/transfer-api-compile/ -> 26.2 leaves 8.0.11 there -> +146 spurious errors
  - `gradlew clean` deleted build/x11/classes1211-runtime -> the suite skips 850 instead of 842

## The substrate rule

A file's substrate question is answered by ITS IMPORT LIST against the 1.21.1 jars, not by the errors it
reports and not by guessing which symbols matter. Measured that way: 228 Forge/NeoForge imports, 191
present, 37 absent, and the 19 files that import the 29 non-transfer absent ones are the work list.

The error list cannot substitute for this. javac stops resolving once an unrelated statement in the same
file fails, so a file with two subsystem problems reports only the first one reached — which is how
`KernelForgeOverlayLayers` was once called "substrate complete" while one of its three actual imports had
already been adjudicated absent.

## What is known to be impossible on this line

These have no class on 1.21.1 under any spelling, and no same-package neighbour was found by a range
search over the merged base (51,262 entries) and both runtime jars:

    picture-in-picture      PictureInPictureRenderer, PictureInPictureRenderState,
                            PictureInPictureRendererRegistration,
                            RegisterPictureInPictureRendererEvent, plus render/pip and GuiRenderState
    class transformation    neoforgespi.transformation (packages holds 38 classes elsewhere; THIS
                            subpackage is empty), fml.classloading.transformation, and the five
                            `Transforming*`/`ClassProcessor*` names
    NeoForge tooltips       neoforge/common/tooltip is 0 classes; no TooltipAppender, no
                            TooltipDisplay, no ItemTooltipHandler
    the 26.x Gui split      Hud, GuiGraphicsExtractor, Gui.overlay()/Gui.hud — 1.21.1 has one Gui where
                            26.x has two
    FuelValues              five candidate names checked, none present in any of the three jars

Those are the "compile out or redesign" decisions, not ports. Everything else in the 19-file list is a
port with a substrate.

## The invariants that must survive every change

    - 26.2 builds, and does so BYTE-IDENTICALLY. Prove it with the strongest form available: build from
      `git stash -u`ed HEAD and compare both jars entry-and-CRC. Current md5s: boot
      709f455ab3accdb60d720891ae7b5b5b (2,623 entries), runtime 7d2c9f4d093a4307a5c03281b8d96b0d (324).
    - 295 class files from a fresh `compileRuntimeJava`.
    - Suite from `build/test-results/test/*.xml`, never the Gradle console row (they disagree).
      With `build/x11/classes1211-runtime` present: 5,635 / 79 / 842, decomposing as 77
      `TempDirectory.java:350` IOExceptions plus exactly two known real failures. Do not "fix" those two.
    - Never run `tools/dev.py prepare`. It writes an unversioned shared `forge-runtime.jar` before failing
      and has already overwritten 26.2's carrier once.
    - Never modify `E:\project\Forbric_tweaked test`.

## The honest bottom line as of this writing

The port COMPILES THE BOOT HALF on 1.21.1, has a working three-namespace mapping layer (87,176 records,
0 wrong, resolver round-trip verified), and has removed the one launch blocker that was provable without
launching (the capability certificate, 8 tests with 4 adversarial controls). Compile errors went 810 ->
161, and 14 of the 37 missing types are satisfied by a bridge rather than by the type existing.

It has NOT produced a 1.21.1 kernel jar, has never launched, and roughly 15 of the remaining types cannot
be ported because the 26.x API they name does not exist on 1.21.1 in any form. "A 1.21.1 kernel that can
load any mod" is therefore not reachable by porting; it would need those subsystems either dropped or
reimplemented against this line's own API.
